# SlipStream pre-migration audit — recovered findings

Run `wf_28646067-85c`, stopped by the user on 2026-09-05 before synthesis. 16 finder dimensions completed; 8 of the critical/high findings received at least one verifier verdict before the stop. **Treat critical/high as REPORTED, not CONFIRMED, unless the verdicts below say otherwise.**

Totals after de-duplication: **8 critical · 45 high · 66 medium · 77 low** across 16 dimensions.

Every finding carries `target`: `repo` = code at 87f9eb1, `deployed` = the program as it runs on devnet (which lacks all 12 remediate P0 fixes), `both`.

Hard rules that bound every fix: no program deploy (human-only); authorization is never weakened; no protected test is modified; the faucet never echoes RPC text.


## Critical and high, all dimensions

- **[critical/both] locked-funds** — Every undelegation is a one-way door into 'half-delegated': the program has no handler for the delegation program's undelegate callback, so the validator's L1 undelegate fails and the credit is stranded on both layers  `programs/slipstream/src/instructions/mod.rs:177`
- **[critical/both] oracle-er-trust** — Funding premium is TWAP-of-oracle minus oracle spot (pure momentum, never the book); the ring now spans ~5.6h so the R6 clamp binds 41% of intervals, and the deployed unclamped index (-10.38) has $69,149 of phantom funding claimable today that R6 does not rewind  `programs/slipstream/src/instructions/compute_funding.rs:74`  _verified: 2 stand / 0 refuted of 2_
- **[critical/both] prog-deployed-exposure** — claim_funding is unlatched: the poisoned funding index now pays $68,401.93 to 10 long holders, and the repo fix (R6) does not neutralise it  `programs/slipstream/src/instructions/claim_funding.rs:99`
- **[critical/both] prog-margin-liq** — Poisoned funding index (-10.38) is live and unlatched: $68,989 of minted funding credits are claimable now at a fresh mark, $39,733 withdrawable immediately, and the 10 credited longs are immune to liquidation; R6 preserves it across a redeploy  `programs/slipstream/src/instructions/claim_funding.rs:209`
- **[critical/both] prog-r-fixes** — Delegation-program undelegate callback (EXTERNAL_UNDELEGATE 0xC4) is not dispatched, so no delegated TradingCredit can ever return to L1; R1/R2's whole migration and seed_credit_ledger are unreachable for 100% of live users, and R1 turns the stuck state into a free_collateral lock  `programs/slipstream/src/instructions/mod.rs:180`

## api-routes  (7 findings)

**Measured:** Live, read-only, 2026-09-05 against https://slipstream.ansht.tech (droplet 139.59.45.112, Caddy edge, NO Cloudflare -- prior audit's cf-connecting-ip premise is moot), the ER (https://devnet.magicblock.app) and public devnet. RPC-proxy amplification: getAccountInfo(orderBook 83zMFL...) on ER = 835,887 B; getMultipleAccounts x50 duplicate orderbook = 2,499 B request -> 41,790,824 B response (41.8 MB, 19.9 s); via the LIVE proxy /api/rpc/er getMultipleAccounts x10 = 8,358,224 B decoded / 513,307 B gzipped wire; batch-of-3 executed (array of 3 results). ER enforces NO 100-account cap (101 accounts -> HTTP 200); public devnet returns -32602 above 100 and its own HTTP 413 'request body size exceeds allowed maximum' at ~54 KB body (threshold between 50,499 B/N=1400 pass and 54,099 B/N=1500 reject). Body-cap math: 2000 orderbook pubkeys = 96,098 B (<100,000 cap) -> 1.67 GB egress; 2075 = 99,698 B -> 1.73 GB. Proxy guards verified: getProgramAccounts -> 403; batch of 21 -> 403; 101 KB body -> 413 'request body too large' (route msg); GET unknown layer -> 200, POST unknown layer -> 404. pyth/history: normal window -> 200/1236 B src:Coinbase; to=9007199254740991 and to=1e308 -> HTTP 500, 0-byte body (unhandled RangeError). Faucet: invalid wallet -> 400, oversized body -> 413, empty -> 400 (all no-mint); operator cost/drip 0.05 SOL top-up + 0.00203928 SOL ATA rent (165-byte, from prior audit) + fee ~= 0.052 SOL; at the 60/hr cap -> ~3.12 SOL/hr + 600,000 test USDC/hr; deploy.json authority balance 1,850,355,758 lamports (1.85 SOL). Live /api/status: base.ok:true degraded:TRUE (keyed base primary currently failing over to public), er.ok:true, indexer.lastFillAt 1787407245120 vs at 1788584897525 = ~13.6 days stale but surfaced with no staleness flag, keepers.ageSecs 25497 (~7.1 h) stalled:false (implies market fundingIntervalSecs > ~8499 s). Deployment is a single long-running Next process on the droplet (not Vercel serverless), so the faucet's in-memory limiters persist across requests until a restart -- the prior audit's 'resets every invocation' claim does not hold here; the binding bound is the global 60/hr cap.

**Not covered:** SSRF/relay-pivot: the proxy honours NO caller-supplied URL or forwarding header -- the only routing input is the `layer` path segment gated through Object.hasOwn(UPSTREAMS,layer) (route.ts:311), so base/er are the only reachable upstreams; not a pivot. Confirmed clean, no finding. Error-envelope key leakage on the proxy and faucet: both log the raw error (which can embed the keyed upstream URL) server-side only and return fixed classified messages (route.ts:171-176, faucet :386-419) -- clean on this branch; the prior S8-06 raw-error echo in pyth/history is FIXED (now returns a fixed errmsg, getWithRetry logs status only). S8-07 (unencoded from/to URL injection) is FIXED -- from/to are Number()-parsed so `&`/`#` injection is impossible, though the missing range/integer bound is now finding F2. The faucet X-Forwarded-For trust question (does Caddy append vs pass through the leftmost) could not be settled read-only -- there is no echo endpoint and probing it would require minting; the finding I filed (F4) does not depend on that answer because a single honest IP already saturates the whole 60/hr cap. I did not exercise the full ~2000-account getMultipleAccounts against the live proxy (it would pull >1 GB from the deployer's paid quota -- the exact harm); the 1.7 GB figure is a linear extrapolation from the measured 50-account (41.8 MB) and 10-account-via-proxy (8.35 MB) points. The market fundingIntervalSecs and the full Market/OrderBook decode are outside the api-routes dimension.


### [high/both] RPC proxy caps request bytes and batch count but never account count, so one ~100 KB getMultipleAccounts pulls ~1.7 GB from the paid RPC quota

`frontend/src/app/api/rpc/[layer]/route.ts:285`  · prior: S8-01

**Evidence.** methodsAllowed (:285-301) validates only that each call's `.method` is a string in ALLOWED_METHODS and that a batch has 1..20 calls; it never reads `.params`. `getMultipleAccounts` is allowlisted (:239). The only size bounds are the 100 KB body cap (MAX_BODY_BYTES :222, readCapped :271-283) and the 20-call batch cap (:293). Neither bounds the accounts array. LIVE MEASURED against the ER upstream (https://devnet.magicblock.app), read-only 2026-09-05: getAccountInfo(orderBook 83zMFL...) = 835,887 B; getMultipleAccounts with the orderbook duplicated 50x = a 2,499 B request returning 41,790,824 B (41.8 MB, 19.9 s). The ER enforces NO per-call account limit (a 101-account request returned HTTP 200 with data, whereas public devnet returns -32602 above 100 and a ~50 KB body 413). A single getMultipleAccounts with 2075 orderbook pubkeys is a 99,698 B body (under the 100,000 cap) -> ~2075 x 835,887 = 1.73 GB upstream egress; 2000 pubkeys = 96,098 B -> 1.67 GB. The batch form also works: a batch of 20 getMultipleAccounts x 100 accounts each fits ~94 KB and is ~1.67 GB even where the upstream enforces the 100-account cap; verified end-to-end that the LIVE proxy /api/rpc/er executes batches (a batch of 3 returned an array of 3 results). attemptUpstream retries up to 3x on 5xx (:106,:128-135), tripling worst-case cost when the upstream drops the request.

**Failure.** Any anonymous internet client turns a single ~96 KB POST into 1.7 GB of upstream reads on the deployer's paid RPC quota (and MagicBlock ER quota). Looped, this is a remote trigger for the same quota-exhaustion outage the file's own comment (:217-221) says burned the quota before -- and the base primary is ALREADY reading degraded live (see measured), so headroom is thin. On mainnet it is a paid-egress bill an outsider writes.

**Fix.** In methodsAllowed, when method is getMultipleAccounts sum params[0].length across all calls in the request and reject if the total exceeds a small N (e.g. 100, matching the standard RPC per-call limit); reject getMultipleAccounts calls whose params[0] is not an array. NEEDS-DEPLOY (frontend redeploy).


### [medium/both] /api/pyth/history returns a bare HTTP 500 (empty body) for an out-of-range `to`, breaking its JSON error contract

`frontend/src/app/api/pyth/history/route.ts:124`

**Evidence.** The GET handler (:63-89) has no try/catch. Input validation (:70) checks only Number.isFinite(from/to), from>0, to>from -- never Number.isSafeInteger or an upper bound. fetchCoinbase then does `new Date((to - span) * 1000).toISOString()` (:124) and `new Date(to * 1000).toISOString()` (:125); for to greater than ~8.64e12 seconds the Date is Invalid and toISOString() throws a RangeError that nothing catches, so Next returns its default 500. LIVE MEASURED 2026-09-05: `GET /api/pyth/history?...&from=1&to=9007199254740991` -> HTTP 500, 0-byte body; `to=1e308` -> HTTP 500, 0-byte body; a normal window -> HTTP 200, 1236 B `src:Coinbase`. This route is the chart's history source, reachable unauthenticated.

**Failure.** An unauthenticated caller crashes the request with a value the validator accepts; the chart panel gets an opaque 500 instead of the graceful `{s:'error',errmsg:'Price history is unavailable right now.'}` the route is written to return, and the 500 carries no cache-control so it is uncacheable churn.

**Fix.** Require Number.isSafeInteger(from) && Number.isSafeInteger(to) and a max (to - from) span in the :70 guard before building any URL, or wrap the GET body in try/catch returning json({s:'error',errmsg:'...'},502). NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [medium/both] /api/status always returns HTTP 200, so the deploy smoke test cannot detect an RPC outage after a deploy

`frontend/src/app/api/status/route.ts:150`  · prior: S8-05

**Evidence.** GET (:150-161) unconditionally returns Response.json(cached) with HTTP 200; base/er failures are carried as `ok:false` INSIDE a 200 body (getSlot :78-84 returns {ok:false,slot:null}). The post-deploy smoke test in .github/workflows/deploy.yml:74-84 asserts only `[ "$code" = "200" ]` for /api/status (and for /, /trade, /docs). So a deploy performed during a total base+er RPC outage passes the smoke test green -- the one automated gate meant to catch a broken deploy is blind to the exact outage class that took this system down for 17 days. LIVE 2026-09-05 the endpoint returns 200 with base.ok:true/degraded:true, er.ok:true; there is no code path that returns non-200.

**Failure.** Operators get a green deploy and a green /api/status while the chain reads are dead; the health signal the last outage was supposed to teach them to watch reports success through the next one.

**Fix.** Return HTTP 503 from /api/status when both base.ok and er.ok are false (keep 200 for degraded), or make the smoke test parse the JSON and fail on ok:false. NEEDS-DEPLOY (route) / CI change.


### [medium/both] Faucet per-IP cooldown (60/hr) equals the global cap (60/hr) and per-wallet is defeated by fresh pubkeys, so one client drains the operator at the full cap

`frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:86`  · prior: S8-02

**Evidence.** Three limiters: per-wallet COOLDOWN_MS=60_000 (:53), global GLOBAL_MAX_PER_HOUR=60 (:83), per-IP IP_COOLDOWN_MS=60_000 (:86). The per-IP cooldown permits 1 drip/60 s = 60/hr, numerically identical to the global 60/hr cap, so a single IP is allowed to consume 100% of the global budget; the per-wallet cooldown is bypassed with fresh keypairs (free, as the file's own comment concedes :78-82). Each first-time drip costs the operator 0.05 SOL top-up (:343, gated by bal < 0.02 SOL floor :336 which is always true at bal=0) + ATA rent 0.00203928 SOL (measured, 165-byte account) + fees ~= 0.052 SOL, and mints 10,000 test USDC (:319). At the cap: 60 x 0.052 = ~3.12 SOL/hr drained and 600,000 test USDC/hr minted into the live market. The operator key falls back to KEEPER_KEYPAIR (:190-196), so draining its SOL stops the keepers. deploy.json authority balance measured live at 1.85 SOL -- these keys run thin.

**Failure.** An anonymous client with fresh pubkeys, from one IP, drains the operator/keeper SOL at ~3.12 SOL/hr (emptying a ~2 SOL key in well under an hour, which halts keeper fee payment) and inflates the live market by 600,000 test USDC/hr, distorting every position and liquidation. Worthless devnet token keeps it below critical.

**Fix.** Set the per-IP cap materially below the global cap (e.g. IP 5/hr, global 60/hr) so one client cannot saturate the budget, and require a sign-message proof of pubkey ownership before the operator pays ATA rent + SOL top-up. NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [low/both] FAUCET_MAX_PER_HOUR is parsed with Number() and never validated, so a non-numeric value silently removes the only durable faucet bound

`frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:83`  · prior: S8-03

**Evidence.** `const GLOBAL_MAX_PER_HOUR = Number(process.env.FAUCET_MAX_PER_HOUR || "60")` (:83) is unvalidated. Number('sixty') is NaN, and globalCapReached returns `dripTimes.length >= GLOBAL_MAX_PER_HOUR` (:150-153) -- every comparison against NaN is false, so an unparseable value does not fall back to 60 and does not error: it deletes the global cap silently, turning finding F4's bounded 3.12 SOL/hr into unbounded. Still unfixed on factory/remediate (the prior audit's S8-03). Same untreated Number() pattern at :43 (FAUCET_USDC), :50 (FAUCET_SOL), :52 (SOL_TOPUP_FLOOR) -- those fail closed, only this one flips a guard off.

**Failure.** One typo or shell-quoting slip in the deploy env removes the faucet's global cap with no log and no error; the route behaves normally until someone loops it, then drains the operator without bound. Requires operator misconfig, not attacker action.

**Fix.** Parse once with a guard: `const n = Number(process.env.FAUCET_MAX_PER_HOUR); const GLOBAL_MAX_PER_HOUR = Number.isFinite(n) && n > 0 ? n : 60;` NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [low/both] RPC proxy GET answers 200 {ok:true,layer} for any layer, including ones POST rejects with 404

`frontend/src/app/api/rpc/[layer]/route.ts:339`  · prior: S8-10

**Evidence.** GET (:339-347) echoes any `layer` back with {ok:true,layer} and status 200; POST (:311-317) gates on Object.hasOwn(UPSTREAMS,layer) and 404s unknown layers. LIVE MEASURED 2026-09-05: `GET /api/rpc/doesnotexist` -> HTTP 200 {"ok":true,"layer":"doesnotexist"}; `POST /api/rpc/doesnotexist` -> HTTP 404. The two halves of one route disagree on which layers exist.

**Failure.** A health probe or monitor that GETs the proxy reads any layer name as healthy, so the proxy's layer set is unreliable to probe and a typo'd layer looks live. Hygiene, no exploit path.

**Fix.** Apply the same Object.hasOwn(UPSTREAMS, layer) check in GET and 404 unknown layers so both verbs agree. NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [low/both] /api/trades wallet query sorts an unbounded row set (WHERE maker=? OR taker=? ORDER BY), full temp-B-tree sort on an anonymous request

`frontend/src/app/api/trades/route.ts:72`  · prior: S8-09

**Evidence.** The wallet path (:72-75) is `SELECT * FROM fills WHERE maker = ? OR taker = ? ORDER BY sequence DESC LIMIT ?`; SQLite resolves the OR with a multi-index scan but must materialise and sort every matching row via a temp B-tree before LIMIT (prior audit's EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN, unchanged on this branch). fills retention is unbounded by default (keepers/src/shared/fill-db.ts). The caller picks `wallet`, so an anonymous request can name the market-maker bot -- party to nearly every fill -- and pay for a full table sort even at limit=1. Also returns the identical {fills:[],indexed:false} for a missing DB (:58-59) and a thrown/corrupt read (:84-87), so a broken indexer is indistinguishable from an idle one. LIVE the DB is absent (returns indexed:false), so no impact until the fill-log keeper repopulates it.

**Failure.** Once the fills table accumulates, an anonymous caller forces an unbounded per-request sort on a host shared with the keepers, and operators cannot tell a broken indexer from a fresh one. Low today because the DB does not exist live.

**Fix.** Replace the OR with `UNION ALL` of two index-driven halves each with its own ORDER BY sequence DESC LIMIT ?; return a distinct error state from the catch (e.g. {fills:[],indexed:false,error:'unavailable'}). NEEDS-DEPLOY.

### [high/repo] Phantom embedded-wallet session lives in app-origin IndexedDB and signs owner transactions with no user prompt, so any same-origin script gets silent owner-level signing for 31 days

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/frontend/src/components/wallet/wallet-provider.tsx:9`

**Evidence.** wallet-provider.tsx:9 claims the embedded wallet's "key [is] held by Phantom, never in app storage"; :95 embeddedWalletType "user-wallet", :96 autoConnect:true. @phantom/browser-sdk 2.0.2 (frontend/package.json:16-17) persists the whole session object to IndexedDB db "phantom-browser-sdk" store "sessions" key "currentSession" (browser-sdk/dist/index.js:2465-2466, saveSession :2503-2521) including bearerToken/walletId/organizationId/authUserId written at :2804-2809 after the OAuth exchange. The request-signing key is a WebCrypto Ed25519 CryptoKey generated with extractable=false (indexed-db-stamper/dist/index.js:232-236) and stored in db "phantom-indexed-db-stamper" store "crypto-keys" (:62-63); non-extractable only blocks exportKey — any same-origin script can indexedDB.open() both DBs and call crypto.subtle.sign (:124) with the stored key. embedded-provider-core/dist/index.js:995-1032 signTransaction() is a straight client.signTransaction() POST to https://api.phantom.app/v1/wallets (constants/dist/index.js:379) with no in-page confirmation UI; auto-confirm APIs exist only for the injected provider (browser-sdk README:812-814). Authenticator lifetime AUTHENTICATOR_EXPIRATION_TIME_MS = 31 days (embedded-provider-core:53). The app never calls disconnect/clearSession (grep 0 hits in frontend/src). Every owner-only instruction — withdraw_collateral.rs:73-75, close_position.rs:106, withdraw_trading_credit, close_trading_credit, authorize_session.rs:57-59 (which S1-07 shows accepts any expiry) — is therefore reachable from an XSS with no popup, unlike the injected-extension path where the extension prompts. Not active on the live site today: the live chunk /_next/static/chunks/0kf_._pv3h7tk.js still reads `t.default.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID` through the browser process polyfill (env={}) so providers resolves to ["injected"]; README.md:117-119 and frontend/README.md:46 say the Google/Apple config is the intended one.

**Failure.** With NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID set (the documented config), one script executing on slipstream.ansht.tech — via the unsanitized /docs renderer, a compromised npm dependency (mermaid is a 2.5 MB dynamic import), or a build-time DOCS_DIR — can, without any popup, sign withdraw_collateral to an arbitrary token account, authorize_session with a far-future expiry, and close positions for every Google/Apple user who has logged in within 31 days. The custody bound the codebase reasons from ("session key can only place/cancel") does not hold for embedded-wallet users.

**Fix.** Treat the CSP (headers finding) and docs sanitization as blockers before ever setting NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID; rewrite wallet-provider.tsx:9 to say the signing session (bearer token + stamper CryptoKey) lives in this origin's IndexedDB for 31 days. The app cannot move the SDK's key out of the origin, so the only real mitigation is making XSS unreachable.


### [high/both] An honest ER matches limit orders at any price with no band and no size cap, so two wallets can wash-fill at $0.001 and settle unbounded PnL against the L1 vault — this also voids S9-04's 'churn inside one credit' bound for a stolen session key

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/programs/slipstream/src/instructions/place_order.rs:173`  · prior: S4-C8

**Evidence.** place_order.rs:173-177: for every non-MARKET order `reference_price = price` (the order's own limit); :181-183 margin_required = compute_notional(size, price)/max_leverage — a SELL of 1,000,000 SOL at $0.001 needs $50 of credit at 20x (deploy.json maxLeverage 20). No band against mark and no size cap exist: the only size checks are `size == 0` (:98) and lot alignment (:134); the slippage window at :230-240 applies to MARKET only. Self-trade rejection compares credit owners (S2-C6), so the attacker needs two wallets, which the faucet hands out. settle_from_log.rs:173,315,326 applies fill.price verbatim (S4-C8 table: price → "none"); R4's ledger bound (:233-246) caps only filled_margin, not entry price. close_position.rs:226-231 checked_add's `settlement = collateral + pnl` into free_collateral with no counterparty; the loser's deficit goes to the insurance fund and then to bad debt (:232-240; S3-05 pattern). withdraw_collateral gates only on free_collateral (S3-05). Deployed revision e1304e1 (ProgramData slot 484488785, blockTime 1786894734 = 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z) has the identical block at place_order.rs:159-166. Extends S4-C8: that contract assumes a hostile ER authors the price; here an honest ER produces it from a user limit order. Live reach today is gated by two outages, not by code: the fill-log keeper is stopped (fills never reach L1) and close_position is blocked whenever the 60s mark gate is stale (Pyth publishing every ~309s). Session-key relevance: a stolen 24h key (S9-04) lets the attacker post the victim's side of this wash fill, so the key's blast radius is the victim's whole available credit plus a vault drain, not order churn.

**Failure.** Whenever settlement runs and the mark is fresh, an attacker with two faucet wallets ($10,000 test USDC each) opens opposite positions at $0.001 on the ER, lets the short get liquidated (deficit to a $20 insurance fund, then bad debt) and closes the long at mark ≈ $200 for ~$200M of free_collateral, then withdraw_collateral drains the USDC vault (ER credits alone total $62,485.68 measured). On devnet this is test USDC; the mechanism is mainnet-fatal.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. In place_order.rs next to :173, reject a limit `price` outside a band around market.mark_price_for_close(now) (or fall back to OracleStale like MARKET does), and cap positive `settlement` in close_position/liquidate_position at what the counterparty side actually posted. Do not rely on the stopped keeper as the guard.


### [medium/both] The 64-byte session secret is still written to localStorage, enumerable without knowing the owner, and never cleared on sign-out, owner switch, expiry, undelegate or withdraw

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:201`  · prior: S9-04

**Evidence.** Restates S9-04 / S12-X01 on this branch: storeSession writes Array.from(keypair.secretKey) under `slipstream:session:<owner>:<market>` (use-session.ts:165-167, :170-172, :190-205, setItem at :201) after each successful delegate/rotate/fund flow (:776, :939, :1250); loadStoredSession :174-187 reads it back on every 5s poll; TTL 24h (:62). `grep -rn removeItem frontend/src` = 0 hits, so the secret outlives the on-chain session, a wallet switch, undelegate and withdraw. New: the key prefix is fixed, so an injected script needs no owner pubkey — `Object.keys(localStorage).filter(k=>k.startsWith('slipstream:session:'))` returns every secret ever stored in that profile; and the same-origin /api/rpc/er proxy allowlists sendTransaction (rpc/[layer]/route.ts:246) so exfiltration is not even required. Program side bounds the key to exactly two instructions in both revisions: is_authorized_signer (state/trading_credit.rs:138-145) has two callers, place_order.rs:150 and cancel_order.rs:56; authorize_session.rs:57-59 and every money path check credit/position/user.owner == signer, so the key cannot extend itself or move funds — but see the wash-fill finding for what place_order alone permits. Live: 21 TradingCredits on the ER, 8 have carried a session, 0 unexpired at 2026-09-05T04:55Z; the largest session-bearing credit is $18,845.65 (DxnhAUbP, expired 2026-08-28).

**Failure.** Any script on the origin, any extension with storage access, or anyone with the browser profile recovers a live signing key for up to 24h per delegation and can place/cancel orders against the victim's credit on the ER — and, via the off-market limit-order path, post the victim as the losing side of a wash fill.

**Fix.** Three-line fix: localStorage.removeItem(sessionStorageKey(owner, market)) when loadStoredSession sees expiry <= now, when publicKey changes/disconnects, and after undelegate/withdraw/close. Stronger, ~15 lines: keep the key as a non-extractable WebCrypto Ed25519 CryptoKey in IndexedDB and sign with subtle.sign + tx.addSignature, so an XSS can use it only while resident, never exfiltrate it.


### [medium/deployed] Zero security headers on every measured endpoint; the origin is Caddy (not Vercel) so vercel.json is dead config; the minimum CSP that keeps the app working is small and known

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/frontend/next.config.ts:3`  · prior: S10-X03

**Evidence.** next.config.ts:3-5 is empty; no src/proxy.ts or middleware.ts exists; vercel.json:1-13 sets no headers and is inert anyway — live responses carry `via: 1.1 Caddy`, `x-nextjs-prerender: 1`, `x-powered-by: Next.js` (faucet/route.ts:103 confirms Caddy is the only hop). Measured 2026-09-05T04:51Z on https://slipstream.ansht.tech for /, /trade, /docs, /docs/06-session-keys, /api/status, /api/rpc/base: no Content-Security-Policy, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy, Strict-Transport-Security or Permissions-Policy on any; /api/rpc/er answers a cross-origin POST with no Access-Control-* (good, same-origin only). Constraints for a CSP, measured from the live HTML and the code: /trade SSR has 7 inline <script> blocks (theme bootstrap layout.tsx:63-68, hash sha256-HjgeJMy73nd/ePg5wudDVldXi9h32VlWK0ur0XSPOSg=, plus 6 Next RSC flight pushes that change per build) and 14 same-origin chunks; /docs/00-architecture-diagrams has 12 inline scripts; 0 style= attributes and 0 <style> tags in either SSR document; no eval/new Function in the 14 /trade chunks or in the Phantom SDK dist. Network: all RPC goes through same-origin /api/rpc/* (manifest.ts:166-170), fetches to /api/trades, /api/pyth/history, /api/faucet, /api/status, and ONE direct socket: use-live-price.ts:118-119 opens wss://devnet.magicblock.app. Fonts are next/font self-hosted (layout.tsx:11-27); only image is /logo-32.png; canvas (price-chart.tsx) and WAAPI are not CSP-governed; mermaid (mermaid-runner.tsx:21, securityLevel strict :39) injects a <style> into its SVG; the Phantom react-sdk styles via CSSOM props and reaches connect.phantom.app by top-level navigation (browser-sdk:2747), api/auth.phantom.app by fetch only when an appId is set; the extension provider is exempt from page CSP.

**Failure.** No second line of defence: a single injected script anywhere on the origin reads the localStorage session secret and (with the embedded wallet enabled) the Phantom signing session; the site can also be framed for clickjacking of the Withdraw/rotate popups.

**Fix.** next.config.ts: poweredByHeader:false and headers() returning `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-<req>' 'strict-dynamic'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' wss://devnet.magicblock.app; frame-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'` (add https://api.phantom.app https://auth.phantom.app to connect-src only when NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID is set; 'unsafe-eval' in dev only), plus X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin, HSTS. The nonce needs a src/proxy.ts (Next 16's middleware) and turns the year-cached static shells (cache-control: s-maxage=31536000, x-nextjs-cache: HIT) dynamic — accept that, or hash the 7 scripts per build. style-src can drop 'unsafe-inline' everywhere except /docs (mermaid's SVG <style>).


### [medium/both] Docs pipeline still renders unsanitized marked output into the session-secret origin; marked 14 passes raw HTML and javascript: hrefs through, proven on the live site, and the build reads markdown from an env-selected directory

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/frontend/src/lib/docs.ts:104`  · prior: S9-05

**Evidence.** Restates S9-05 on this branch: docs.ts:104 marked.parse → :107 rewriteLinks → dangerouslySetInnerHTML at app/docs/page.tsx:12 and app/docs/[slug]/page.tsx:39; the mermaid runner re-decodes the escaped source and writes it back with innerHTML on every themechange (mermaid-runner.tsx:28-33, dispatched by theme-toggle.tsx:28). No sanitizer anywhere (grep dompurify|sanitize = 0). New evidence: marked 14.1.4's cleanUrl (lib/marked.cjs:81-89) only encodeURIs — `marked.parse('[x](javascript:alert(1)) <img src=x onerror=alert(2)>')` returns both verbatim (run locally against the pinned tarball); the live page https://slipstream.ansht.tech/docs/01-architecture-overview contains a literal `<term>` element from docs/01-architecture-overview.md:203, i.e. raw HTML from markdown is reaching the production DOM today (it swallows the word 'term'). Content provenance: scripts/copy-docs.mjs:13 takes `process.env.DOCS_DIR` first, so whoever controls the build environment controls the HTML; all 38 raw `<br` tags in docs/00-architecture-diagrams.md sit inside ```mermaid fences (measured), so dropping raw-HTML tokens outside fences breaks nothing.

**Failure.** Any attacker-influenced markdown — a docs PR, a build-time DOCS_DIR, a future user-supplied doc — executes on the origin that holds the session secret (and, with the embedded wallet, the Phantom signing session); with no CSP nothing stops it.

**Fix.** No new dependency: in getDoc set `renderer.html = () => ""` and make `renderer.link`/`renderer.image` return plain text unless href matches /^(https?:|\/|#|\.\/)/; in mermaid-runner.tsx store the escaped markup (el.innerHTML) in data-src, or restore with el.textContent = src instead of innerHTML. Pair with the CSP.


### [medium/deployed] The faucet accepts a cross-site simple POST, so any web page can drip to an attacker wallet using each visitor's IP as a fresh cooldown bucket

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:237`

**Evidence.** POST() at faucet/route.ts:237-247 reads the raw body with readCapped and JSON.parses it with no Content-Type, Origin or Sec-Fetch-Site check; the only anti-abuse controls are the per-wallet cooldown (:63), the per-IP cooldown (:86, IP taken from the rightmost X-Forwarded-For, :100-116) and GLOBAL_MAX_PER_HOUR = 60 (:83). A `fetch('https://slipstream.ansht.tech/api/faucet',{method:'POST',mode:'no-cors',body:'{"wallet":"<attacker>"}'})` from any origin is a CORS 'simple request' (text/plain), needs no preflight and is sent with the visitor's IP. Measured 2026-09-05T04:56Z: a cross-origin text/plain POST with Origin: https://evil.example and an invalid wallet returned HTTP 400 `Provide a valid base58 wallet pubkey.` in 0.39s — the body was parsed and the drip path reached (deliberately tested with an invalid key so nothing was minted). Each drip is FAUCET_USDC 10,000 test USDC (:43) + up to FAUCET_SOL 0.05 SOL and ATA rent from the operator key (:50-53). The legitimate client always sends application/json (use-session.ts:630-634). S8-04 covered the forgeable header; this bypasses the cooldown with real third-party IPs.

**Failure.** An attacker embedding the fetch on any page they control drains the operator's SOL at the global cap (60/h ≈ 3 SOL/h plus rent) and mints 600,000 test USDC/h of collateral into the live market to wallets they own, which is exactly the counterparty capital the wash-fill finding needs.

**Fix.** One line at the top of POST(): `if (!req.headers.get('content-type')?.startsWith('application/json')) return json({ok:false,error:'unsupported media type'},415);` — a JSON content type is a non-simple request, so browsers preflight it and the route's absence of CORS headers blocks it. Optionally also require Sec-Fetch-Site to be same-origin/none.


### [medium/both] With the extension wallet, an XSS reaches owner funds by rewriting the destination of a withdraw the user is already approving, because withdraw_collateral never binds user_token_acc to the owner

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/programs/slipstream/src/instructions/withdraw_collateral.rs:142`  · prior: S1-06

**Evidence.** On the live (injected-only) build the owner signs through the extension, which prompts — so an XSS needs the user to click. use-wallet-compat.ts:96-99 passes the Transaction object to `solana.signTransaction` (a writable property on the injected window.phantom.solana object) and submits the returned bytes itself; the withdraw builder derives the destination client-side (use-session.ts:1161-1176: getAssociatedTokenAddress(USDC_MINT, publicKey) → userTokenAccount). withdraw_collateral.rs:28-40 destructures user_token_acc and :142 transfers to it with no owner/ATA/mint check (S1-06 notes only the vault≠dest gap; deposit_collateral pins the vault the same way). A same-origin script that wraps signTransaction and swaps key index 3 makes the popup the user expects for 'Withdraw all to wallet' pay the attacker's USDC account; Phantom's simulation shows only a missing +USDC, not a foreign program. Same code in deployed revision e1304e1. Session keys cannot do this (owner-only, :44-46, :73-75) — it needs the owner's one click.

**Failure.** One XSS plus one legitimate withdraw click sends the user's entire free_collateral to an attacker token account; the transaction is genuinely owner-signed so nothing on chain can distinguish it.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: in withdraw_collateral (and withdraw_trading_credit/close paths that pay tokens out) require user_token_acc to be an SPL token account whose owner == owner.key() and mint == market quote mint, or the canonical ATA. Client needs no change. This tightens, not weakens, authorization.


### [low/deployed] README advertises Google/Apple sign-in on the live demo, but the deployed bundle was built without NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID and offers the extension only

`/home/anshtyagi/Documents/slip-grant/SlipStream/README.md:117`  · prior: S8-C7

**Evidence.** README.md:117-119 and :126 say the live demo needs no extension because Google/Apple creates an in-app wallet. wallet-provider.tsx:88-91 offers google/apple only when appId is set. The live chunk https://slipstream.ansht.tech/_next/static/chunks/0kf_._pv3h7tk.js contains `let e=t.default.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID ... providers:e?["google","apple","injected"]:["injected"]` — the reference was not inlined at build time (Turbopack inlines set NEXT_PUBLIC_ vars as literals; the unset NEXT_PUBLIC_SESSION_FUND_SOL is left unreplaced the same way at 0czoi0fnfec0n.js) and resolves through the browser `process` polyfill in 007d_-r1-s95d.js whose env is `{}`; trade.html contains no NEXT_PUBLIC string. Local frontend/.env.local holds only BASE_RPC_UPSTREAM. Not verified in a running browser (see notCovered).

**Failure.** A new user without a Phantom extension follows the README's click path and cannot sign in; conversely, the moment an operator 'fixes' this by setting the appId, the embedded-session finding above goes live with no CSP in front of it.

**Fix.** Either set NEXT_PUBLIC_PHANTOM_APP_ID at build time after the CSP and docs sanitizer land, or change README.md:117-119/:126 to say the live demo currently requires the Phantom extension.



## fe-hooks-async  (20 findings)

**Measured:** Live, 2026-09-05, public devnet + devnet.magicblock.app, reads only. TradingCredit population: 52 delegated 96-byte credits (owner DELeGGvX…, disc 6), 1 program-owned; 52 UserAccounts (56B, disc 3), 52/52 with reserved_margin == 0 at offset 48. Base-vs-ER divergence over all 52 delegated credits: 20 differ in credit/committed/activeOrders/session fields; 19 have ER.credit < base.credit; 0 have ER.credit > base; 22 carry credit == 7,017,392,213,830,636,841 on both layers; 32 have ER activeOrders > 0 vs 22 on the base copy. Worst cases: dd4KqsiCA7by2DxW4SWdFWEkU94sNVSJ9Mq3ArwbqLa base 5,000.000000 USDC vs ER 6.565900; 37PvXBYWaup4MtYTA3bw57yoJAPHA5gzSCCZ3iSgBfVg base 0 orders/0 committed vs ER 12 orders/122.2529 committed; 5aGGXYNYiPwhNj6a9J6ESE899buqRrT2SZHw6ZpCWdft base 10,000 vs ER 4,994.372980 + 12 orders/88.9847 committed; 2Fe6uBNMiTLrTE14Y3yMnv7HpiYK4BzsHJQZEPNtv9UJ base copy carries sessionAuthority 3tLMmA6263Etc5gpQZ1mS92KbsuW5XwugbcL7Mb7N1tX (proves the authority is in the L1 snapshot). Latency: base getMultipleAccounts(4 keys) 244/253/252/285/370ms, over 20 more: p50 378ms max 681ms, 0 failures; ER getAccountInfo 363/411/358/375/269ms, over 40 at 1/s: p50 325ms max 890ms, 0/40 failures; base getSignatureStatuses 246/291/369ms; getProgramAccounts(DELEG, 96B) 398ms. ER blockhash: lastValidBlockHeight - slot = 1200, ~20 slots/s, isBlockhashValid true at t+45s, false at t+60s (window ≈ 60s). Proxy worst-case per client request (both upstreams dead): 2 × (3 × 20s + 1.5s backoff) ≈ 123s. Mount counts on /trade: useSession ×4 (session-panel, order-form, positions-table, open-orders). Phantom react-sdk 2.0.2: `addresses` is React state set only on connect/disconnect (index.mjs:766-830), so `publicKey` from use-wallet-compat.ts:60-70 is referentially stable — no effect-thrash from that dep.

**Not covered:** Read-only, so no race was reproduced by executing a flow (two-tab setup, mid-withdraw order, navigation during waitForUndelegation); each is argued from code paths plus measured latencies. Phantom embedded-wallet signTransaction behaviour for ER-blockhash transactions (whether it simulates against devnet and refuses) not examined. The K5 half-delegated deadlock (a second undelegate on an ER account whose flip is pending) cannot be probed without sending a transaction. Concurrent-render tearing in lib/shared-source.ts (mutable registry read during render without useSyncExternalStore) judged improbable and not tested. client/src (the diverged second SDK copy) and the 7 integration scripts were out of this dimension. Frontend node_modules are absent locally (cleanit), so no test/lint run; the Phantom SDK was read from a scratchpad copy. S9-03's RPC-rate table was not re-measured beyond the useSession ×4 mount count.


### [high/repo] withdraw() sends the full ER-authored credit as the withdraw amount and knows nothing about the R1 ledger, so the moment the remediate program ships the UI withdraw fails for 52/52 existing credits (0x138 rendered as "custom 0x138") and can never pass for the 22 credits holding 7.0e18

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1128`

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:1123-1138 — leg 2 reads `creditInfo`, sets `credit = decodeTradingCredit(...).credit` and sends `createWithdrawTradingCreditInstruction(publicKey, marketIndex, credit, PROGRAM_ID)`; nothing in the file reads the UserAccount ledger (`grep -rn 'seed_credit_ledger|reserved_margin|creditOutstanding' frontend/src` → only accounts.ts:244,258 which decodes it, and use-positions.ts:19 in the unused useUserAccount). Repo program: withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63-65 `if user.reserved_margin < amount { return Err(CreditCeilingExceeded) }`; seed_credit_ledger.rs:11-14 documents that every pre-existing credit's ledger reads zero until the operator seeds it, and :80-82 writes `min(credit.credit, amount)` so the ledger is always <= the authority's number, never the ER value. errors.ts:24-37 ERR_NAMES ends at PositionStillOpen (56 entries) while error.rs:66-69 appended CreditCeilingExceeded/FillSequenceOutOfRange/LegacyLayoutRejected, so decodeProgramError (errors.ts:88) renders the refusal as `Rejected on chain: custom 0x138.`; ERR_ADVICE has no ReservedMarginExists entry either, which withdraw_collateral.rs:85-87 now raises on leg 3 whenever the ledger outlives the credit. LIVE (2026-09-05, api.devnet.solana.com): 52 delegated 96-byte credits + 1 program-owned; 52 UserAccounts, 52/52 have reserved_margin == 0 at offset 48; 22/52 credits carry credit == 7,017,392,213,830,636,841 on BOTH layers.

**Failure.** On the day the repo program is deployed, every existing user who presses "Withdraw all to wallet" gets leg 1 (undelegate) to land — the credit is now on L1 — then leg 2 refuses with a message that is a hex number. For the 22 whales-by-ER-accident the frontend can never withdraw at all, because it always sends credit.credit (7e18) and the ledger can never equal that; they need an amount <= the seeded ledger, which the UI never computes. Money sits on L1 behind a UI that cannot move it; the panel keeps showing the credit as available.

**Fix.** In withdraw() read the UserAccount before leg 2 (it is already read for leg 3 at :1143) and send `amount = user.creditOutstanding > 0n ? min(credit, user.creditOutstanding) : credit` (the `> 0n` branch keeps the deployed program working, where the ledger is always 0 and ignored); append the three new names to ERR_NAMES and add advice for CreditCeilingExceeded ("ledger not seeded yet — contact the operator") and ReservedMarginExists. Frontend-only, no program change.

**Verifier verdicts (partial):**
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→repo): CORE DEFECT CONFIRMED, QUANTIFICATION AND WHALE MECHANISM WRONG.

Confirmed by code: frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1123-1132 decodes `credit.credit` from the freshly-undelegated L1 copy and :1134 sends it verbatim as the withdraw amount; nothing in withdraw() reads UserAccount offset 48 (the :1143-1148 read only pulls freeCollateral). Repo program programs/slipstream/src/instructions/withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63-65 refuses with CreditCeilingExceeded when `user.reserved_margin < amount`; that gate landed in 385414d (2026-08-22), after the 2026-05-30 deploy, so it is repo-only. error.rs ordinal count puts CreditCeilingExceeded at 0x100+56 = 0x138; frontend/src/lib/slipstream/errors.ts:24-37 ERR_NAMES has exactly 56 entries (idx 0-55), so errors.ts:88 returns `custom 0x138` and :112 renders "Rejected on chain: custom 0x138." No seed_credit_ledger client exists anywhere in frontend/client/keepers/scripts TS (grep: only the Rust program, tests, spec). Retry re-enters leg 2 (delegated=false) and fails identically until the operator seeds.

Live (2026-09-05, api.devnet.solana.com + devnet.magicblock.app, read-only getProgramAccounts/getMultipleAccounts): 52 UserAccounts, 52/52 reserved_margin==0. 75 modern 96-byte credits total, but only 43 at market_index 0 (deploy.json:13 marketIndex 0); the rest sit at garbage indices 2894/22977/25900/40800/44734.

CORRECTED POPULATION: replicating the :1068-1087 pre-check (`activeOrders>0 || committed>0n` on the ER copy), exactly 21 market-0 credits pass it with credit>0 (20 delegated + 1 program-owned, 29,390.90 USDC of credit, all 21 owners have a UserAccount with ledger 0). Those 21 are the ones that will undelegate then hit 0x138. The other 10 market-0 credits with UserAccounts are pre-check-blocked by real active orders. "52/52" is wrong.

WHALE SUB-CLAIM REFUTED: the 22 credits holding 7,017,392,213,830,636,841 are all at market_index 25900, so use-session.ts's findTradingCreditPda(publicKey, 0) never resolves to them; 0/22 owners have a UserAccount (withdraw_trading_credit.rs:38,47 would reject regardless of frontend); and all 22 carry committed=11,016,084,772,046,666,000 > credit and active_orders=59767 on BOTH layers, so :1081 refuses before leg 1. Even if undelegated, reconcile_credit (state/trading_credit.rs:165-167, called from undelegate_trading_credit.rs:97) saturating-subs credit to 0 and :1130 `credit > 0n` skips leg 2. They never reach the "ledger can never equal 7e18" path. Restates remediate.md Decision 4 / S5-01.

SEVERITY: medium, not high. The zero-ledger-until-seeded state is R1's designed migration (docs/spec/remediate.md:325-328: seeded "after the owner undelegates"); funds are operator-recoverable, bounded to 21 credits / 29.4k devnet USDC, and the frontend's incremental defect is an opaque hex message plus a retry loop, not a new lock. Note the proposed fix's `creditOutstanding > 0n ? min : credit` branch still sends the full credit against an unseeded ledger and still yields 0x138; only the ERR_NAMES/ERR_ADVICE part addresses the deploy-day experience. The absence of any seed_credit_ledger operator tool is a separate ops gap worth its own finding.
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→repo): Not a restatement: R1 (credit ceiling) is the program-side P0; this is the frontend never being updated for it, and neither docs/spec/remediate.md:947 (only the reservedMargin→creditOutstanding decoder rename) nor docs/checks/remediate/R1.md:41 touched frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts or frontend/src/lib/slipstream/errors.ts. Not in the prior audit (s7.md:720/1022 checked the keepers' ERR_NAMES, not the frontend's) and not among today's established items. Target "repo" is right: the deployed program has no ceiling, so the UI works on devnet today and breaks only when the remediate program ships.

Verified on 87f9eb1: use-session.ts:1122-1138 sends decodeTradingCredit(...).credit as the full withdraw amount and never reads the ledger (grep for creditOutstanding hits only accounts.ts:244,258 and use-positions.ts:19,73). withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63-65 gates on user.reserved_margin < amount → CreditCeilingExceeded. error.rs enum has 59 variants (CreditCeilingExceeded = ordinal 56 = 0x138); errors.ts ERR_NAMES has 56 entries ending at PositionStillOpen, so errors.ts:88 renders it as `custom 0x138`, and ERR_ADVICE has no ReservedMarginExists entry. Live devnet 2026-09-05: 52 delegated 96-byte credits + 1 program-owned, 52 UserAccounts all with ledger == 0 at offset 48, 22 credits at 7,017,392,213,830,636,841. All confirmed.

Two parts of the claim are overstated, which is why severity drops to medium:
(1) "The 22 whales can never withdraw" is wrong. All 22 carry committed == 11,016,084,772,046,666,000 (> credit), and the ER order book (83zMFL6…, read from devnet.magicblock.app) has only 30 active slots with a max per-owner margin_reserved of 122,182,500. undelegate_trading_credit.rs:107-112 calls reconcile_credit before the commit, and trading_credit.rs:165-168 does credit.saturating_sub(committed - actual) → credit lands on L1 as 0. use-session.ts:1129 then sees credit == 0n, skips leg 2 entirely, and leg 3 passes withdraw_collateral.rs:85 because the ledger is 0. The proposed min(credit, ledger) is never reached for them.
(2) "52/52 fail" is therefore 30/52, and for those 30 the block is the program's own designed migration gap (docs/spec/remediate.md:325-329: "the moment the cap goes live their payout bound is zero until the operator seeds each one", seeding "after the owner undelegates"). The frontend cannot unlock it; its actual defects are (a) an unreadable hex error with no advice, and (b) sending the full ER credit instead of min(credit, ledger), which only bites if the operator seeds below credit.credit (seed_credit_ledger.rs:80 writes min(credit, amount)). Funds are not lost, the operator's seed unlocks them, and a re-press of Withdraw skips leg 1 (already undelegated) and retries leg 2. Bounded, temporary, no money moves wrongly → medium, not high. The proposed frontend-only fix (read the UserAccount already fetched at :1143 before leg 2, send min when ledger > 0, append the three new ERR_NAMES, add advice for CreditCeilingExceeded and ReservedMarginExists) is sound and touches no protected test. Sibling worth noting: keepers/src/shared/ertx.ts ERR_NAMES (s7.md:720) is now stale by the same three variants.
- `?` → stands (severity→low) (target→repo): Survives only as a low hygiene item; every severe sub-claim is guarded or factually wrong.

DEAD: "22 credits holding 7.0e18 can never withdraw". Live read 2026-09-05 (api.devnet.solana.com, getProgramAccounts on DELeGG… filtered disc=6/len=96): the 22 accounts carrying credit=7,017,392,213,830,636,841 have data market_index=25900, active_orders=59767, committed=11e18, and their addresses derive from NO Slipstream seed — find_program_address([b"credit", owner, u16le(0)]) and (…, u16le(25900)) under 7qujfsb4… both miss (e.g. uydHfurWa9g412dqxhvz62eHyaf57F3cLFjNVKGb6om vs derived 7vSfby…/7vdJDt…). Their MagicBlock delegation record (PDA [b"delegation", account]) names owner program HnezRqHTQm32Jth9wPvZZRNQR2ej8MEcF9ZsUC7abSio, not Slipstream (a real credit, FeN2ja…, names 7qujfsb4…). None of the 22 "owners" has a UserAccount. They are another protocol's delegated accounts colliding on byte0=6/len=96; the UI derives [credit, wallet, 0] (use-session.ts:1063) and can never load them. Even for a genuine credit with committed>credit, use-session.ts:1073-1087 reads the ER copy and refuses before leg 1 when committed>0, and undelegate_trading_credit.rs:98-103 runs reconcile_credit (trading_credit.rs:165-167) which zeroes credit, so leg 2 at :1129-1134 is skipped (credit>0n false) — 0x138 unreachable. (This also invalidates the "22 of 45" premise in docs/spec/remediate.md:333-337.)

DEAD: "52/52". Real population for the 52 UserAccount owners (all reserved_margin==0, confirmed): 46 market-0 credits exist (41 delegation-owned, 5 program-owned), 6 absent; all credits <= 20,000 USDC; 16 of the 41 have ER committed>0/active>0 and are stopped by the :1083 pre-check. At most 30 accounts could hit 0x138 once, and the 5 program-owned ones can be seeded the moment the upgrade lands.

DEAD: "never pass / money stuck behind the UI". The guard is the spec's own grandfather step: seed_credit_ledger.rs:80-82 writes min(credit.credit, amount); seeding with amount >= credit.credit (e.g. u64::MAX) makes ledger == credit.credit, and withdraw() is resumable (:1096 skips leg 1 once program-owned; :1129 sends credit.credit == ledger) so the retry passes. remediate.md:320,327,341-345,572 documents exactly this order (owner undelegates, then operator seeds). Only a deliberate below-credit seed would leave the UI unable to withdraw, and client/src/instructions.ts:480 still exposes an amount-taking path.

WHAT SURVIVES (low, repo): the first post-upgrade UI withdraw of a delegated credit lands leg 1 and then shows "Rejected on chain: custom 0x138." because frontend/src/lib/slipstream/errors.ts:24-37 ERR_NAMES stops at PositionStillOpen (56) while error.rs:66-69 has 59 variants; keepers/src/shared/ertx.ts:21-33 is stale the same way. Appending the three names plus advice, and sending min(credit, creditOutstanding) when creditOutstanding>0 at use-session.ts:1128, is a correct one-file hygiene fix; no funds are locked.


### [medium/both] oneShotSetup's session-key rescue tests the wrong predicate, misses send-phase throws, and discards the key under the exact outage it was written for — a retry then persists a never-authorised key

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:934`

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:923 `const sig = await sendTransaction(tx, connection)` sits OUTSIDE the try at :926-938; use-wallet-compat.ts:96-99 signs then `connection.sendRawTransaction(...)`, so any throw after the upstream accepted the bytes (proxy route.ts:113 `AbortSignal.timeout(20_000)`; a browser fetch flap → 'Failed to fetch' → errors.ts:93,118-121 renders "Can't reach Solana right now — nothing was sent. Check your connection and retry.") propagates to autoStart's catch at :1025 and key #1 is never stored. The catch at :926-938 then catches EVERY confirmSignature throw, not only timeouts: confirm.ts:41-45 throws `Transaction failed: {err}` for an on-chain failure and confirm.ts:36 rethrows any RPC error. Its 'landed' test at :935 is `after?.owner.toBase58() === DELEGATION_PROGRAM` — ownership, not `credit.sessionAuthority` — and the read at :934 is `.catch(() => null)`, so when the RPC that made confirm throw is still down, landed=false and the key is discarded (:936) although the tx lands. Interleaving, single tab: click 1 → send-phase throw → key #1 discarded, user told nothing was sent → click 2 while tx #1 is landing → preflight :824-831 still sees an undelegated credit → tx #2 includes delegate (:884-892) → either (a) preflight simulation fails (owner already delegated) → autoStart catch → chain holds discarded key #1; or (b) simulation passed before #1 confirmed, tx #2 fails on-chain → :934 read now shows owner == DELEGATION_PROGRAM from tx #1 → :939 stores key #2 → :1016 notice "You're ready to trade — orders sign instantly, no wallet popups". Either way readSessionState :324-326 `localMatches` is false → :336 sessionActive=false → order-form.tsx:669 "No active session key — orders will prompt your wallet" and every order goes through the wallet until the user finds "New session key" (session-panel.tsx:390). The comment at :801-803 claims the one-shot design closed exactly this class. Same wrong predicate covers a second client on the same wallet (localStorage key :170-171 is shared per origin).

**Failure.** A trader who followed the UI's own retry advice ends with a credit delegated to a key no browser holds (or a stored key the chain never authorised) and a panel saying popup-less trading is active; every place/cancel then opens a wallet prompt, and nothing on screen explains why. Recoverable only by discovering 'New session key'.

**Fix.** In oneShotSetup: move :923 inside the try; replace the :935 predicate with `after && after.data[0] === DISC_TRADING_CREDIT && decodeTradingCredit(after.data).sessionAuthority.equals(sessionKp.publicKey)`; and when the :934 verify read itself throws, `storeSession` anyway before rethrowing — an unauthorised stored key is inert because :324-326 gates it, and autoStart's :982 gate guarantees no live session exists on an undelegated credit to overwrite. Frontend only.


### [medium/both] useSession is four independent pollers with four `busy` flags: only the acting instance refreshes, so the order form, open-orders and positions surfaces run on a state up to 5s (40s under backoff) behind the panel that just changed it — and are not locked during withdraw's 60s undelegation window

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:368`  · prior: S9-03

**Evidence.** Mounted at dashboard.tsx:60 (OrderForm → order-form.tsx:61), dashboard.tsx:61 (SessionPanel → session-panel.tsx:47), activity-drawer.tsx:22 default tab 'positions' → positions-table.tsx:120, and open-orders.tsx:23 on the orders tab. Each mount owns its own `state` (:371), `busy` (:400), `seq` (:448) and `refresh` (:450) — the seq guard is per instance and never sees another instance's reads. Eager refreshes are instance-local: :778, :1017, :1183, :1251. Poll gap is 5s nominal and `ms * min(2**fails, 8)` = 40s at max backoff (poll.ts:41-45). (a) After autoStart succeeds in SessionPanel, OrderForm's `session.delegated` stays false for one gap → order-form.tsx:362-363 renders "Start a trading session first" while the panel shows 'trading'. (b) In the acting panel itself, :1017 is `void refresh()` and the finally at :1031-1034 drops busy first, so for one read (measured base getMultipleAccounts 283-650ms + ER 351-443ms) 'Start trading' is re-enabled on delegated=false; a second click hits :982-1001 and prints "...the funds are in a trading credit this build cannot reach; use a fresh wallet" (:999) to a wallet that just set up correctly. (c) withdraw sets busy only in SessionPanel (:1060) while waitForUndelegation polls for up to 60s (:1113, :350-366); OrderForm/OpenOrders/PositionsTable keep delegated=true (L1 owner is still the delegation program until the validator flips it, :282) and sessionActive=true, so handleSubmit (order-form.tsx:190-193) and handleCancel (open-orders.tsx:31-62) still send to the ER. A write that lands before the validator's commit rides into the L1 copy as committed>0, and step 2 (:1130-1138) fails withdraw_trading_credit.rs:50-52 → errors.ts:112 "Rejected on chain: CreditStillActive." (no ERR_ADVICE entry) with the credit already off the rollup. positions-table.tsx:242-252 acknowledges the per-instance lag for `available` only. Restates S9-03's 'hoist into one provider' suggestion with new consequences.

**Failure.** Contradictory panels after every session action (setup says ready, the order button says start a session; a false 'use a fresh wallet' notice on a double-click), and a withdraw whose gating is invisible to the three surfaces that can break it mid-flight.

**Fix.** Back the read side with the existing `useSharedSource` (lib/shared-source.ts) keyed `session:${owner}:${marketIndex}` so one loop and one seq serve all mounts and an action's refresh is a `revalidate(key)`; keep the in-flight flag for withdraw/autoStart in a module-level ref (not a per-instance useRef) and have order-form/open-orders/positions-table read it. Frontend only.


### [medium/both] withdraw's idle pre-check reads a layer the program will never check: the raw ER counters, or on an ER read error the delegation-time L1 snapshot, whereas withdraw_trading_credit checks the post-commit, reconciled L1 copy

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1083`  · prior: S9-01

**Evidence.** Program: undelegate_trading_credit.rs:93-98 runs `reconcile_credit(&ob, credit)` against the ER book before the commit, and withdraw_trading_credit.rs:41-43 (owner) and :50-52 (`is_idle()`) both return CreditStillActive on the L1 copy that results. Client: :1065-1066 reads L1; when delegated, :1075 reads the ER credit and :1082-1083 gates on its raw `activeOrders`/`committed` (unreconciled — the stale-nonzero refusal is S9-01); when that ER read throws, :1077-1079 silently falls back to `creditInfo.data`, the snapshot taken at delegation, which is zeros for any credit delegated fresh, so the gate PASSES, and ten lines later :1106 `erConn.getLatestBlockhash()` needs the same ER. On a flapping ER (first read fails, next call succeeds): gate passes on stale zeros → undelegate lands → L1 copy = reconciled ER copy with committed>0 for genuinely resting orders → step 2 :1132 fails CreditStillActive → the credit is program-owned on L1 while its orders rest on the ER book; cancel_order (open-orders.tsx:40-62) runs on the ER and cannot write a non-delegated credit. Recovery exists but is undocumented: session-panel.tsx:241-265 shows 'Start trading' (oneShotSetup :864-869 re-delegates existingCredit), then cancel, then withdraw — three signatures. The panel's own disable at session-panel.tsx:286 uses the same unreconciled `state.activeOrders`. The reconcile-on-undelegate is in the deployed program (0aba01a 2026-07-27 < live deploy 2026-08-16), so the client gate is redundant on both targets.

**Failure.** Under an ER blip a user's withdraw either refuses on numbers the program would have zeroed (S9-01) or proceeds and strands the credit off-rollup with resting orders it can no longer cancel, ending in "Rejected on chain: CreditStillActive." with no next step.

**Fix.** Delete the :1071-1090 gate and let withdraw_trading_credit be the gate (S9-01's recommendation); if a pre-check is kept, gate on the owner's active slot count from the shared book — `useOpenOrders`' filter at use-open-orders.ts:40-50 is exactly what reconcile_credit computes — and add an ERR_ADVICE entry for CreditStillActive naming the re-delegate → cancel → withdraw path. Frontend only.


### [medium/both] useTriggers has no sequence or owner guard: a poll tick in flight during a confirmed SL/TP change lands last and resurrects the cancelled trigger (or erases the new one), with its Clear button live

`frontend/src/hooks/use-triggers.ts:43`

**Evidence.** use-triggers.ts:26-50: `refresh` awaits `connection.getMultipleAccountsInfo` at :34 and calls `setTriggers` at :43-46 unconditionally — no seq ref, no owner comparison — while the effect at :52-55 polls it every 5s. positions-table.tsx calls `refreshTriggers()` immediately after confirmSignature at :547 (cancel_trigger), :531 (place_trigger), :375 and :483 (flatten/close, for the leftover-trigger banner). Interleaving: tick issued at t=0 (pre-cancel state) is slow; the cancel confirms at t=0.4s and refreshTriggers' read resolves at t=0.8s showing no SL; the tick resolves at t=1.2s and writes the pre-cancel SL back. It stays until the next tick — 5s, or 40s at poll.ts:41-45 backoff. Wallet switch: A's in-flight read writes A's SL/TP under B. use-positions.ts:131-141 describes this exact defect ("a poll issued before a close resolved after it and put the closed row back — with live Close ... buttons") and guards it at :140-141, :186, :195; the fix was not applied here. Measured base RPC latency for the read shape: 283-650ms, so the overlap window is real at the 5s cadence plus manual refreshes.

**Failure.** A trader who just cleared a stop-loss watches it reappear with a working Clear button, or sets one and watches it vanish; the leftover-trigger banner flips on and off after a close.

**Fix.** Copy usePositions' guard: `const owner = publicKey?.toBase58() ?? null; const seqRef = useRef(0);` bump at entry, and `if (seq !== seqRef.current || ownerRef.current !== owner) return;` before setTriggers — ~6 lines.


### [medium/both] oneShotSetup's confirm-timeout branch persists the session key on `owner == DELEGATION_PROGRAM`, so a transaction that FAILED on-chain after another transaction delegated the credit overwrites the real key with one the chain never saw, and reports success

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:935`

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:926-938 — the catch around confirmSignature does not discriminate a timeout from an on-chain failure: confirm.ts:38-46 throws `Transaction failed: {...}` (with `txErr`) into the SAME catch; :934-935 then decides `landed = after?.owner.toBase58() === DELEGATION_PROGRAM` — ownership only, never the authority — and :939 `storeSession(publicKey, marketIndex, sessionKp, expiry)` overwrites the localStorage slot keyed by owner+market (:170-205), :941 returns true, autoStart :1016 sets "You're ready to trade — orders sign instantly, no wallet popups." Interleaving (two tabs of one wallet, or one tab retrying after a 60s confirm timeout whose tx1 lands late): tab A sends tx1(keyA); tab B's autoStart read at :971 still sees a program-owned credit, builds tx2(keyB), passes preflight (wallet-compat.ts:96-99 signs then sendRawTransaction with default skipPreflight:false — the window is until tx1 is visible to the simulating upstream, wider across the proxy's two upstreams); tx1 lands; tx2 executes and fails (credit now owned by DELeG…); B's catch reads owner==DELEG → stores keyB over keyA. The base-layer copy DOES carry the authority the program wrote before staging (delegate_trading_credit.rs:171-181; live: 2Fe6uBNMiTLrTE14Y3yMnv7HpiYK4BzsHJQZEPNtv9UJ base sessionAuthority 3tLMmA…), so the correct check was available.

**Failure.** Both tabs now hold keyB; the chain authorises keyA. readSessionState :324-326 sets sessionActive=false on the next tick, every order/cancel/close falls back to a Phantom popup, the panel shows "New session key", and the user just read a notice saying orders sign with no popups. Recovery is a rotate the user has to guess at; no funds at risk.

**Fix.** In the catch: `if ((err as {txErr?: unknown}).txErr) throw err;` (an on-chain failure is never a landed success), and require `after && after.data[0] === DISC_TRADING_CREDIT && decodeTradingCredit(after.data).sessionAuthority.equals(sessionKp.publicKey)` before storeSession.


### [medium/both] useSession is mounted four times on /trade as four unsynchronised copies of state/status/busy, so an action in the session panel is invisible to the order form, positions table and open-orders for up to a full poll tick, the success notice contradicts the order form, and nothing stops an order being placed mid-withdraw

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:371`  · prior: extends S9-03, S9-06

**Evidence.** Mounts: session-panel.tsx:47, order-form.tsx:61, positions-table.tsx:120, open-orders.tsx:23 (S9-03 counted 3; open-orders is the fourth). Each call owns its own `useState<SessionState>` (:371-387), `seq` (:448), `busy` (:400) and poll loop (:481-484 `startPoll(refresh, 5_000)`); the seq guard therefore only orders reads INSIDE one instance. After autoStart succeeds the panel's instance calls `void refresh()` (:1017) — the other three learn of `delegated`/`sessionActive` only on their own next tick. order-form.tsx:190-193 refuses `if (!session.delegated)` with "Start a trading session first" and :362 disables the button on the same field, while the panel is showing "You're ready to trade — orders sign instantly" (:1016). Same for withdraw: the panel's `busy` (:1060) disables nothing in order-form/open-orders/positions-table, whose `session.delegated`/`session.sessionActive` (order-form.tsx:233, open-orders.tsx:36-37, positions-table.tsx:76-77) stay true while leg 1 is being sent, so a session-signed place_order can be submitted between the :1071-1089 idle pre-check and the ER executing undelegate. Also within one instance: :1030-1034 clears busy/autoRunning before the un-awaited refresh lands, so session-panel.tsx:106 `startDisabled` re-enables "Start trading" for one round trip (measured base 244-370ms + ER 269-890ms) on a wallet that is already delegated. The shared primitive that fixes exactly this already exists: shared-source.ts:71 `useSharedSource(key, fetcher, ms)` + `revalidate`, used by useMarket/useOrderBook.

**Failure.** User clicks Start trading, reads "orders sign instantly", clicks Long within ~0-5.6s (mean ~3.1s: U(0,5s) poll phase + ~0.6s read) and gets "Start a trading session first"; or places an order during Withdraw and, if it lands on the ER before the undelegate, the reconcile at undelegate carries active_orders>0 onto L1 and leg 2 fails CreditStillActive with the order stranded on the delegated book until they re-delegate. Four pollers also mean 4× the getMultipleAccounts + ER reads per tab (the uncapped proxy path).

**Fix.** Move the read into `useSharedSource(owner ? `session:${owner}:${marketIndex}` : null, fetcher, 5_000)` and call `revalidate(key)` after every leg of autoStart/withdraw/rotate; keep the action callbacks in one owner (the panel) and expose `busy` through the same entry so the other three consumers read one truth.


### [medium/both] readSessionState (and withdraw's pre-check) silently substitute the delegation-time base-layer snapshot when the ER read throws and label it "live" — live data shows 20/52 delegated credits diverge, worst case $5,000.00 shown for a credit whose real value is $6.57 and 0 open orders shown for a credit with 12

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:295`  · prior: restates S9-01 for the layer question; new: the fallback

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:291-300 `try { erInfo = await erConnection.getAccountInfo(pda) } catch { /* fall back */ }`, :318-320 `authoritative = erInfo && ... ? erInfo.data : data` — `data` is the base copy (:283); refresh :457-458 then `setRead({owner, status: "live"})`. session-panel.tsx:60-67 `trusted = read === "live" || "stale"` renders those numbers as money; order-form.tsx:150-151,222 uses `session.available` for the margin pre-check. Same pattern at withdraw :1073-1080 — on ER failure `live` stays the base copy and the idle gate at :1083 evaluates base `activeOrders/committed`; the program's actual gate is is_idle() on the L1 copy AFTER undelegate's ER-side reconcile_credit (undelegate_trading_credit.rs:40-48, trading_credit.rs:128-130,153-173) — so the pre-check reads the right layer only when the ER answers. The proxy turns every upstream failure into a 200 + JSON-RPC error (route.ts:211-214) which web3 throws, so the catch is the normal outage path. LIVE 2026-09-05: 52 delegated credits, 20 have base != ER in credit/committed/activeOrders/session fields; 19 have ER.credit < base.credit; 32 have ER activeOrders>0 vs 22 on base. dd4KqsiCA7by2DxW4SWdFWEkU94sNVSJ9Mq3ArwbqLa base credit 5,000.000000 / ER 6.565900; 37PvXBYWaup4MtYTA3bw57yoJAPHA5gzSCCZ3iSgBfVg base 0 orders/0 committed vs ER 12 orders/122.2529 committed; 5aGGXYNYiPwhNj6a9J6ESE899buqRrT2SZHw6ZpCWdft base 10,000 vs ER 4,994.37 + 12 orders. ER read health at measurement time: 0/40 failures, p50 325ms, max 890ms — the precondition is an ER/proxy blip, which the file itself documents as routine (:437-438, route.ts:9-10).

**Failure.** During an ER blip the panel and order form show the delegation-time balance as live (for dd4Kq: "$5,000.00 available" against a real $6.57), the order-form pre-check passes and the ER rejects with InsufficientCredit; if the user rotated since delegating, the base snapshot's old authority makes sessionActive false and orders fall back to wallet popups. If the blip hits withdraw's pre-check on a 37PvX-shaped credit the gate passes with 12 orders resting, undelegate reconciles active_orders=12 onto L1, leg 2 fails CreditStillActive and the user must re-delegate to cancel.

**Fix.** In readSessionState, when `isDelegated` and the ER read threw, `throw err` (refresh already keeps last-known state and marks it "stale", :459-478) instead of decoding the base copy; in withdraw, when delegated and the ER read fails, setError("can't reach the rollup") and return false instead of falling back.


### [medium/both] Every startPoll'd hook except the shared sources swallows its own failure, so poll.ts's geometric backoff never engages for useSession (x4), usePositions and useTriggers — 2 req/s per tab sustained at full rate through the very outages the backoff was written for, and use-positions documents the opposite

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:459`  · prior: extends S9-03

**Evidence.** poll.ts:37-41 increments `fails` only when `fn()` rejects. use-session.ts:459-478 catches everything and returns (comment :466 explains why it must not setError, but nothing rethrows); use-positions.ts:190-198 swallows and the comment at :193 asserts "startPoll's backoff is driven by its own catch and this hook has always swallowed here" — the first clause is false, its catch only fires on a rejection; use-triggers.ts:47-49 `catch { /* keep last state */ }`. shared-source.ts:117-129 rethrows for exactly this reason ("swallowing it here left the backoff dead ... Rethrowing decays it to one attempt every 16s"). Rate through an outage: 4 useSession instances × (1 getMultipleAccounts + 1 ER getAccountInfo)/5s = 1.6 req/s, + positions 0.2/s + triggers 0.2/s = 2.0 req/s per tab, each of which the proxy expands into up to 3 attempts × 2 upstreams (route.ts:106-114,187-199).

**Failure.** A 429/quota outage that the remediate run's backoff was meant to decay to 1/16s per poller instead holds ~2 client req/s per open tab (up to 12 upstream attempts each), which is the load shape that burned the paid RPC quota once already and reproduced the Helius quota outage on 2026-09-04.

**Fix.** Keep `refresh` non-throwing for its action callers but make it return a boolean, and have the poll effect throw on false: `startPoll(async () => { if (!(await refresh())) throw new Error("read failed"); }, 5_000)`; same one-liner in usePositions and useTriggers; fix the comment at use-positions.ts:193.


### [low/both] startPoll arms its first timer at subscription, not after the manual first fetch, so a cold first read slower than the interval is downloaded and then dropped by the seq guard

`frontend/src/lib/shared-source.ts:137`

**Evidence.** shared-source.ts:137 `void run().catch(() => {})` then :138 `e.stop = startPoll(run, intervalMs)`; poll.ts:48 `timer = setTimeout(tick, ms)` fires `ms` after the call regardless of the manual run. If the first fetch exceeds `intervalMs`, the scheduled tick starts a second `run` (seq :108 increments) and the first result is discarded at :114 (`if (my !== e.seq) return`). Same pattern in use-session.ts:482-483, use-positions.ts:231-233, use-triggers.ts:53-54 (their seq/none guards drop or apply the older read). Measured against devnet.magicblock.app: the 626,736-byte order-book read (835,887 B on the wire, no gzip from upstream) took 6,697ms cold and 824-1,189ms warm, against a 2,000ms interval — so on a cold load the first book is fetched, dropped, and the ladder stays 'loading' until the second read completes. Bounded at two in flight per key.

**Failure.** One wasted ~836KB upstream read and a ladder that appears one interval later than it needed to on every cold load; noise rather than a wrong number.

**Fix.** Add `opts.immediate` to startPoll: `immediate ? void tick() : (timer = setTimeout(tick, ms))` (tick already schedules the next after completion), and delete the manual first calls in shared-source.ts:137 and the three hooks.


### [low/both] initialize/fund/delegate and useUserAccount are exported transaction paths with no consumer; delegate() carries none of the key-rescue oneShotSetup has

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:733`

**Evidence.** use-session.ts returns initialize/fund/delegate at :1306-1309; session-panel.tsx:35-47 destructures autoStart, withdraw, requestFaucet, rotate, closeLegacyCredit only, and a grep over frontend/src finds no other caller. delegate() at :733-789 persists the key only after :773 confirm (:776) with no landed check at all, so a future call site inherits the lost-key state the :801-803 comment describes. useUserAccount (use-positions.ts:49-93) has no consumer and its :72-81 setUser after await has no seq/owner guard. ~330 lines of unguarded, untested wallet-signing code kept live. The prior audit's poller table (s9.md:28) already noted useUserAccount is mounted by nothing.

**Failure.** No user impact today; the risk is a future caller picking up delegate()/initialize() and re-introducing the lost-session bug the one-shot path was built to remove.

**Fix.** Delete initialize, fund, delegate and useUserAccount (and their exports).


### [low/both] rotate() persists the new key only after an ER confirm that any RPC error aborts, with no authority check afterwards — a landed rotation then leaves the browser holding the old key

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1250`

**Evidence.** rotate at :1236-1247 sends authorize_session on the ER and awaits confirmSignature (45s); confirm.ts:36 propagates any getSignatureStatuses throw immediately and :52 throws on timeout. storeSession runs only on the success path at :1250; the catch at :1252-1260 sets the error and returns false (success returns void at :1251 — `Promise<void|false>`). If the authorize applied but confirm threw, the chain holds the new authority while localStorage holds the old key → readSessionState :324-326 localMatches=false → session-panel.tsx:370 'none', order-form.tsx:669 'orders will prompt your wallet'. The error rendered is errors.ts:138-141 "Timed out waiting for confirmation. The transaction may still land — check before retrying." — there is nothing the user can check. oneShotSetup has a rescue for this class (:926-938); rotate does not. Measured ER getLatestBlockhash 272ms, so the 45s timeout is rare; the RPC-throw path is the realistic one.

**Failure.** After a rotation during an ER/RPC blip the user is back to wallet prompts on every order and must rotate again on a hint they are never given.

**Fix.** Extract the authority-equality check from the fix to the oneShotSetup finding into one helper and call it in rotate's catch: if `credit.sessionAuthority.equals(sessionKp.publicKey)`, storeSession and return true. Make rotate return boolean consistently.


### [low/both] waitForUndelegation's 'press Withdraw again' branch and a confirm-time RPC throw both re-send ScheduleCommitAndUndelegate for an account already scheduled; nothing guards a second withdraw flow after an SPA remount

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1113`  · prior: S9-07

**Evidence.** S9-07 already covers RPC errors consuming the :356-365 retry budget. New: on `waitForUndelegation` returning false the user is told to press Withdraw again (:1114-1116); the retry re-reads L1 at :1065-1066, still sees the delegation program (the validator has not flipped it), and :1094-1110 sends a second undelegate on the ER for the same credit. A getSignatureStatuses throw at :1110 (confirm.ts:36, no retry) does the same on the next click even though the first undelegate was accepted. Neither guards funds — the program owns them — but each costs a wallet signature and an ER error string. Separately, `busy` (:400) and `autoStartInFlight` (:407) are per-instance `useState`/`useRef`: a client-side navigation away from /trade and back mounts a fresh SessionPanel with busy=false while the old withdraw is still polling and will still open wallet prompts for :1132 and :1162; clicking Withdraw again starts a parallel flow whose step 2 fails InsufficientCredit (withdraw_trading_credit.rs:53-55) after a second prompt.

**Failure.** Extra wallet prompts and an uninterpretable ER error during a slow undelegation; two withdraw flows racing after a navigate-and-return.

**Fix.** Before :1094, skip the undelegate leg when a module-level `withdrawInFlight` ref (shared across instances) is set or when the L1 account already shows the program as owner; hold that ref for the life of the flow. Have waitForUndelegation return 'flipped' | 'pending' | 'rpc' so the message can say which (S9-07).


### [low/repo] deploy.json's deployedAt is 78 days behind the live ProgramData slot, which dates the deployed program between the undelegate fixes and the remediate wave

`deploy.json:17`  · prior: S10-02

**Evidence.** deploy.json:17 `"deployedAt": "2026-05-30T09:00:00.000Z"`. Live: getAccountInfo(7qujfsb4ZPbQHYVZdqiXq1r8tVAMyyukX94obPqXbVwz) → ProgramData 6BBrTYsX6ioS9Fu9uAPiLPRVDsBxCvy6wYWtzi85WihN; its slot field = 484488785; getBlockTime(484488785) = 1786894734 = 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z; programdata length 205,909 B. git log on undelegate/withdraw: f9a213d 2026-07-26 (undelegate CPI fix), 0aba01a 2026-07-27 (reconcile on undelegate), 385414d 2026-08-22 (remediate wave 1a). So the deployed program includes working undelegation and reconcile but not R1-R7 — every 'deployed' target in this report assumes that ordering. s10.md:47 printed the same slot inside S10-01 and s10.md:148 (S10-02) notes deployedAt is hand-maintained without a build hash; neither converted the slot to a date or flagged the mismatch.

**Failure.** Anyone triaging 'is fix X live?' from deploy.json gets the wrong answer by three months; the remediate run's own 'never redeployed' framing is only half right.

**Fix.** Write the ProgramData block time (and ideally a build hash) into deploy.json at deploy time; until then a one-liner over getAccountInfo(ProgramData).slot → getBlockTime replaces the hand-typed date.


### [low/both] withdraw() keeps running after the trade page unmounts: waitForUndelegation polls for up to 60s, then legs 2-3 raise Phantom prompts on whatever page the user is on, and a remounted panel can start a second withdraw that collides with the orphan

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1113`  · prior: extends S9-07

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:1054-1194 has no mounted ref, AbortSignal or module-level in-flight guard (the only guard in the file is autoStartInFlight :407, a per-instance ref); :1113 `await waitForUndelegation(connection, creditPda)` (30 tries × 2s, :350-366), then :1132 and :1162 call `sendTransaction` (Phantom signTransaction, wallet-compat.ts:96) unconditionally. terminal-nav.tsx:16 and :39 are next/link client navigations to `/` and `/docs`, so the page unmounts without a reload.
