A fresh mount of the panel has `busy=false` (:400) and `delegated` read from base (:1066) still true until the flip lands, so a second click sends a second undelegate/withdraw_trading_credit; the second leg-2 fails `credit.credit < amount` → InsufficientCredit (withdraw_trading_credit.rs:53-54) which ERR_ADVICE renders as "Not enough trading credit — add credit in the Session panel".

**Failure.** User navigates to /docs during "Waiting for the rollup to settle…", gets an unexplained wallet prompt a minute later on the docs page (approving it completes the withdrawal, rejecting it strands the credit on L1 — resumable, but nothing says so); or returns and presses Withdraw again and is told they have insufficient credit while the orphaned flow is withdrawing it.

**Fix.** Add a module-level `withdrawInFlight: Set<string>` keyed by owner+market checked at :1055 (covers remounts), and a `mountedRef` set false in a useEffect cleanup that `return false` after :1113 when unmounted — the flow is already resumable so abandoning it is safe.


### [low/both] waitForUndelegation counts tries, not time: with the proxy's 20s upstream timeout × 3 attempts × 2 upstreams a single try can take ~125s, so the "Waiting for the rollup to settle…" spinner can hold every button disabled for ~62 minutes

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:356`  · prior: extends S9-07

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:350-366 loops `for (let i = 0; i < tries; i++)` with `tries = 30` and no wall-clock deadline; each try is `await conn.getAccountInfo(...)` then 2s sleep. route.ts:106-114 `AbortSignal.timeout(20_000)` per attempt, 3 attempts with 250/500/750ms backoff (:166-169) ≈ 61.5s per upstream, then the fallback upstream (:196-199) ≈ 123s, returned as a 200 JSON-RPC error (:211-214) that web3 throws into the swallowing catch at :360-362. 30 × (123s + 2s) ≈ 62.5 min. During that time `busy` is true (:1060) and every panel button is disabled (session-panel.tsx:167,246,286,387). Measured healthy base read 244-681ms, so the designed budget is ~68-80s; the 62-minute case needs both upstreams dead, which is the 2026-09-04 outage shape plus a public-endpoint stall.

**Failure.** A withdraw started into a base-layer RPC outage freezes the whole session panel for up to an hour with no way to cancel, then reports "The rollup hasn't handed your funds back yet" — blaming MagicBlock for the RPC (S9-07's misattribution, now with a duration).

**Fix.** Replace the try counter with `const deadline = Date.now() + 90_000; while (Date.now() < deadline)` and pass an AbortSignal.timeout to the read (web3 Connection accepts none, so wrap with Promise.race against a 10s timer).


### [low/both] useTriggers has no owner or sequence guard, so the previous wallet's SL/TP render as a "Leftover trigger" with a live Clear button for the next wallet, and a cancelled trigger can reappear for up to 5s

`frontend/src/hooks/use-triggers.ts:26`

**Evidence.** use-triggers.ts:26-50 — `refresh` awaits getMultipleAccountsInfo and calls `setTriggers` with no `ownerRef`/`seqRef` check (contrast usePositions :129-137,186,195); the only clear is `if (!publicKey)` (:27-30), so a switch A→B never clears A's triggers synchronously and a failed read for B (:47-49 "keep last state") keeps A's indefinitely. positions-table.tsx:921-945 renders `(triggers.stopLoss || triggers.takeProfit)` with `positions.length === 0 && !positionsUnknown` as "Leftover trigger — with no position open it will close whichever position you open next" plus Clear SL/TP buttons wired to `handleCancelTrigger` (:537-549) for B's PDA. Cancel race: :546 `refreshTriggers()` after confirm overlaps the 5s poll; a poll read issued before the cancel landed (base reads measured 244-681ms) can resolve after the manual read and re-set the cancelled trigger; startPoll only serialises its own loop (poll.ts:1-23).

**Failure.** Wallet B sees wallet A's stop-loss price flagged as a leftover that "will close whichever position you open next", and clicking Clear sends a cancel_trigger for a PDA that does not exist (AccountNotInitialized / "custom" error). After a cancel, ~5-10% of the time the row comes back for one tick with a Clear button that now fails the same way.

**Fix.** Copy the ownerRef + seqRef pattern from use-positions.ts:129-137 into refresh (drop the result unless `seq === seqRef.current && ownerRef.current === owner`) and reset triggers in the effect when owner changes.


### [low/both] A transport failure AFTER the wallet has signed and the proxy has forwarded sendTransaction is reported as "nothing was sent", and in oneShotSetup the session key it would have persisted is lost because the send sits outside the landed-check

`frontend/src/lib/slipstream/errors.ts:118`

**Evidence.** errors.ts:118-122 returns "Can't reach Solana right now — nothing was sent" for any network-shaped message; wallet-compat.ts:96-99 signs then `connection.sendRawTransaction`; route.ts:106-114 forwards the POST with a 20s timeout and on AbortError retries (:166-169) and fails over (:196-199) — an upstream that accepted the transaction and then stalled still ends as a 200 JSON-RPC error (:211-214) → web3 throws → use-session.ts:923 `sendTransaction` is above the landed-check try at :926, so autoStart's catch (:1025-1029) prints the "nothing was sent" line while `sessionKp` (:882) is dropped. Next click: :982 "already delegated" branch, notice "Your session is already open — nothing to set up" with `sessionActive` false. Precondition measured as rare (sendTransaction upstream latency not measured; healthy reads 244-890ms), but the proxy timeout path is the one that produced yesterday's outage shape.

**Failure.** User is told nothing was sent, the credit is delegated to a key that exists nowhere (the exact case the :801-803 comment says the one-shot flow eliminated), and every order pops the wallet until they find "New session key".

**Fix.** In wallet-compat, compute the signature from the signed transaction before sendRawTransaction and attach it to a thrown transport error (`err.signature`); in oneShotSetup move the send inside the try so the same `after`-authority check runs on a transport failure; in humanizeError say "may or may not have been sent" when the error carries a signature.


### [low/repo] ~330 lines of dead async paths still exported: initialize/fund/delegate in useSession (delegate persists a session key with no landed check), useUserAccount and usePriceHistory have zero callers

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:488`

**Evidence.** `grep -rn` over frontend/src: no call site destructures `initialize`, `fund` or `delegate` from useSession (only session-panel.tsx:35-47 destructures, without them); use-session.ts:488-619, :669-720, :733-789 remain and are returned at :1306-1309. `delegate` :770-776 persists the key on a bare confirm with none of oneShotSetup's landed reasoning; `initialize` is the multi-transaction flow the :791-803 comment says was removed. use-positions.ts:49-93 `useUserAccount` and use-price-history.ts:23-58 `usePriceHistory` (which does a synchronous localStorage write inside a setState updater at :44-53) have no importers. The :1037-1043 dependency comment already documents a bug caused by leaving the trio in the closure.

**Failure.** No user impact today; a future caller reaching for the documented `delegate()`/`initialize()` gets the pre-remediate stranding behaviour, and the unused code keeps the file at 1,317 lines for reviewers.

**Fix.** Delete the three callbacks and their return entries, useUserAccount, and use-price-history.ts.


### [low/both] No CSP or security headers on the origin that holds four in-memory copies of the session secret — unchanged on this branch

`frontend/next.config.ts:3`  · prior: restates S9-X01

**Evidence.** next.config.ts:1-7 is still the empty config; no frontend/src/middleware.ts; vercel.json has no headers block. use-session.ts:190-205 still writes the 64-byte secret to localStorage and :253 reads it on every 5s tick in each of the four mounted instances.

**Failure.** Any script injection on the origin reads a usable trading key; nothing new since S9-X01, confirmed still open.

**Fix.** As S9-X01: a headers() block with script-src CSP, frame-ancestors 'none', nosniff, Referrer-Policy.



## fe-rendering-perf  (17 findings)

**Measured:** All measured 2026-09-05 against production https://slipstream.ansht.tech/trade (built from factory/remediate) with headless Playwright, viewport 1440x900, no wallet; plus direct RPC/WS reads. ER order book 83zMFL6…: 626,736 bytes, 835,887 base64 wire (direct), header maxOrderSlots 2048 / maxPriceLevelsPerSide 512 / maxFillEvents 4096 / fillEventCount 4096 (ring full) / activeOrderCount 30 / bidLevels 18 / askLevels 12 / nextOrderId 1,877,219 / nextFillSequence 53,734; ring sequences 49,638..53,733. Base copy (owner DELeG…): fillEventCount 9, nextFillSequence 10 (stale delegation snapshot). Market ECUp8…: 2,064 bytes, lastSettledSequence 44,189, lastMarkPrice 101.877385, markPriceMinute == now (stamp fresh). Direct RTT from Ghaziabad: ER getAccountInfo(book) 2,107 ms; base getAccountInfo(book) 420 ms; base market 79 ms. Via the /api/rpc proxy from the browser: ER book 410-497 ms (avg 432, max 808), transfer 51,557 B / encoded 51,257 B gzip / decoded 835,924 B per request, identical across 9 consecutive requests; issue times 113, 2114, 4628, 7054, 9482, 11909, 14332, 16762, 19197 ms (period 2.43 s); /api/rpc/base market every ~5.2 s (3,053 B decoded); /api/status every 10 s; /api/pyth/history ×2 at load (resolution 60: 8,936 B, resolution 5: 7,535 B, ~1 s each). 22 /api/rpc requests in 35 s = 12.56 MB decoded, 784 KB wire. Oracle WS wss://devnet.magicblock.app accountSubscribe ENYweb…: 398 notifications / 20 s = 19.9/s, 342 price changes (~17/s), 21 distinct publishTime (1/s), price 101.872. Browser: exactly 1 WebSocket, 20.0 msg/s; canvas clearRect 20.4/s, getComputedStyle 20.4/s, clientWidth reads 20.4/s, canvas.width reassigned 1× in 95 s, draw body avg ~0.2 ms max 0.6 ms (~4-8 ms/s), 0 long tasks in 60 s, 12 setInterval registrations at load (constant), addEventListener +4 net over 60 s, JS heap 37.8 → 48.4 → 44.7 → 44.2 MB at 15 s steps (stable), page load to networkidle 6.3 s. V8 benchmark on the real book bytes (node 24, scratchpad/live/bench_fe.mjs): decodeOrderBook-equivalent 3.7 ms/tick (10,240 PublicKey constructions), recentFills ×2 0.2 ms, base64 decode 0.3 ms, PublicKey.toBase58 2.0 µs each; reconstruct() with lastSettledSequence below the ring (the live state) 15.2-18.7 ms/call over 4096 fills (→ ~260-370 ms/s at 17-20 calls/s), 0.78-0.88 ms with 200 pending, 0.03 ms with none; same reconstruct using PublicKey.equals 0.26 ms; copy+filter+sort alone 0.20 ms. @phantom/react-sdk 2.0.2 verified: useAccounts returns React state (stable), useSolana returns the provider's cached chain object. web3.js 1.98.4 verified: Connection ctor RpcWebSocketClient autoconnect:false at lib/index.cjs.js:6104-6106; toBase58 uncached at :208-210.

**Not covered:** Could not measure the signed-in path in a real browser (no wallet in headless Playwright): the reconstruct()/PositionsTable cost in finding 1 is V8-benchmarked on the live bytes, not profiled in Chrome — same engine, but React commit overhead on top is unmeasured. useSession's action handlers (initialize/fund/delegate/withdraw/rotate, use-session.ts:488-1317) and order-form.tsx submit path were read only for their effect on polling, not audited for rendering. The 30 Element.animate() calls observed in the first 5 s (with a byte-identical book) could not be attributed — the counter was global and the Phantom SDK UI or motion/react may own them; the useFlash first-paint guard reads correct in code. Did not audit landing/ components beyond price-ticker.tsx, the docs renderer, or the API routes (other dimensions). The RPC proxy's getMultipleAccounts cap was excluded by brief.


### [high/both] useErPosition replays and base58-encodes the entire 4,096-fill ring on every oracle frame (~20ms x 19.5/s = ~40% of the main thread) to produce null

`frontend/src/hooks/use-er-position.ts:71`

**Evidence.** reconstruct() at :71-73 copies, filters and sorts `fills`, then :82-83 call f.maker.toBase58() and f.taker.toBase58() for EVERY fill. It runs from the useMemo at :203-208 whose deps include `markPrice`; positions-table.tsx:77-78 derives that as BigInt(Math.round(reference * PRICE_SCALE)) from useMarkPrice -> useLivePrice, so it changes on every WebSocket frame. LIVE: ER book header fillEventCount=4096, ring seqs 49,638..53,733; Market.lastSettledSequence=44,189 (base, u32 at offset 2058), so the `f.sequence > lastSettledSequence` filter at :72 keeps all 4,096 entries. Feed measured 19.5 accountNotification/s (390 in 20.0s, p50 gap 49ms). Benchmarked with the pinned @solana/web3.js 1.98.4 on node v24 (same V8) against the live account bytes: reconstruct walk = 20.3ms median (toBase58 x8192 alone = 19.6ms; PublicKey.equals x8192 = 0.15ms). 20.3ms x 19.5/s = ~396ms CPU per second whenever a wallet is connected, and for any wallet with no ring fills the result is null. The comment at :199-202 ('Reconstructing from a bounded ring already in memory is pure CPU') matches the code but is wrong about the cost. Same pattern: trade-history.tsx:75-102 (fe.maker.toBase58()/fe.taker.toBase58() at :81-82 over all 4,096 fills on every book tick and every /api/trades poll; measured 21.8ms) and use-open-orders.ts:43 (slot.owner.toBase58() per active slot; 30 active, 0.14ms, harmless but same shape). Mobile V8 is typically 3-5x slower.

**Failure.** Every connected-wallet /trade tab spends ~40% of a desktop main thread (likely over budget on mid-range phones) inside positions-table; order-form re-renders at the same 19.5/s so typing a price competes with it; the drawer drops frames while scrolling. Grows with nothing but a connected wallet, and stays at the ceiling forever because the settlement cursor never advances past the ring.

**Fix.** Compare keys as bytes, not strings: pass the owner PublicKey into reconstruct() and use f.maker.equals(owner) / f.taker.equals(owner) (measured 130x cheaper); and split the memo so the owner-filtered, sorted fills are memoized on [fillEvents, owner, settledSeq] and only the pnl() call re-runs on markPrice. Apply the same equals() change at trade-history.tsx:81-82 and use-open-orders.ts:43. No program change.


### [high/repo] useErPosition replays the full 4096-entry fill ring with 8,192 uncached toBase58() encodes on every oracle price change (~17/s) — ~260-320 ms of main thread per second for every signed-in user

`frontend/src/hooks/use-er-position.ts:82`

**Evidence.** use-er-position.ts:203-209 memoises reconstruct() on [owner, fillEvents, markPrice, settledSeq]; markPrice is positions-table.tsx:77-78 `referenceAtoms = BigInt(Math.round(reference * 1e6))`, which changes with the oracle price (measured wss://devnet.magicblock.app: 398 accountNotifications / 20 s = 19.9/s, 342 of them distinct prices = ~17 changes/s). reconstruct() (use-er-position.ts:71-84) first copies+filters+sorts the WHOLE ring and only then tests ownership with `f.maker.toBase58() === ownerB58` and `f.taker.toBase58() === ownerB58` — two bs58 encodes per fill; PublicKey.toBase58 in the pinned web3.js 1.98.4 is `bs58.encode(this.toBytes())` with no cache (lib/index.cjs.js:208-210). Live ER book (83zMFL6…, read 2026-09-05): maxFillEvents 4096, fillEventCount 4096, ring sequences 49,638..53,733; Market.lastSettledSequence 44,189 (settlement cursor below the ring's oldest entry, S4-C8 territory) so the `sequence > lastSettledSequence` filter at :72 keeps all 4096. Node/V8 benchmark on the real bytes (scratchpad/live/bench_fe.mjs): reconstruct = 15.2-18.7 ms/call with 4096 pending, toBase58 = 2.0 µs each; same function with `PublicKey.equals` = 0.26 ms; copy+filter+sort alone = 0.20 ms. PositionsTable is the DEFAULT drawer tab (activity-drawer.tsx:22-23) and mounts useMarkPrice→useLivePrice, so it re-renders at the feed rate whenever a wallet is connected — the 15 ms cost is paid before the owner check, i.e. by every signed-in user, not only those with fills. The comment at :200-202 ('Reconstructing from a bounded ring already in memory is pure CPU') is true and is exactly the problem. (positions-table.tsx is 1,135 lines because ~55% is commentary and the ER row and L1 row bodies are duplicated inline with IIFEs at :646-672 and :749-777; what re-renders per tick is the whole component, since useMarkPrice returns a fresh `live` object per WS frame.)

**Failure.** Signed-in traders lose ~30% of one core to a memo that produces the same answer 17 times a second (0 → 100%+ on a 3-4× slower laptop): dropped frames across the whole terminal, laggy order-form typing, and the same class of pathology that previously ended in ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES — with no visible cause because nothing is slow in the network panel.

**Fix.** Two-line change: compare keys with `f.maker.equals(owner) || f.taker.equals(owner)` (pass the PublicKey, drop ownerB58) — measured 15.2 ms → 0.26 ms. Better still, split the memo: select+sort the owner's fills in a useMemo keyed on [fillEvents, owner, settledSeq] (book cadence, 2.4 s) and price them in a second memo keyed on markPrice. No deploy.

**Verifier verdicts (partial):**
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→repo): CONFIRMED, reproduced end to end from an unprivileged position (any connected wallet on /trade). Path: dashboard.tsx:50 -> ActivityDrawer, tab defaults to "positions" (activity-drawer.tsx:22) -> PositionsTable (activity-drawer.tsx:60) -> useMarkPrice (positions-table.tsx:70-76) -> useLivePrice, whose store writes a fresh `feed.live` object per WS frame (use-live-price.ts:151-153) so useSyncExternalStore does not bail and the component re-renders at feed rate; referenceAtoms = BigInt(Math.round(reference*1e6)) (positions-table.tsx:77-78) is passed as markPrice to useErPosition (:92); the memo at use-er-position.ts:203-209 depends on markPrice, and Object.is on a BigInt compares by value, so reconstruct() re-runs on every DISTINCT price. reconstruct() (use-er-position.ts:71-84) copies/filters/sorts the whole ring and then calls f.maker.toBase58() and f.taker.toBase58() unconditionally per fill (:82-83, no short-circuit) = 2 encodes per fill. web3.js 1.98.4 PublicKey.toBase58 is bs58.encode(this.toBytes()) with no cache (lib/index.cjs.js:208-210); equals() is a BN compare (:201-203). Live reads 2026-09-05: ER order book 83zMFL6 maxFills 4096, count 4096, 4096 nonzero, seq 49638..53733; base Market ECUp8 lastSettledSequence 44189 (offset 2058, accounts.ts:173) < 49638, so the `sequence > lastSettledSequence` filter at :72 keeps all 4096 -> 8,192 encodes per call. Re-ran scratchpad/live/bench_fe.mjs on real bytes: reconstruct = 14.93 ms/call with 4096 pending, toBase58 = 1.75 us each, PublicKey.equals variant = 0.20 ms, copy+filter+sort alone = 0.14 ms. Measured wss://devnet.magicblock.app for 19.7 s: 394 frames (20.0/s), 291 distinct referenceAtoms changes (14.8/s) -> ~220 ms/s of main thread now (claim's 260-320 ms/s used a 17/s sample; same order). Single call site of useErPosition, so the proposed equals()-based fix at the source is the root-cause fix and touches no protected test. Severity corrected high -> medium: by the rubric high requires funds locked, wrong money numbers, or an exploit with one precondition; this is a pure CPU/UX defect (~22-30% of one core on a desktop-class core, no money or authz impact, terminal still functions). The "100%+ on a 3-4x slower laptop" figure is extrapolated, not measured. Target stays repo: it is frontend code on this branch, not program bytecode.
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→repo): No guard exists anywhere in the chain; every link verified on factory/remediate @ 87f9eb1. (1) Mount path: activity-drawer.tsx:23 defaults tab to "positions" and :67 renders PositionsTable, which calls useMarkPrice (positions-table.tsx:70-76) → useLivePrice (use-mark-price.ts:78) → useSyncExternalStore over a store whose `feed.live` is a NEW object per accountNotification (use-live-price.ts:159-162), so the whole component re-renders per WS frame; nothing throttles or debounces `reference` (use-mark-price.ts:159 `spot ?? ...`, spot = live.price). (2) referenceAtoms = BigInt(Math.round(reference*1e6)) (positions-table.tsx:77-78) is passed straight into useErPosition (:92) and sits in the memo deps at use-er-position.ts:208; BigInt deps compare by value, so the only "guard" is identical 6-dp prices — measured live 2026-09-05 (wss://devnet.magicblock.app, 15 s): 293 frames = 19.9/s, 242 distinct 6-dp atoms = 16.4 recomputes/s. (3) reconstruct() (use-er-position.ts:71-84) copies/filters/sorts the whole ring and only then does two toBase58() per fill; fillEvents is deliberately the full live window, not a tail (use-orderbook.ts:52-58, :153 `recentFills(book, book.header.fillEventCount)`); FillEvent.maker/taker are PublicKey objects (accounts.ts:532-533, :545-546 via `new PublicKey(...)`) and web3.js 1.98.4 toBase58 is an uncached `bs58.encode(this.toBytes())` (lib/index.cjs.js:208-210) while equals() is a BN compare (:201-203). (4) Live state: ER order book 83zMFL6… fillEventCount 4096, ring seq 49,638..53,733; Market ECUp8p… lastSettledSequence read at offset 2058 (accounts.ts:173) = 44,189 on BOTH api.devnet.solana.com and devnet.magicblock.app, so the `sequence > lastSettledSequence` filter at :72 keeps all 4096. (5) Re-ran the benchmark against the live bytes: 15.5 ms/call with 4096 pending vs 0.20 ms with PublicKey.equals and 0.13 ms for copy+filter+sort alone; toBase58 = 1.97 µs each. 15.5 ms × 16.4/s ≈ 255 ms/s of main thread for any connected wallet (reconstruct is gated only by `owner && settledSeq !== null`, :205). Severity corrected to medium: it is a real, reproducible defect but bounded (ring cap 4096, feed cap 20/s → ≤ ~310 ms/s), produces no wrong money numbers, locks no funds, and is not an outage; the "100%+ on a slower laptop" extrapolation is unmeasured. Target repo is correct (frontend code, no on-chain component). Proposed fix (equals() instead of toBase58, or split the memo so ownership selection keys on the 2 s book tick) touches no protected test.
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→repo): NOT a restatement. None of the 12 remediated P0s (R1-R7) touch the frontend; none of the ESTABLISHED TODAY items (RPC failover, TWAP crank, /api/status, Pyth cadence, getMultipleAccounts, landing page) cover this; the prior audit mentions use-er-position only as an RPC poller (s9.md:22 S9-C5 table, s9.md:191 S9-03 "9.33 RPC round trips/s") and for the L1 fallback (s9.md:155 S9-02), and S13-01..10/X01 are correctness/display findings. No prior ID identifies the per-oracle-tick reconstruct() CPU cost. PRIOR: none is accurate.

Substance verified on factory/remediate @ 87f9eb1: use-er-position.ts:71-73 copies+filters+sorts the whole ring before the per-fill `f.maker.toBase58() === ownerB58` / `f.taker.toBase58()` at :82-83; memo deps at :208 include markPrice; positions-table.tsx:77-78 derives referenceAtoms from useMarkPrice().reference, which is fed by useLivePrice's per-frame decodeFeed() object (use-live-price.ts:156-159, new object each frame so useSyncExternalStore does not bail). activity-drawer.tsx:23-24 defaults to tab "positions", open=true, so PositionsTable is mounted on every /trade load. use-orderbook.ts:146 passes recentFills(book, fillEventCount) = all 4096. web3.js 1.98.4 lib/index.cjs.js:208-210 toBase58 = bs58.encode(this.toBytes()) with no cache; equals at :201-203 is a BN compare. Live reads 2026-09-05: Market ECUp8... lastSettledSequence 44189 (slot 493389166), ER ring sequences 49638..53733, fillEventCount 4096, so the :72 filter keeps all 4096. Re-ran scratchpad/live/bench_fe.mjs: reconstruct 16.75 ms/call with 4096 pending vs 0.29 ms with PublicKey.equals, 0.15 ms copy+filter+sort only; toBase58 1.75 us each. Feed measured over 14.6 s: 20.0 frames/s, 17.9 distinct raw prices/s.

One quantification correction: the memo key is the 6-dp rounded BigInt (positions-table.tsx:78), and Object.is on equal BigInts bails, so the recompute rate is the distinct-after-rounding rate, measured 12.8/s (188/14.6 s), not ~17/s. That gives ~215 ms/s of main thread, not 260-320 ms/s; still ~20% of a core for a memo that returns null for every wallet without pending fills.

Severity corrected high -> medium: no funds impact, no wrong money numbers, no authz issue; it is a real, reproducible performance defect with bounded impact (dropped frames / input lag on slower machines), which is the medium definition. Target "repo" is correct: this is frontend code on the production branch; the repo-vs-deployed distinction applies to the on-chain program only. Proposed fix (PublicKey.equals, or split the owner-selection memo from the pricing memo) is consistent with the hook's own stated intent at :164-166 and touches no protected test.


### [medium/both] The oracle WebSocket republishes the same reading ~19 times per second; only ~1 distinct update/s arrives, so ~95% of feed-driven re-renders are no-ops

`frontend/src/hooks/use-live-price.ts:156`

**Evidence.** ws.onmessage at :149-164 decodes every accountNotification and unconditionally sets feed.live = next (:158) and publish() (:159); decodeFeed returns a fresh object each call so useSyncExternalStore's Object.is bail-out (:261) never fires. LIVE measurement, 20.0s accountSubscribe on ENYweb...jPu at devnet.magicblock.app: 390 notifications (19.5/s, p50 gap 49ms, 9,049 bytes/s) but only 21 distinct publishTime values, i.e. ~1.05 real price updates per second. The docstring at :189-190 ('fixed 50ms cadence, ~20 updates/sec') is right about frames and wrong about updates: the pusher re-sends the identical reading. Consumers of `live`/`reference` that therefore render 19.5/s instead of ~1/s: positions-table.tsx:76 (1,135 lines, runs finding 1), order-form.tsx:70, market-bar.tsx:34, price-chart.tsx:21,30 (full canvas redraw per render, see finding 5), status-panel.tsx:54-55.

**Failure.** Five components and one canvas redraw run ~19x more often than the data changes; this is the multiplier on every other hot path on the page. On its own it is bounded jank; combined with finding 1 it is the difference between 20ms/s and 400ms/s of CPU.

**Fix.** One guard in onmessage before :158: if (feed.live && next.price === feed.live.price && next.publishTime === feed.live.publishTime) return; (feed.live is already cleared on reconnect at :117 so the first frame after a reconnect still publishes). useMarkPrice's 5s nowSec tick keeps the age gate alive independently.


### [medium/both] useSharedSource re-renders every subscriber and re-decodes 626,736 bytes on ticks where the account did not change (measured 4/4 consecutive ER reads byte-identical)

`frontend/src/lib/shared-source.ts:115`

**Evidence.** run() at :107-132 always assigns e.data = next and calls every subscriber's rerender() (:131), and useOrderBook's fetcher (use-orderbook.ts:92-151) always returns a fresh object with a fresh Date.now() at :149, so no consumer can bail. LIVE: four consecutive ER getAccountInfo reads of 83zMFL6...nMqe spaced 2s apart (spanning ~55s of wall time because of latency) all hashed sha256 7fcd0dc916bc, same nextFillSequence 53734, head 485, activeOrderCount 30 -- the book did not change once. Each such tick still moves 835,887 wire bytes, runs decodeOrderBook (6.9ms, 2,048 OrderSlot + 4,096 FillEvent + 10,240 PublicKey constructions), recentFills twice (:123, :146), and re-renders the 9 useOrderBook consumers (order-book-display + 30 Row/useLevelStamps/useFlash JSON.stringify per row, status-panel, fill-toasts, trade-history's 21.8ms pending walk, order-form, positions-table, use-open-orders, use-er-position). ER read latency for this account measured 771 / 2,633 / 5,784 / 9,326 / 12,018 / 33,913 ms, so the nominal 2s cadence is really 3-36s and the shared-source.ts:163-165 note about 2.3-12.3s render gaps is consistent with it.

**Failure.** Every book tick costs a decode plus a full re-render fan-out across half the page for data that, at the current trading rate, is identical almost every time; the cost scales with mounted consumers, not with new information.

**Fix.** In use-orderbook.ts fetcher keep the last info.data and the last result in module scope and return the SAME result object when Buffer.equals(); in shared-source.ts run() add `if (next === e.data) { e.error = null; return; }` before :115 so identical results do not notify. Keep updatedAt honest by bumping only that field on the cached object if the stale banner needs it.


### [medium/both] useSession is still a per-instance 5s poller; its three simultaneous mounts issue half of the tab's remaining RPC traffic and hold three divergent copies of the credit state

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:481`  · prior: S9-03

**Evidence.** Restates the residual of S9-03: every other polled hook was moved to useSharedSource, but useSession's poll effect at :481-484 (refresh at :450-479) is still per-instance. Call sites: session-panel.tsx:47, order-form.tsx:61, positions-table.tsx:120 (default drawer tab) and open-orders.tsx:23 (its tab) -- 3 mounted at once, 4 across tabs. Each refresh does readSessionState: getAssociatedTokenAddress + one base getMultipleAccountsInfo of 4 accounts (:232) + one ER getAccountInfo when delegated (:294), i.e. 2 RPC per mount per 5s = 1.2 req/s versus 0.4 needed. Steady-state on this branch is ~2.4 req/s per idle tab (book <=0.5, market 0.2, session 1.2, positions 0.2, triggers 0.2, /api/status 0.1, candles 0.03) so useSession is ~50% of it, down from the 9.33/s S9-03 measured. Each mount also setState()s a fresh SessionState object at :457 every tick, re-rendering its consumer whether or not anything changed. positions-table.tsx:242-249 already documents the consequence: 'this copy of available can be up to 5s behind' another instance's.

**Failure.** Three redundant reads of the same four accounts every 5s against the base RPC and the ER (whose getAccountInfo latency measured up to 34s), and three UI surfaces that can disagree about the same credit number for a full poll period.

**Fix.** Route the READ through useSharedSource keyed `session:${owner}:${marketIndex}` (the fetcher is readSessionState, already pure over its inputs) and have each action's post-write refresh() call revalidate(key); leave the per-instance action callbacks and busy/step/error state where they are.


### [medium/both] CandleCanvas redraws the whole chart on every render (every feed frame and every unthrottled mousemove) and re-resolves CSS tokens with getComputedStyle each time; no ResizeObserver

`frontend/src/components/trading/price-chart.tsx:463`

**Evidence.** The draw effect's deps at :463 include `visible`, built at :254 as candles.slice(start, end) -- a fresh array on every render -- so the effect can never bail out. CandleCanvas renders on every feed frame (PriceChart's `candles` memo at :35-52 depends on `live`, 19.5/s per finding 2) and on every pointer move, because onMove at :497-512 calls setHover unconditionally at :510 with a fresh object (mouse events arrive at 60-125+/s). Each run calls getComputedStyle(canvas) plus four getPropertyValue reads (:302-320), a forced style resolution, before painting ~80 candles. Comment claims checked: the re-alloc guard at :280-286 is correct (dims compared before canvas.width/height are touched, so the 9.6MB backing store is no longer churned); the 'only lineWidth survives' note at :290-297 is correct (font/textBaseline are re-set at :344-345 each frame, setLineDash is reset at :443 and :461, every fillStyle/strokeStyle/textAlign is assigned before use). Not handled: no ResizeObserver -- resize is only noticed by the next effect run, so while the oracle stream is down (`live` null -> `candles` memo stable) the canvas keeps its old size until the user hovers.

**Failure.** Moving the mouse across the chart triggers a full React render plus a full canvas repaint per pointer event on top of the 19.5/s feed redraws; on a high-rate mouse that is 100+ repaints/s of a 1200x500@2x canvas, visible as crosshair stutter. A resize during an oracle outage leaves a mis-sized chart.

**Fix.** Coalesce draws through requestAnimationFrame (keep a pending flag in a ref; the effect schedules one frame and returns a cancel), memoize `visible` on [candles, start, end], cache the four token colours in a ref keyed on themeTick, and skip setHover when hover.i is unchanged. Finding 2's dedup removes 95% of the feed-driven runs by itself.


### [medium/repo] The shared order-book poller re-decodes and re-renders the whole page every 2.4 s even when the account bytes have not changed — measured 9 consecutive byte-identical responses

`frontend/src/lib/shared-source.ts:131`

**Evidence.** shared-source.ts:115-131: run() always assigns `e.data = next` and calls every subscriber's `rerender()` (a useReducer increment, :76 — never bails). use-orderbook.ts:92-151 builds a brand-new object graph per tick: decodeOrderBook constructs 2048 + 2×4096 = 10,240 PublicKeys (measured 3.7 ms in V8 on the live 626,736-byte buffer), recentFills walks+sorts the 4096 ring twice (:123, :146), and returns fresh bids/asks/orderSlots/fillEvents arrays. Live measurement on https://slipstream.ansht.tech/trade (Playwright, no wallet): /api/rpc/er getAccountInfo issued at 113, 2114, 4628, 7054, 9482, 11909, 14332, 16762, 19197 ms (period 2.43 s), every response 51,557 transfer bytes / 835,924 decoded — identical, the book did not change once in 20 s — and each one fanned out to the 8 useOrderBook mounts (order-book-display, status-panel, fill-toasts, order-form, positions-table ×3 incl. useOpenOrders + useErPosition, trade-history when open): ladder reconciliation of 30 Rows plus a second render from useLevelStamps' setStamps (order-book-display.tsx:404-432), useOpenOrders' 2048-slot walk (use-open-orders.ts:40-50), and — because `fillEvents` is a new array — the 15 ms reconstruct() of the finding above, all for a no-op. Measured 12.56 MB decoded per 35 s (~360 KB/s decoded, 21 KB/s on the wire).

**Failure.** Every idle /trade tab burns decode + 8 component-tree re-renders + the reconstruct replay ~25×/min for a book that is not moving; with a wallet connected the no-change tick alone costs ~20 ms of main thread. Scales linearly with subscribers, so every new consumer of the shared book makes idle ticks more expensive.

**Fix.** In the use-orderbook fetcher keep the last raw Buffer and its decoded result; if `info.data.equals(lastRaw)` (~0.1 ms for 626 KB) return the previous object. In shared-source run(), `if (Object.is(next, e.data)) return;` before notifying — subscribers' useMemo/Object.is guards then hold. ~6 lines, no deploy.


### [medium/repo] Every poller fires its first scheduled tick `ms` after the initial fetch STARTS, not after it completes, so the overlap guard poll.ts exists for is missing on mount

`frontend/src/lib/shared-source.ts:137`

**Evidence.** shared-source.ts:137-138 `void run().catch(() => {}); e.stop = startPoll(run, intervalMs);` and poll.ts:48 `timer = setTimeout(tick, ms)` — startPoll's contract (:17-18, 'never more than one in flight per poller') only holds from the second tick on; the immediate call and the first scheduled tick are independent. Same shape at use-positions.ts:231-232, use-session.ts:482-483 (×3 instances), use-triggers.ts:53-54, use-pyth-candles.ts:109-112, status-panel.tsx:90-91, trade-history.tsx:65-66. Measured on production: the second ER book request started at 2,114 ms, exactly 2,001 ms after the first STARTED at 113 ms (it completed at ~541 ms). From here the proxied read takes 410-497 ms so they do not overlap, but the direct ER read of the same account measured 2,107 ms (835,887 wire bytes) and use-session.ts:437-438 documents 10-30 s 429 windows on the base layer — in either case the mount fetch and the first tick run concurrently and the seq guard (:108-114) throws one 836 KB read away. Every ActivityDrawer tab switch remounts a useSession instance (open-orders.tsx:23 / positions-table.tsx:120) and repeats it.

**Failure.** Under exactly the latency the backoff was written for, each mount issues two concurrent copies of its heaviest request (an 836 KB book, a 4-account getMultipleAccountsInfo + ER read) and discards one; a 429 during page load doubles the burst that caused it.

**Fix.** Give startPoll an `immediate` option that awaits `fn()` before arming the timer (3 lines in poll.ts), and drop the manual first call at the 8 sites. No deploy.


### [medium/repo] useSession is still a per-instance poller mounted 3× on /trade: 6 RPC round trips per 5 s for one wallet, and three copies of `available` that disagree for up to 5 s

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:481`  · prior: S9-03

**Evidence.** use-session.ts:368-484: each useSession() call owns its own useState + startPoll(refresh, 5_000); refresh → readSessionState issues one getMultipleAccountsInfo over 4 accounts (:232-234) plus one ER getAccountInfo when delegated (:294). Mounted simultaneously by order-form.tsx:61, session-panel.tsx:47 and positions-table.tsx:120 (or open-orders.tsx:23 on the Orders tab) — 3 instances, 6 requests per 5 s, all fired within the same mount burst. positions-table.tsx:242-252 documents the consequence ('mounted four times on this page and only the instance that ran an action refreshes eagerly — so this copy of `available` can be up to 5s behind the chain') and downgrades a refusal to a warning to work around it. useMarket/useOrderBook/useLivePrice were all migrated to one loop per key (S9-03's remediation, shared-source.ts:6-29); useSession, the one hook that also reads the ER, was not. Restates the residual half of S9-03 (the setInterval/no-backoff half is fixed on this branch).

**Failure.** With a wallet connected the tab's RPC budget is dominated by three identical session reads (1.2 req/s of ~2.4 total); an action confirmed through one instance renders as not-yet-happened in the other two panels for a poll period, and the flatten pre-flight has to warn-and-send instead of trusting its own number.

**Fix.** Route readSessionState through useSharedSource keyed `session:${owner}:${marketIndex}` and replace the eager `refresh()` calls in the action handlers with `revalidate(key)`; keep the per-instance busy/error/notice state. No deploy.


### [low/both] TradingDashboard subscribes to useMarket only to compute a prop that positions-table documents as dead, re-rendering the entire terminal tree every 5s

`frontend/src/components/trading/dashboard.tsx:27`

**Evidence.** dashboard.tsx:27-28 reads useMarket(0) to build `markPrice`, passed at :50 to ActivityDrawer and on to PositionsTable at activity-drawer.tsx:60. positions-table.tsx:47-56 declares that prop 'DEAD, and kept only so the parent compiles unchanged' and destructures nothing from it (:58). No React Compiler is configured (next.config.ts is empty, no babel-plugin-react-compiler in package.json) and no child is wrapped in memo(), so each 5s market tick (useMarket's fetcher returns a fresh object every time, use-market.ts:530) re-renders TerminalNav, MarketBar, PriceChart, OrderBookDisplay and its 30 Row components (each re-running useFlash's JSON.stringify at order-book-display.tsx:292), OrderForm, SessionPanel, StatusPanel, StatusStrip and FillToasts from the root.

**Failure.** One redundant full-tree render every 5s for the life of the tab, on top of the per-hook renders those components already do; pure waste, bounded.

**Fix.** Delete dashboard.tsx:27-28, the markPrice prop at :50 and activity-drawer.tsx:60, and the dead PositionsTableProps at positions-table.tsx:46-58.


### [low/both] useMarkPrice runs one 5s setInterval per mount (5 clocks on /trade) so 'nowSec' differs across surfaces by up to 5s, contradicting its own stated purpose

`frontend/src/hooks/use-mark-price.ts:85`

**Evidence.** useState(() => Date.now()) at :84 and setInterval(..., 5_000) at :85-88 are per hook instance; the hook is mounted by market-bar.tsx:34, price-chart.tsx:30, status-panel.tsx:55, order-form.tsx:70 and positions-table.tsx:76, each interval phase-locked to its own mount time. The doc comment at :162-166 (and :47-51) says nowSec is exported so two rows on one page cannot 'disagree about what time it is' -- true within one component, false across the five: market-bar's dayStale (market-bar.tsx:64) and status-panel's ageMins can flip on different seconds. Each interval also forces an extra render of its consumer every 5s, and the hook returns a fresh object every render (:159-171) so nothing downstream can memoize on its identity.

**Failure.** Five staleness clocks instead of one: staleness banners on different panels can disagree for up to 5s, and five unsynchronised re-render timers run for the life of the tab.

**Fix.** A module-level ticker exposed via useSyncExternalStore (the same shape use-live-price.ts already uses at :261-262): one setInterval started by the first subscriber, stopped by the last.


### [low/both] Fill toast expiry timers are never cleared on unmount

`frontend/src/components/trading/fill-toasts.tsx:70`

**Evidence.** The effect at :53-75 schedules one setTimeout(..., 6_000) per new own-fill at :70-73 and returns no cleanup; the timers call setToasts on a component that may have unmounted (route change away from /trade). React 19 silently ignores the state update, so this is a 6s-bounded stray timer rather than a leak.

**Failure.** Nothing user-visible; stray timers fire after navigation for up to 6s. Hygiene.

**Fix.** Keep the timer ids in a ref and clearTimeout them in the effect cleanup, or derive expiry from a single interval over toast timestamps.


### [low/both] use-price-history.ts and useUserAccount are dead code that would write up to 2,000 samples to localStorage per price change if anything mounted them

`frontend/src/hooks/use-price-history.ts:39`  · prior: S9-03

**Evidence.** Restates the S9 hook inventory (s9.md:27-28): zero call sites for usePriceHistory/bucketCandles (grep across frontend/src) and for useUserAccount (use-positions.ts:49-93). usePriceHistory's effect at :39-54 would JSON.stringify the whole capped array and localStorage.setItem it on EVERY price change (:48) -- at the measured 19.5 frames/s that is ~70KB serialised 20x/s -- and it keys a global 'slipstream:price-history:0' with no market scoping. Still present on this branch.

**Failure.** No runtime impact today (unmounted); a future mount would add a synchronous ~70KB localStorage write per oracle frame. Dead surface the next reader has to rule out.

**Fix.** Delete frontend/src/hooks/use-price-history.ts and useUserAccount (use-positions.ts:49-93).


### [low/repo] TradingDashboard subscribes to the market source solely to pass a prop the receiver declares DEAD, so the entire terminal tree re-renders every 5 s for nothing

`frontend/src/components/trading/dashboard.tsx:27`

**Evidence.** dashboard.tsx:27-28 `const { market } = useMarket(0); const markPrice = market?.lastMarkPrice ?? null;` → :50 `<ActivityDrawer markPrice={markPrice} />` → activity-drawer.tsx:21,60 forwards it → positions-table.tsx:58 `export function PositionsTable({}: PositionsTableProps)` destructures nothing, and :46-56 says the prop is 'DEAD, and kept only so the parent compiles unchanged … Delete the prop and the parent's argument together.' Because TradingDashboard is the root of /trade (app/trade/page.tsx:11) and none of its children are React.memo, every market tick (measured /api/rpc/base every ~5.2 s) re-renders TerminalNav, MarketBar, PriceChart, OrderBookDisplay (30 Rows), OrderForm, SessionPanel, StatusPanel, StatusStrip and FillToasts — none of which read anything from this component.

**Failure.** A guaranteed full-page reconciliation 12×/min that no component asked for; harmless today, but it is the pattern that turns every future hook added to the root into a page-wide re-render.

**Fix.** Delete dashboard.tsx:27-28 and the `markPrice` prop from ActivityDrawer/PositionsTable (the file's own instruction). TradingDashboard then has no state and never re-renders. No deploy.


### [low/repo] PriceChart re-renders its whole tree and redraws the canvas at the feed rate (measured 20.4 draws/s with a forced style read each) — cheap today, but `visible` is unmemoised so the draw also fires on hover, the 5 s clock and the parent's tick

`frontend/src/components/trading/price-chart.tsx:254`

**Evidence.** Measured on production: canvas clearRect 20.4/s, getComputedStyle 20.4/s, clientWidth reads 20.4/s (one of each per WS frame, price-chart.tsx:263-264, :298, :302), draw body ~0.2 ms (max 0.6 ms) → ~4-8 ms/s; zero long tasks in 60 s; canvas.width reassigned once in 95 s — so the re-alloc guard at :280-286 works as its comment claims, and the 'only lineWidth survives' note at :290-297 is correct (font :344 and textBaseline :345 are reset every frame, textAlign is assigned before each read at :356/:362/:447, setLineDash is cleared at :443/:461, no globalAlpha/composite mutation). What the comment does not say: `visible = candles.slice(start, end)` at :254 is computed in the render body, so the effect's `[visible, …]` dep at :463 is a new array on EVERY render of CandleCanvas, and CandleCanvas re-renders on every PriceChart render — the WS frame (useLivePrice :21), useMarkPrice's own 5 s setInterval (use-mark-price.ts:85-88, one per instance — 5 instances on /trade, 12 setInterval registrations measured at load), the market tick, the dashboard tick above, and every mousemove (setHover :510). The toolbar's 9 buttons and the legend (:113-176) reconcile at 20 Hz for a header string that changes ~17×/s. getComputedStyle+clientWidth run immediately after React commits the legend text in the same task — a forced style/layout per frame, small only because the DOM is small.

**Failure.** None user-visible at today's DOM size; the chart is the first thing to jank once the page grows (a depth tab, a second market) because the draw is coupled to five unrelated render triggers instead of to data change.

**Fix.** useMemo `visible` on [candles, start, end]; resolve the four CSS tokens once per themeTick into a ref instead of per frame; wrap the toolbar/legend in React.memo or move the header price into its own leaf component so the 20 Hz update touches one text node. No deploy.


### [low/repo] Comment claims that no longer match the code or the measurements: a retracted WebSocket-leak diagnosis still asserted in use-wallet-compat, two files giving different root causes for ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES ×1479, and bandwidth figures quoted in decoded bytes that are 16× the wire

`frontend/src/hooks/use-wallet-compat.ts:31`

**Evidence.** (1) use-wallet-compat.ts:31-33 'Constructing one is not free: each spawns a WebSocket client that reconnects forever against a ws:// URL the HTTP proxy cannot serve' — connections.ts:12-19 explicitly retracts this; verified against web3.js 1.98.4 lib/index.cjs.js:6104-6106 (`autoconnect: false`) and _updateSubscriptions at :8313 reached only from subscribe paths the app never calls. (2) poll.ts:3-15 attributes the ×1479 ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES to setInterval pile-up across nine hooks; connections.ts:25-27 says 'The real cause … was elsewhere — an unstable effect dependency re-firing fetches ~20x/second (see use-positions.ts)'. use-positions.ts:98-106 agrees with connections.ts and its deps are now [connection, owner] (:199) — verified stable: @phantom/react-sdk 2.0.2 `addresses` is React state (dist/index.mjs:766) and useConnection is a constant memo (use-wallet-compat.ts:35-37). (3) use-orderbook.ts:82-89 ('~835 KB/s of pure duplication'), shared-source.ts:19-21 ('~1.25 MB/s … three quarters of everything this tab moves') and use-er-position.ts:157-160 quote decoded sizes; measured wire size of one book response is 51,257 B gzip-encoded (835,924 decoded, 16.3×), i.e. ~21 KB/s at the 2.43 s cadence — the duplication argument still holds, the magnitudes do not. Claims that DO match: use-live-price.ts:74-80 (one socket — measured 1 WebSocket, 20.0 msg/s), use-mark-price.ts:40-41 (~50 ms cadence — measured 19.9/s; publishTime advances only 1/s, 21 distinct in 20 s), use-orderbook.ts:84-85 'status-panel reads eight bytes' (status-panel.tsx:53 reads only nextFillSequence, a u64), shared-source.ts:163-164 render gaps (measured 2.43 s).

**Failure.** The next engineer chasing a socket or bandwidth problem is pointed at a diagnosis this codebase has already retracted twice; the file that claims to be the RPC chokepoint and the file that claims to be the overlap fix disagree about what the outage was.

**Fix.** Delete use-wallet-compat.ts:31-33 (connections.ts owns the explanation), make poll.ts:3-15 cite connections.ts:25-27 for the ×1479 root cause, and restate the three bandwidth figures as decoded bytes with the measured ~51 KB gzip wire size. No deploy.


### [low/repo] Hygiene batch: two dead hooks still exported, a toast timer never cleared, an index key on the positions rows, and the market bar fetching 7 days of candles to use 24

`frontend/src/hooks/use-price-history.ts:23`  · prior: S9-C5

**Evidence.** (a) use-price-history.ts:23-58 usePriceHistory / :72 bucketCandles and use-positions.ts:49-93 useUserAccount have zero call sites (grep over frontend/src) — restates the S9-C5 producer table rows 'mounted by nothing'; usePriceHistory would JSON.stringify a 2,000-sample array into localStorage on every price change if anyone ever mounted it with the 20 Hz feed (:44-50). (b) fill-toasts.tsx:69-73 arms one setTimeout per toast and never clears it on unmount (no cleanup in the effect at :34-74); React 19 no-ops the late setState, so cost is a retained closure per toast for 6 s — bounded. (c) positions-table.tsx:706 `<tr key={i}>` on the L1 rows; benign only because MARKET_INDEX is the single market. (d) market-bar.tsx:9,35 mounts a second usePythCandles instance at the 1H resolution whose lookback is 7 days (use-pyth-candles.ts:29, 168 buckets, measured 8,936 B) to slice the last 24 (:39) — a second 60 s poller with no sharing with the chart's instance. Heap over 60 s idle: 37.8 → 48.4 → 44.7 → 44.2 MB, listeners +4 net, setInterval count constant at 12 — nothing else in scope scales with tab-open time.

**Failure.** None today; each item is a small trap (dead hook someone revives, a timer that outlives its component, a key that breaks the day a second market ships).

**Fix.** Delete use-price-history.ts and useUserAccount; return a cleanup in the fill-toasts effect that clears the pending timers; key the position rows on marketIndex; give the market bar a `lookback: 24h` resolution entry or derive the 24h window from the chart's shared candles. No deploy.

### [high/both] fill-log keeper enters an unbounded epoch-rotate loop (0.059 SOL per ~45 s) whenever settlement cannot advance; it has fired twice and re-arms on the next keeper start

`keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:433`  · prior: S7-12

**Evidence.** Rotation trigger: mirror() maps program error 0x11e FillQueueFull to "error" (fill-log-keeper.ts:151-153); tick() rotates after MIRROR_ERRORS_BEFORE_ROTATE=3 (:65, :431-440); rotate() -> ensureEpochReady() -> initialize_fill_log CreateAccount paid by the keeper (:113-114; programs/slipstream/src/instructions/initialize_fill_log.rs:87-104), rent 58,965,120 lamports for 8,344 bytes. A fresh epoch re-mirrors the OrderBook ring from sequence 0 by design (mirror_fills.rs:111-114 `virgin`), the FillLog holds 80 (fill_log.rs:14), so once the ring holds >80 fills that L1 settlement cannot consume, every fresh epoch fills, refuses (mirror_fills.rs:150-158, :166-171), and rotates again. Settlement cannot consume them because settle_from_log needs the contiguous run from Market.last_settled_sequence+1 (:250-259): LIVE (2026-09-05T05:30Z) cursor @market.bin[2058] = 44,189 while the ER ring holds seq 49,638..53,733 (4,096/4,096 slots, 0 gaps) -- fill 44,190 exists nowhere (overwritten by the deployed lossy ring), so the queue is wedged forever. LIVE proof of the loop: epochs 431..460 created 2026-08-31T06:19:44Z..06:41:24Z (30 rotations, 44.8 s apart, 1.769 SOL) and epochs 404..430 on 2026-08-27T14:15:57Z..14:31:43Z (27 rotations, 36.4 s apart, 1.592 SOL); ER getSignaturesForAddress on epoch 455's PDA shows the exact signature: 3 OK mirror/commit pairs then 3x `{"Custom":286}` (0x11e) at 06:38:14/19/24Z then a new epoch. Epochs 451-459 all end at last_mirrored_sequence=48,623/count=80 (identical), i.e. each new epoch re-copied the same 80 fills. Epoch 460 is virgin (count 0) = the keeper was stopped mid-loop. The loop is what "fill-log STOPPED on purpose" is hiding; restarting it against the current ring (fresh epoch mirrors 53,249..53,328, commit gate passes since 53,328 > 44,189 at :178-179, settle() returns at :259, next mirror -> FillQueueFull x3 -> rotate) re-enters it immediately. ecosystem.config.js:43 lists fill-log for pm2, so `pm2 resurrect`/`pm2 start ecosystem.config.js` is the precondition.

**Failure.** Every ~45 s the shared keeper/authority wallet loses 0.059 SOL (4.74 SOL/hour) to a delegated account that can never be reclaimed (see next finding). At the live balance of 1.850 SOL the wallet is empty in ~23 minutes, after which twap/funding/liquidation/expiry all fail preflight on fees and keep looping silently (no exit, no alert), so mark price goes stale and the S5-C7 contract is broken -- a total keeper outage triggered by one ordinary restart.

**Fix.** Keeper-side guard, no deploy: in tick() before rotate() (fill-log-keeper.ts:433-440) read the L1 cursor and the current epoch's committed L1 FillLog; if `cursor+1` is not among its sequences (settlement wedged, not budget exhausted), log `settlement wedged at seq ${cursor+1}` and sleep 5 min instead of rotating. Add a hard cap (e.g. `rotationsThisHour > 3` -> refuse) and a wallet floor (`getBalance(keeper) < 0.5 SOL` -> refuse to init). The wedge itself (cursor 44,189 vs ring min 49,638) needs an operator cursor-advance path = NEEDS-DEPLOY (S4-C8/R4 territory).


### [high/both] 27.18 SOL of keeper-wallet rent is permanently stranded in 461 delegated FillLog accounts; the program has no undelegate or close path for them (105 epochs, 6.19 SOL, were never even mirrored into)

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/mod.rs:163`  · prior: S7-12

**Evidence.** LIVE getMultipleAccounts over derived PDAs [b"fill_log", u16le(0), u32le(epoch)] for epochs 0..699: 461 exist (0..460), each 58,965,120 lamports / 8,344 bytes, ALL owned by DELeGGvXpWV2fqJUhqcF5ZSYMS4JTLjteaAMARRSaeSh; sum = 27,182,920,320 lamports = 27.183 SOL; 105 of them have count=0 && last_mirrored_sequence=0 (virgin: created by the rotate loop and never used) = 6.191 SOL; 356 are full (count=80). Epoch 0 created 2026-05-30T20:59:38Z, epoch 460 2026-08-31T06:41:24Z. The dispatch table has initialize_fill_log (0x1D), delegate_fill_log (0x1E), commit_fill_log (0x20) and nothing that undelegates or closes a FillLog (mod.rs:30-33, :163-166); commit_fill_log explicitly commits WITHOUT undelegating (commit_fill_log.rs:33-35). fill-log-keeper.ts:36-40 documents the design as 'old epoch logs are drained then abandoned (each ~8 KB, trivial devnet rent)' -- 0.059 SOL each is 15x the wallet's entire daily fee spend (0.004 SOL) and 461 of them is 15x the wallet's current balance.

**Failure.** Operator SOL is locked with no code path to recover it, and each future rotation adds 0.059 SOL to the pile. On a real-money deployment this is a rent liability that grows with fill volume and with every transient error (next finding), never with revenue.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: add `undelegate_fill_log` (ER: CPI magic ScheduleCommitAndUndelegate for the FillLog PDA, mirroring undelegate_trading_credit) and `close_fill_log` (L1: authority-or-keeper signer, require epoch < current and count settled, transfer lamports to payer, assign to system). Until deployed, stop the bleeding with the rotate guard above and set FILL_LOG_START_EPOCH=460 so boot does not walk 461 ER getAccountInfo calls (discoverEpoch, :99-106).


### [high/deployed] Keeper wallet 7.96 -> 1.85 SOL attributed: 75% is manual transfers to an unrelated project's treasury, 23% is program-deploy buffers for four foreign program IDs, 1.1% is keeper fees -- the shared authority key is being used as a general-purpose deployer

`keepers/src/shared/connection.ts:95`  · prior: S7-04

**Evidence.** Aggregated 14,072 base-layer transactions of A5sV4PkkVM4gm3rejACvKFgxEMmj8ouGsffSKT5qYVc8 between 2026-08-31T16:01Z and 2026-09-04T20:30Z (getSignaturesForAddress + getTransaction preBalances/postBalances; 139 of 14,211 not retrievable). Net delta -6.0907 SOL, of which: (a) -4.600 SOL = 4 System transfers (1.0 @ 09-01T08:16Z, 0.6 @ 09-01T16:58Z, 1.0 @ 09-01T21:22Z, 2.0 @ 09-04T06:25Z) to FbDoanjAUonn5wM4KbRNS3jigHe6zDa4ThoEUGPnS7ka -- an address absent from this repo that the operator's own notes identify as the HEARTROT worker treasury; (b) -1.417 SOL net BPF-upgradeable-loader activity: 22 write-buffer CreateAccount+InitializeBuffer (-14.485), 4 CreateAccount+DeployWithMaxDataLen creating NEW program IDs E5NnKCk6.., 3dHhnysH.., FqpkfB9u.., 42KtVUEv.. (-3.099), 2,551 Write chunks (-0.0128 fees), 21 buffer Close refunds (+15.332), +0.835 close; none of these program IDs is 7qujfsb4 (deploy.json:3), i.e. one write-buffer (~0.7 SOL) is still open and 4 throwaway programs were funded from this key; (c) -0.0698 SOL total fees: 11,187 crank_twap (disc 0x0B) = 0.0559, 5 compute_funding (0x06), 82 landed-but-failed cranks (0x104 OracleStale after preflight), no settle/fill-log/liquidation tx at all in the window; (d) -0.0044 SOL two dust transfers. Keeper fees are 0.041 SOL/day at full 8 s cadence (8,228 cranks on 09-01) and 0.0008 SOL/day at the publish-aware rate (163 cranks on 09-04). The FillLog rotate loop (1.769 SOL) fell just before this window (06:19-06:41Z on 08-31). There is exactly one key path in the package (connection.ts:95-121 loadKeypair; bot-wallets.ts:453-455 getOperator = loadKeypair) and it is deploy.json:5 `authority` = the program upgrade authority and USDC mint authority. Live balance 2026-09-05T05:34Z: 1.850280758 SOL.

**Failure.** The fee budget of every keeper is coupled to unrelated operator activity on the same key: one more `solana transfer <treasury> 2` and every keeper fails preflight and spins silently (no exit, no alert, S7-01). Conversely the keepers cannot be given a rent/fee floor because the wallet's balance is not theirs to reason about. This also puts the upgrade authority on a box that is used interactively for other projects' deploys.

**Fix.** Create a dedicated keeper keypair funded with ~1 SOL, point keepers/.env KEEPER_KEYPAIR at it (crank_twap/compute_funding/liquidate/execute_trigger/cancel_order are permissionless today; only initialize_fill_log needs authority on the DEPLOYED program, and R7 set_keeper 0x2A removes even that once deployed). Add a 3-line balance floor to each keeper loop: `if ((await connection.getBalance(keeper.publicKey)) < 0.3e9) { log(...); await sleep(300_000); continue; }`. Stop using A5sV.. as a deployer for other program IDs (close the open buffer: `solana program show --buffers` / `solana program close --buffers`).


### [medium/repo] mirror() counts ANY thrown error toward the rotate threshold, and the ER send path still confirms over the websocket the base path abandoned, so three 10 s ER confirm timeouts cost 0.059 SOL and abandon a live epoch

`keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:148`  · prior: S7-12

**Evidence.** mirror() catch (fill-log-keeper.ts:148-157): only FillQueueEmpty returns "empty"; FillQueueFull AND every other throw (code===null) return "error", which tick() counts toward MIRROR_ERRORS_BEFORE_ROTATE=3 (:65, :431-440). sendErTx confirms with `er.confirmTransaction(sig, "confirmed")` (shared/ertx.ts:308-309) = signatureSubscribe over the ER websocket with confirmTransactionInitialTimeout 10_000 (shared/connection.ts:110-115); connection.ts:126-141 documents that this exact mechanism produced 'Transaction was not confirmed' for a tx that HAD landed on the base layer and was replaced by getSignatureStatuses polling -- only for the base layer. A timed-out mirror_fills that actually landed still returns "error"; three in a row (one flaky ER minute, or an ER RPC 429/5xx, or a blockhash fetch failure) trigger rotate() and a 0.059 SOL CreateAccount for an epoch whose commit budget was unspent. commit() has the same substring-only recovery already filed as S7-12 (:192). Confirm-timeout idempotence elsewhere is sound: base sends are state-gated on chain (compute_funding interval compute_funding.rs:36-43; liquidate/execute_trigger by position state; settle by Market cursor re-read at :237; initialize_fill_log by getAccountInfo at :111-112), and crank_twap is ungated (crank_twap.rs:44-76) but a duplicate sample is harmless; ER place_order timeouts in the MM are reconciled next cycle via myOrders (market-maker-bot.ts:183-192) so no double ladder.

**Failure.** Rent burn proportional to ER flakiness rather than to fills, plus loss of the epoch's remaining sponsored commits; combined with the previous finding this is the second independent path to the same drain.

**Fix.** In mirror(), return "error" only for `c.code === ERR_FILL_QUEUE_FULL || c.code === 0x11c /*InvalidFillSequence*/`; for `c.code === null` return a new "transient" that does not touch mirrorErrors. In sendErTx replace confirmTransaction with the same getSignatureStatuses poll used by confirmBySignatureStatus (export it from connection.ts and pass the ER connection) so a landed tx is never reported as failed.


### [medium/deployed] Bot fleet is economically dead but still pays full read cost: 3 of 4 bot credits are under $10, one MM credit is delegation-program-owned on both layers, yet each cycle still downloads the 836 KB order book per wallet before checking credit

`keepers/src/taker-bot.ts:141`  · prior: S7-03

**Evidence.** LIVE 2026-09-05T05:34Z (bot owners identified from the ER fill ring: 26ESjFZz.., biry5wEE.., HjfonG4B.., Cq1Gw5fX.. account for 4,096/4,096 ring entries, all qty 200,000,000 = BOT_TAKER_SIZE_LOTS 2, fee 6/1 bps): ER TradingCredit available = $9.45 (26ESjF, and its ER copy is OWNED BY DELeGG.. = the 'stuck on both layers' state ecosystem.config.js:60-66 describes), $0.91 (biry), $0.98 (Hjfon), $3,880 with 12 active orders / $122 committed (Cq1Gw5 -- the only wallet quoting; ecosystem.config.js:50-53 promises 2 wallets x 12). Bot base/ER SOL 0.072-0.080 each. Per cycle the taker calls fetchOrderBook on the ER (taker-bot.ts:141, 836 KB per S7-03 measurement) and only then readTradingCredit (:199-203) which itself is 2 RPCs (bot-wallets.ts:256-270: isCreditDelegated on base + getAccountInfo on ER); with INTERVAL 15 s + JITTER 8 s (ecosystem.config.js:73-74) and CROSS_PROB 0.8 that is ~7,275 wallet-cycles/day = ~6.1 GB/day of ER reads and ~7,275 base calls/day to learn, every time, that the wallet has $0.9. The MM does fetchOrderBook once PER WALLET per 2 s cycle (market-maker-bot.ts:183, called from :288-290 in a loop) = 86,400 downloads/day (72 GB) although the book is identical for both wallets, fetchMarket every 2 s (:272) for fields that are static (tickSize/lotSize/maxLeverage) or slow (circuitBreakerActive), and isCreditDelegated on base on every refresh (bot-wallets.ts:264) for a fact that is constant after bot-setup. Base calls/day for the MM alone: 43,200 (market) + 43,200 (pyth) + up to 86,400 (isCreditDelegated) = 86k-173k, matching the 86k-260k range in scope. The taker's stated purpose (ecosystem.config.js:69-71: feed Recent Trades / fills indexer) is void: the indexer is the stopped fill-log keeper and the deployed ring wraps every 4,096/7,275 = 13.5 h, destroying every fill (cursor 44,189 vs ring min 49,638).

**Failure.** Roughly 78 GB/day of ER egress and up to ~180k base RPC calls/day (the quota that produced the 2.7-day outage) are spent by bots that cannot trade, while the demo book shows one wallet's 12 orders instead of the advertised 24-order ladder and nothing crosses it.

**Fix.** (1) Reorder: read credit first, skip fetchOrderBook when `credit.available < marginForOrder(...)` (taker-bot.ts:141 <-> :199; market-maker-bot.ts:183 <-> :227). (2) Fetch the order book once per cycle in main() and pass it into cycleWallet (market-maker-bot.ts:288-290). (3) Cache isCreditDelegated=true for the process lifetime and fetchMarket every 30 s. Net: ~46k base calls/day and ~36 GB/day, no behavior change. (4) Run topup-takers or retire the two exhausted takers; the 26ESjF credit needs the fresh-prefix workaround again (S7-08).


### [medium/both] Nothing pages a human: the only liveness signal is a passive `keepers.stalled` boolean (funding only, 24 h threshold) that no process polls; the smallest fix is a dead-man ping per keeper loop plus a balance floor

`keepers/ecosystem.config.js:7`  · prior: S7-01

**Evidence.** grep -rniE 'heartbeat|webhook|alert|pagerduty|healthcheck' keepers/src keepers/ecosystem.config.js keepers/.env.example -> no matches (re-verified 2026-09-05 on 87f9eb1; S7-01's proof still holds). What changed since S7-01: frontend/src/app/api/status/route.ts:70-73 and :140-143 now compute `keepers.stalled = ageSecs > fundingIntervalSecs * 3` -- with the live interval of 28,800 s (market.bin @160) that is a 24-hour threshold, covers only compute_funding, and is a JSON field on a page nobody polls. Every keeper still resets consecutiveErrors after its backoff (twap-keeper.ts:97-101, funding-keeper.ts:176-180, liquidation-keeper.ts:335-339, fill-log-keeper.ts:490-494, market-maker-bot.ts:295-299, taker-bot.ts:284-288) and none exits, which is how pm2 showed 'online' through the 2.7-day outage and how the rotate loop ran unwitnessed twice. The wallet has no floor anywhere; at 1.850 SOL a third rotate-loop episode empties it in 23 min.

**Failure.** Every failure in this dimension -- fee wallet empty, rotate loop, exhausted bot credits, stale mark -- is discovered by a human reading the UI days later; MTTR is measured in days (17-day and 2.7-day outages on record).

**Fix.** Smallest change, no new dependency, ~4 lines per keeper: after each SUCCESSFUL iteration `if (process.env.HEARTBEAT_URL) fetch(process.env.HEARTBEAT_URL).catch(() => {});` where HEARTBEAT_URL is a free dead-man's-switch check (healthchecks.io / cronitor style, one per keeper with a grace period = 3x its cadence: twap 15 min, funding 25 h, liquidation 5 min, expiry 10 min, mm 5 min); pings stop -> the service emails/pushes. Gate the ping on `getBalance(keeper) >= 0.3 SOL` so a low wallet also trips it. This covers the wedge, the drain, and the outage classes with zero infrastructure on the box.


### [low/repo] settlement-keeper.ts, if it ever wakes via `npm run all`, subscribes to every ER order-book change (one per MM cancel/replace, ~every 2 s) and spends the OrderBook's 10 sponsored commits pushing a 626 KB account it can never settle from

`keepers/src/settlement-keeper.ts:337`  · prior: S7-06

**Evidence.** package.json:14 still starts `npm:settlement` and omits fill-log (S7-06). On wake: subscriber.subscribe(orderBookPda) on the ER (:337-339) fires on every account change; scheduleTick debounces 200 ms (:328-334); tick() -> commitErToL1() sends commit_orderbook = magic ScheduleCommit of the 626,736-byte OrderBook (:106-123) whenever erHeader.fillEventCount > 0 -- which is always, the live ring has 4,096 entries -- then polls L1 20 x 1.5 s reading the full 626 KB book each time (:127-145). The sponsored-commit cap fill-log-keeper.ts:36-40 documents as 'a hard 10 PER delegated account' means the 11th commit_orderbook fails forever; the keeper logs 'commit did not land' and retries on the next change. settleFromL1 (:158-231) walks from readMarketCursor()+1 = 44,190 and breaks at the first gap (:212) -- the L1 ring can never contain 44,190 -- so it settles nothing. Nothing exits; main() sleeps forever (:348).

**Failure.** An operator following bot-setup.ts:60's printed instruction gets a process that burns the OrderBook's entire sponsored-commit budget within its first ~10 fill events (each is also a full-book push on the ER), then downloads ~12.5 MB of L1 order book per fill event indefinitely, and never settles a fill; a spent OrderBook commit budget may also block any future emergency_undelegate that relies on a commit.

**Fix.** Delete keepers/src/settlement-keeper.ts and the `settlement` script; change `all` to `concurrently "npm:fill-log-keeper" "npm:funding" "npm:liquidation" "npm:twap" "npm:expiry"`; fix bot-setup.ts:60's message. shared/subscriber.ts then has no importer and can go too.


### [low/both] crank_twap has no on-chain cadence gate, so the 8 s keeper lands ~8,200 identical-price samples/day at full feed cadence and paid for 82 cranks that passed preflight but failed on-chain with OracleStale

`keepers/src/twap-keeper.ts:9`

**Evidence.** crank_twap.rs:44-76 checks only reading.is_fresh(now_ts) and pushes unconditionally; CRANK_INTERVAL_MS = 8_000 (twap-keeper.ts:9), send at :89-91. Measured from the wallet's own history: 2,796 cranks on 08-31 (from 16:01Z), 8,228 on 09-01 (one per 10.5 s), 0 on 09-02/09-03 (outage), 163 on 09-04 (publish-aware faa6145 + 309 s feed). 82 cranks in the window landed with `{"Custom":260}` = 0x104 OracleStale after passing preflight (e.g. 5mGkNHJLFnq4dyDCieLxioVaNY6w2tZZws37Q48gpgmJyd1ApuMDwEFuM836szzARpP3Js6ketwiQQu1i7jvTPs1: 'failed: custom program error: 0x104', 568 CU) -- fee paid, counted as a keeper error toward the 60 s backoff. With the feed publishing every ~309 s and the 45 s send window, each publish is now stamped 5-6 times with the same price into the 225-sample TWAP ring, so the ring's time span stretches from 30 min to ~3 h of wall clock while the sample count stays constant.

**Failure.** 0.041 SOL/day in fees for duplicate samples when the feed is live (10x the '0.004/day ordinary fees' figure), a TWAP whose effective window silently depends on feed cadence, and OracleStale races that still cost fees and trip the backoff.

**Fix.** Keeper-side, 2 lines: remember `px.publishTime` of the last successful crank and `continue` when unchanged (twap-keeper.ts:66-73), which also removes the 0x104 race at the tail of each window. On-chain min-interval (e.g. refuse if mark_price_minute unchanged) would be NEEDS-DEPLOY and is optional.

### [critical/both] Every undelegation is a one-way door into 'half-delegated': the program has no handler for the delegation program's undelegate callback, so the validator's L1 undelegate fails and the credit is stranded on both layers

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/mod.rs:177`  · prior: S5-02

**Evidence.** Root cause (new, corrects S5-02): MagicBlock's delegation program `undelegate` (disc 0x03) CPIs back into the owner program with the Anchor `process_undelegation` discriminator [196,28,41,206,48,37,51,167] + borsh seeds, accounts [delegated, buffer, payer, system]. Live tx 522Ht4yT5bwRaFqoSSmqtPRfKPdJKu6YifakK7buw2x4JTFJ6m7q5Hw7c5aBSwccvcP2KbT7AUfsw6DdFioxPYWv (2026-08-31T06:26Z, signer = validator MAS1Dt9qreoRMQ14YQuhg8UTZMMzDdKhmkZMECCzk57): inner ix to 7qujfsb4 data=c41c29ce302533a7 03000000 06000000 'credit' 20000000 <owner> 02000000 <mkt>; logs 'Program 7qujfsb4… failed: invalid instruction data' after 109 CU. `process` at mod.rs:112-178 does `split_first()` (line 118) and matches one byte 0x00-0x2A; 0xC4 hits `_ => Err(InvalidInstructionData)` (line 177). The repo branch has the identical dispatcher, so target is both. Consequence: the ER commits and releases the account (ER copy becomes DELeGG-owned) but L1 never flips back. Live: 10 of the 30 Slipstream modern delegated credits are in this state — bbUpyr…, 7n8WFu…, 8SJPP7…, 8cp8h4…, AHeeSS…, AnXbnA…, BUVh3b…, CPZWN5…, F3PAZP…, GuWT7z… — and 10/10 carry a failed validator `undelegate` tx (2026-07-26, 07-29, 08-31); 0/20 ER-adopted credits do. Read-only ER simulateTransaction of the frontend's exact 'Withdraw' step-1 (undelegate_trading_credit 0x12) on bbUpyr…: 'ScheduleCommit ERR: account … is required to be writable and delegated in order to be undelegated' → ReadonlyDataModified. L1 fund/withdraw/close/seed all refuse (fund_trading_credit.rs:53, withdraw_trading_credit.rs:42, close_trading_credit.rs:65-67, seed_credit_ledger.rs:70 — owner != program). No TradingCredit has EVER been successfully undelegated on this deployment (the only program-owned modern credit Bkcwr5R4… was never delegated). The frontend's one-click Withdraw (use-session.ts:1091-1117) is the only exit and sends exactly this path; ER simulate of the same ix on adopted credits CBULcy…/DxnhAU… succeeds, i.e. every adopted credit is one click from this state.

**Failure.** Stuck now: 10 credits, 211.60 USDC credit + 15,590,400 lamports rent, owners' wallets can never hold a working credit for market 0 again (PDA is fixed per owner); 4 of the 10 owners are also pending_fills-locked. At risk on the next click: 20 ER-adopted modern credits holding 61,985.68 USDC (ER view; 98,500.00 L1 view), incl. the 4 local bot wallets (16,687.56 USDC ER / 20,000 L1) and a 18,845.65 USDC idle credit (DxnhAU…, owner BnhfPv…). Neither the user nor GlobalState.authority can recover any of them: no L1 instruction touches a DELeGG-owned account and the validator does not retry. Recovery of the existing 10 needs BOTH a program upgrade AND MagicBlock re-submitting `undelegate`.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. In mod.rs::process, before the 1-byte match: `if instruction_data.starts_with(&[196,28,41,206,48,37,51,167])` route to a new `process_undelegation` mirroring ephemeral-rollups-sdk `cpi::undelegate_account`: accounts [delegated, buffer, payer, system]; verify buffer == find_program_address([b"buffer", delegated.key], DELEG); deserialize borsh Vec<Vec<u8>> seeds, require find_program_address(seeds, program_id) == delegated.key (take bump); CreateAccount{from: payer, to: delegated, lamports: rent(buffer.len), space: buffer.len, owner: program_id}.invoke_signed(seeds+bump); copy buffer data into delegated. Works for 96/56-byte credits (not the 626 KB OrderBook). No protected test changes. Separately ask MagicBlock to re-run `undelegate` for the 10 listed accounts after the upgrade; until then the frontend must stop offering Withdraw (see low finding on use-session.ts:1115).


### [high/both] 12 delegated legacy 56-byte credits (36,470 USDC) are triple-locked: ER-side undelegate rejects them, their 20 resting orders can never be cancelled, and the repo's R2 only fixed close_trading_credit

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/undelegate_trading_credit.rs:96`  · prior: S1-X04

**Evidence.** Live (delegation record owner verified == 7qujfsb4 for all 12): 2cGWPX…, 3Cs3TJ…, 3kz2Ex…, 5oPJ3f…, 6sr6Ux…, 7UkUnY…, 7xwyLi…, 9hau7o…, AQWbkh…, D71q4p…, E4nvHe…, EZMfby… — L1 credit sum 36,470.00 USDC; ER copies (all ER-adopted, program-owned on devnet-as) show credit 20,676.41 + committed 7,477.54 across 20 active order slots (3kz2 4, 5oPJ 1, 6sr6 6, 9hau 1, AQWb 4, EZMf 4). Read-only ER simulate of undelegate_trading_credit on 3kz2Ex… and 9hau7o…: InvalidAccountData — `TradingCredit::from_account_info_mut` (line 96) rejects data.len() 56 < 96 before the magic CPI at line 119; cancel_order.rs:54 loads the credit the same way so the resting orders are permanent. Repo R2 added `read_common` only to close_trading_credit.rs:79-82 and states at close_trading_credit.rs:25-27 that the twelve are not recovered. Even after a length-tolerant undelegate, the L1 leg hits the missing callback (critical finding) and the L1 copy would carry committed>0 → close_trading_credit.rs:88-89 CreditStillActive. Owners include the two 200k whales (kgBAo4s… 9hau7o 160 USDC, HrkyqEf… D71q4p 160 USDC) and 4 bot wallets from the local key files (mm-0.legacy56 CDU9QzRg→AQWbkh 5,000; mm-1.legacy56 CUfBAWVo→6sr6Ux 5,000; mm-2 D2aeLE84→EZMfby 8,000; mm-3 FkB7cLff→3kz2Ex 8,000 = 26,000 USDC L1 / 20,238.60 ER).

**Failure.** 36,470.00 USDC of L1-recorded credit (20,676.41 free + 7,477.54 reserved on the ER) is unreachable by users and by the authority with no program path on either layer; 12 wallets can never trade market 0 again; 20 dead maker orders sit on the live book and can be filled by takers against credits nobody can reconcile.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, on top of the callback fix: in undelegate_trading_credit.rs:93-98 accept `data_len() == LEGACY_LEN` by reconciling via `TradingCredit::read_common` offsets (zero committed/active_orders and deactivate the owner's slots in the ER book) instead of the typed load, then let the existing ScheduleCommitAndUndelegate CPI run; cancel_order.rs:54 needs the same tolerant read so the 20 orders can be pulled. After the L1 handover lands, close_trading_credit's legacy branch (close_trading_credit.rs:97-114) returns the balance.
