### [high/repo] Deploy-time lock: the credit ceiling goes live with reserved_margin==0 on every owner, seed_credit_ledger refuses delegated credits, and the frontend withdraw aborts before withdraw_collateral

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/seed_credit_ledger.rs:70`

**Evidence.** Measured: 52/52 UserAccounts read reserved_margin==0 at offset 48; 42 delegated credits (12 legacy + 30 modern, L1 credit 135,181.60 USDC; ER view 82,873.69) plus 1 program-owned credit (Bkcwr5R4…, 500.00) have credit>0. After deploy, withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63-64 refuses every amount > reserved_margin (=0) with CreditCeilingExceeded, and close_trading_credit.rs:92-95 refuses a modern credit with credit>0, so no user can drain a credit until the authority runs seed_credit_ledger. seed_credit_ledger.rs:68-70 returns CreditStillActive unless the credit is program-owned, so 42 of 43 cannot be seeded until an undelegation lands — which the critical finding shows has never happened on this deployment. Frontend: use-session.ts:1134 passes the full `credit` (never min(credit, creditOutstanding)) and the whole `withdraw` callback throws at step 2, so step 3 (withdraw_collateral, which would succeed with reserved_margin==0) is never reached; errors.ts:24-37 has 56 names for 59 variants so the user sees 'Rejected on chain: custom 0x138.' (ertx.ts:21-33 same gap for keepers). seed_credit_ledger.rs:22-23 sizes the grandfather job at '45 live delegated credits' — the real count is 42 (see the foreign-account finding).

**Failure.** On the first post-deploy withdraw, every existing user with a funded credit is refused with an undecoded 0x138 and cannot even take out their free collateral through the UI; the authority can repair exactly one account (Bkcwr5R4…, 500 USDC) and none of the 42 delegated ones.

**Fix.** Program (NEEDS-DEPLOY): let seed_credit_ledger accept a delegated credit by writing `amount` alone when owner()==DELEG (the min(credit.credit, amount) bound is meaningless on a delegation-time snapshot anyway; the write stays authority-gated). Frontend: in use-session.ts withdraw, decode UserAccount.creditOutstanding and pass min(credit, creditOutstanding); on CreditCeilingExceeded continue to step 3 instead of throwing; append 'CreditCeilingExceeded','FillSequenceOutOfRange','LegacyLayoutRejected' to errors.ts:36 and ertx.ts:33. Sequence the deploy after the undelegation-callback fix.


### [high/repo] Gate 2 (ReservedMarginExists) goes live against an exit that has never completed: post-deploy, every funded credit permanently locks the owner's free_collateral and realized PnL

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/withdraw_collateral.rs:86`

**Evidence.** withdraw_collateral.rs:85-87 refuses any withdrawal while reserved_margin>0; close_user_account.rs:81 likewise. fund_trading_credit.rs:75-78 raises reserved_margin on every fund; the one-click Start trading path (use-session.ts:879-885) funds the entire deposit and delegates in the same transaction. The only reducers are withdraw_trading_credit.rs:74 (requires an undelegated credit: withdraw_trading_credit.rs:42) and settlement by filled_margin (settle_from_log.rs:260-271, only for fills that settle), and the only repair is seed_credit_ledger.rs:82, which refuses delegated credits at :68-70. Undelegation has succeeded 0 times on this deployment (critical finding). close_position.rs:228 pays collateral+PnL into free_collateral, i.e. straight behind gate 2. Live today the gate binds nowhere (52/52 reserved_margin==0), so this is a lock the deploy itself creates. The spec accepted gate 2 on the premise 'a user can withdraw credit first' (remediate.md:870-880); that premise is false on chain.

**Failure.** After deploy, a user who closes a profitable position cannot withdraw the profit, or any remaining free collateral, for as long as any credit they ever funded is outstanding — which, with no working undelegation, is forever; the authority cannot seed it to zero while the credit is delegated.

**Fix.** Take the spec's own second option (remediate.md:876-877): drop gate 2 from withdraw_collateral (keep it in close_user_account); free_collateral already excludes funded credit, so the gate protects nothing the ceiling in withdraw_trading_credit does not. NEEDS-DEPLOY. If the gate is kept, land the undelegation callback first and let seed_credit_ledger write a delegated owner's ledger.

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1280`  · prior: S1-01

The repo's R2 path (close_trading_credit.rs:69-114, read_common) fixes this only once deployed. Restates S1-X03 with a live simulation.

### [medium/repo] The audit and spec counted 22 foreign programs' accounts as Slipstream credits: the '7,017,392,213,830 USDC ER-authored garbage' does not exist, the delegated modern population is 30 not 45, and the runbook seeds accounts that are not ours

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/seed_credit_ledger.rs:22`  · prior: S5-01

**Evidence.** getProgramAccounts(DELeGG…, dataSize 96, byte0==6) returns 52; deriving each account's delegation record PDA ([b"delegation", account] under DELeGG) and reading offset 40..72: 30 name owner program 7qujfsb4 (commit_frequency u32::MAX, as delegate_trading_credit.rs:33 sets), 22 name 22 different owner programs (e.g. uydHfur… → HnezRqHTQm32…, vnGA74n… → 9PTYZerg…, commit_frequency 30,000 ms); for those 22, find_program_address([b"credit", data[8..40], market_le], 7qujfsb4) matches the account for 0/22 (30/30 for ours). The 22 are exactly the 'identical 7_017_392_213_830_636_841' rows in remediate.md:331 and :950 and in seed_credit_ledger.rs:22-23, and the 'demonstrably ER-authored garbage' offered as S5-01's live proof. Every genuine Slipstream credit reads a sane credit (max 20,000.00 USDC) on both layers. No production code repeats the mistake (no TS call site does gPA on DELeGG), but the doc comment, the spec's Verified facts row and the grandfather count (46) are wrong.

**Failure.** An operator following the runbook would try to seed 46 ledgers and hunt for ER-forged balances that are other projects' accounts; S5-01's severity was argued from evidence that is void (its structural claim stands on its own).

**Fix.** Correct seed_credit_ledger.rs:22-23 and remediate.md:331/950 to 'no Slipstream credit carries an out-of-range value; 42 delegated + 1 program-owned credits need seeding'; any tooling that enumerates delegated credits must filter delegation records by owner program == 7qujfsb4 (or derive the credit PDA) before decoding.


### [medium/repo] Off-chain error tables are three names short and give no action for the four lock-state errors a user can actually hit

`frontend/src/lib/slipstream/errors.ts:36`  · prior: S8-C7

**Evidence.** error.rs:64-69 appends SelfTrade, PositionStillOpen, CreditCeilingExceeded (0x138), FillSequenceOutOfRange (0x139), LegacyLayoutRejected (0x13A); errors.ts:24-37 ERR_NAMES and keepers/src/shared/ertx.ts:21-33 both end at PositionStillOpen (56 entries), so decodeProgramError (errors.ts:87-88) renders the three as 'custom 0x138' etc. ERR_ADVICE (errors.ts:40-61) has no entry for ReservedMarginExists (withdraw_collateral.rs:86), CreditStillActive (withdraw_trading_credit.rs:42/51, close_trading_credit.rs:89/95), SameSlotWithdrawal (withdraw_collateral.rs:126) or WithdrawalGateFailed, so they render as 'Rejected on chain: <Name>.' with no next step. The remediate run's own frozen check list did not include the frontend table (docs/checks/remediate has no frontend file).

**Failure.** The first users to hit the new ceiling see an undecoded hex code; users refused by the four existing gates get a bare enum name and no way to know whether to wait, cancel, undelegate, or contact the operator.

**Fix.** Append the three names to errors.ts:36 and ertx.ts:33 (ordinal order, matching error.rs); add ERR_ADVICE lines: CreditCeilingExceeded → 'Your credit ledger is behind — ask the operator to seed it', ReservedMarginExists → 'Withdraw your trading credit first', CreditStillActive → 'Cancel orders / leave the rollup first', SameSlotWithdrawal → 'Retry next block'.


### [low/both] emergency_undelegate stays inoperable on both layers and the OrderBook's 4.363 SOL of rent is unrecoverable even after the callback is implemented

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/emergency_undelegate.rs:25`  · prior: S5-02

**Evidence.** emergency_undelegate.rs:25-36 documents that the ER-only magic CPI (line 136) and the L1-only GlobalState write (lines 140-141) cannot both execute; unchanged on the remediate branch. Live: OrderBook 83zMFL… is DELeGG-owned, 626,736 bytes, 4,362,973,440 lamports, 25 signatures, 0 errors, last activity 2026-05-30, delegation record authority MAS1Dt9q…. The delegation program's undelegate callback restores the account via a single CreateAccount (see critical finding), which is capped at 10,240 bytes per CPI, so the book can never be re-created on L1; frontend/src/content/docs/03-ephemeral-rollups-and-delegation.md:85-102 already states the book is un-undelegatable.

**Failure.** No emergency exit exists for the matching engine, and 4.363 SOL of rent is permanently held by the delegation program; only the operator is affected (no user USDC).

**Fix.** None safe without a design change; document the CreateAccount cap in emergency_undelegate.rs:25-36 and remove the instruction from the incident runbook rather than leave a control that cannot fire.


### [low/deployed] After a stuck undelegation the frontend tells the user to 'press Withdraw again', which the magic program then rejects with a raw ReadonlyDataModified

`frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:1115`  · prior: S8-C7

**Evidence.** waitForUndelegation (use-session.ts:350-366) polls 30×2 s for L1 owner == program, then :1113-1117 sets 'The rollup hasn't handed your funds back yet. Give it a moment and press Withdraw again.' On the retry `delegated` (:1067) is still true, so step 1 re-sends undelegate_trading_credit to the ER; read-only ER simulate on bbUpyr…: 'ScheduleCommit ERR: account … is required to be writable and delegated in order to be undelegated' → InstructionError ReadonlyDataModified, which humanizeError (errors.ts:104-143) cannot map (no custom code) and prints raw. The same ER-copy owner check the hook already does for reads (:291-299) would detect the state: ER owner == DELeGG while L1 owner == DELeGG. keepers/ecosystem.config.js:70-79 describes this state for the mm-v2 fleet, but on chain the mm-v2 keys in the local key files (C5jjceWp…, 3ZpMgw6m…) are ER-adopted with live orders and 0 failed undelegates; the comment is stale.

**Failure.** Ten users (and every future one) are told to keep retrying an action that can never succeed, with no indication their credit is stranded or who to contact.

**Fix.** In withdraw, before step 1: fetch the ER copy; if L1 owner == DELeGG and ER owner == DELeGG, set a terminal notice ('This credit is stuck between layers; the operator must resolve it — your free collateral can still be withdrawn') and skip to step 3 (withdraw_collateral) instead of re-sending the undelegate.

### [high/deployed] A push to factory/remediate is root code execution on the key-holding host: the SSH forced command runs npm ci lifecycle scripts and repo prebuild scripts as root, with no branch protection and no CI on that branch

`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:15`  · prior: S10-01

**Evidence.** deploy.yml:15 forced command `restrict,command="/opt/slipstream/deploy.sh factory/remediate"` for `root@$DEPLOY_HOST` (deploy.yml:62); deploy.yml:25-27 triggers on every push to factory/remediate. Run 33902080916 log: `added 1135 packages in 2m` then `> frontend@0.1.0 prebuild > node scripts/copy-manifest.mjs && node scripts/copy-docs.mjs` then `next build` (executes next.config.ts) then `[PM2] restartProcessId on app [slipstream-web]`, pm2 table shows every slipstream-* process `user: root`. So npm postinstall of any of 1135 packages, or any repo script at the tip of the branch, runs as root on the box that holds KEEPER_KEYPAIR (keepers/src/shared/connection.ts:96-97) and the faucet key (frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:192-203). `gh api .../branches/factory%2Fremediate/protection` -> 404 Branch not protected; rulesets []; 100/100 recent commits unsigned; ci.yml:3-7 runs only on master, last CI run 2026-08-17, 15 deploy runs since 2026-08-27 with zero CI runs. deploy.yml:18 claims 'A leaked secret can trigger a deploy of that one branch and nothing more' - true for the SSH key, not for push access.

**Failure.** One compromised GitHub session (single maintainer account, 2FA on) or one hijacked transitive npm package = root on the host = operator key = program upgrade authority + USDC mint authority + GlobalState admin (all verified A5sV4... live at slot 493385904). On mainnet that is the vault; on devnet it is 725,915.85 test USDC in BTWVG... plus 1.85 SOL.

**Fix.** In deploy.sh: `npm ci --ignore-scripts && npm rebuild better-sqlite3` (only native dep that needs its install script), and run the npm/next steps via `runuser -u slipstream --` after chowning /opt/slipstream (Dockerfile:36-48 already documents the uid-1000 intent; .env.example:11 already assumes /home/slipstream). On GitHub: enable branch protection on factory/remediate with required status check CI, and add `factory/remediate` to ci.yml:5 `branches`.

`keepers/ecosystem.config.js:21`  · prior: S10-01

DNS: slipstream.ansht.tech, pay.ansht.tech, onyx.ansht.tech all -> 139.59.45.112. slipstream-web is the Next 16.2.4 server (22 open advisories, npm audit 2026-09-05) that loads the key via OPERATOR_KEYPAIR/KEEPER_KEYPAIR (faucet/route.ts:192-203). ecosystem.config.js:21-53 sets no `uid`/`user`; keepers/Dockerfile:36-48's non-root design is not what production runs. S10-01 enumerated Vercel+VM+laptop; production is not on Vercel (frontend/vercel.json is dead config) and the co-tenants were not known.

### [high/both] Public faucet can empty the fleet's fee payer in ~36 minutes from one IP: operator holds 1.85 SOL, cap is 60 drips/hour at 0.052 SOL each

`frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:83`  · prior: S8-02

S8-02 measured 0.052054 SOL per first-time drip. 60 x 0.052 = 3.12 SOL/hour > 1.85 SOL balance -> empty in ~36 min. Every L1 keeper signs with this key (connection.ts:96-120) and every fee/rent comes from it; pm2 restarts with exp_backoff (ecosystem.config.js:47-49) will loop on insufficient-funds. Mint supply grew 1,253,723 -> 1,357,723 USDC since 2026-08-21 (104 faucet drips of 10k), so the faucet is in real use. No compromise needed.

**Failure.** Anyone with curl takes down liquidation, funding, TWAP, expiry, both bots and the faucet itself for as long as the operator wallet stays empty; positions stop being liquidated and marked.

**Fix.** Before the SOL transfer at route.ts:333, `const opBal = await connection.getBalance(operator.publicKey); if (opBal < RESERVE_LAMPORTS) skip the top-up` with RESERVE defaulting to 1 SOL, and lower FAUCET_MAX_PER_HOUR default at :83 to 20. Two lines, no key change.


### [high/both] Key concentration, code half: A5sV4... is loaded from eight places, three of them on one root-owned host, and the repo branch adds three more admin-gated capabilities to what it can do

`deploy.json:5`  · prior: S10-01

**Evidence.** Live 2026-09-05 slot 493385904: ProgramData 6BBrTY... authority = A5sV4; mint Fakb9g... mintAuthority = A5sV4, freezeAuthority null, supply 1,357,723.325954; GlobalState hyPv4s... offset 8 authority = A5sV4, offset 40 treasury = A5sV4, len 104; vault 725,915.845954 USDC; operator 1.850340758 SOL, last tx 2026-09-05T05:17:09Z. Loaders: (1) keepers/src/shared/connection.ts:96-97 KEEPER_KEYPAIR || ~/.config/solana/id.json, used by 7 pm2 apps (ecosystem.config.js:58-104) as root; (2) frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:192-203 OPERATOR_KEYPAIR inline-JSON-or-path || KEEPER_KEYPAIR, in slipstream-web as root; (3) docker-compose.yml:27-29 x6 mounts; (4) Anchor.toml:15 and tests/integration/setup.ts:38 + *.test.ts AUTHORITY_KEYPAIR default ~/.config/solana/id.json on the laptop; (5) deploy.json:5 (public); (6) GitHub push -> deploy.sh as root (finding 1); (7) co-tenants onyx/paypi (finding 2); (8) inline JSON form at route.ts:194 means `pm2 env`/`~/.pm2/dump.pm2` hold the raw secret if that form is used. Repo adds to the yield: set_keeper.rs (0x2A), seed_credit_ledger.rs (0x28, a standing ceiling bypass per docs/spec/remediate.md:17-21), reset_pending_fills.rs (0x29). Only the fill-log keeper needs the admin key (S10-01 table) and it is the one process that is stopped.

**Failure.** Any one of eight residencies falling yields program replacement, unbounded collateral, oracle repointing and, after redeploy, ledger rewrites; six of the seven running signers hold a key they do not need.

**Fix.** Non-key-ops steps only: point the six non-fill-log pm2 apps at a separate funded fee-payer file via per-app `env: { KEEPER_KEYPAIR: ... }` in ecosystem.config.js (the program checks no signer identity for them, S10-01 table), make faucet/route.ts:194 refuse the inline-array form so the secret can only be a 600 file, and leave the ceremonies in remediate.md decision 6 to the human.


### [medium/deployed] Deploy is in-place on the serving box with no lock: npm ci wipes node_modules under the running server, next build rewrites .next in place, and the only type gate runs after the wipe (two failed deploys on 2026-09-04 prove it)

`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:32`

**Evidence.** Run 33894214951 (5b9fcbc, 16:16Z): `added 1135 packages in 3m` (npm ci deletes node_modules first) -> `next build` -> `Compiled successfully in 78s` -> `Failed to type check. ./src/app/api/status/route.ts:94:60 Cannot find name 'KeeperStatus'` -> exit 1, no pm2 restart; run 33895208233 (fb2656a, 16:27Z) failed the same way; site not healthy again until 33902080916 at 17:49Z. Successful deploy = 5m41s with `host metrics | cpu: 100%` on the same box that runs liquidation/twap. deploy.yml:32-34 serializes only GitHub-triggered runs; a manual run of /opt/slipstream/deploy.sh on the box is unserialized (the orchestrator reports two concurrent runs wiped node_modules mid-npm-ci today). deploy.sh is not checked in anywhere (`git ls-files | grep deploy.sh` empty; only reference is deploy.yml:15). deploy.yml:39 `timeout-minutes: 20` kills the SSH session -> SIGHUP to deploy.sh mid-npm-ci on a slow day.

**Failure.** Every deploy is a 5-6 minute window in which cold routes 500 (MODULE_NOT_FOUND) and open tabs lose chunks; a type error pushed to the branch leaves the box with fresh node_modules, a half-written .next and the old server, for as long as it takes a human to notice. Concurrent runs corrupt node_modules outright.

**Fix.** Add at the top of /opt/slipstream/deploy.sh, before the git fetch:
```
exec 9>/run/lock/slipstream-deploy.lock
flock -w 1200 9 || { echo "deploy: another run holds the lock"; exit 75; }
```
(fd 9 is held for the script's lifetime, so the lock releases on any exit). Check the script into the repo as ops/deploy.sh and `cp` it to /opt (not symlink: bash reads the file while `git reset --hard` rewrites it). skipped: build-in-sibling-dir + symlink swap; add when a failed deploy takes the site down again. Move the type gate off the box by adding factory/remediate to ci.yml:5 and gating deploy.yml on `workflow_run` success.


### [medium/both] getMultipleAccounts is uncapped in the RPC proxy: 5 copies of the order book returned 4.18 MB in 2.3 s, so one 20-call batch of 100 keys is ~1.67 GB of paid upstream egress per HTTP request

`frontend/src/app/api/rpc/[layer]/route.ts:226`

**Evidence.** route.ts:224-253 allowlists `getMultipleAccounts` with no params check; :293 allows batches of 20; :222 body cap 100 KB fits 100 pubkeys x 20 calls. Measured 2026-09-05: POST /api/rpc/er getMultipleAccounts with 5x 83zMFL6cHjgXkQ7KRNcgtHaZ1fhyNgxhM8aMpPpEnMqe (encoding base64, no dataSlice) -> status=200 bytes=4,179,149 time=2.29s. Linear: 100 keys = ~83.6 MB per call, x20 = ~1.67 GB per request, plus the gzip at :146-157 burns CPU on the same box as the keepers. Frontend callers use at most 4 keys (use-session.ts:232, use-triggers.ts:34). Confirmed by the orchestrator as unfixed; re-reported with a fix as invited.

**Failure.** An unauthenticated caller relays the deployer's paid quota away (the exact mechanism of the 2026-09-01 outage) and pegs the keeper host's CPU; /api/status showed `base.degraded: true` at 2026-09-05T06:41Z, i.e. the key is still exhausted now.

**Fix.** In methodsAllowed (route.ts:294), one extra clause: `if (c.method === "getMultipleAccounts" && (!Array.isArray(c.params?.[0]) || c.params[0].length > 8)) return false;` and cap batches containing it at 1 (`calls.length === 1`). skipped: per-key byte accounting; add if a legitimate caller ever needs more than 8.


### [medium/deployed] Keeper code is never deployed by the pipeline: deploy.sh restarts slipstream-web only, so the fleet runs whatever version each process happened to start with (8 days, 115 min, 96 min, 0 s at the same moment)

`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:22`

**Evidence.** deploy.yml:22-23 'Frontend only. The keepers ... are deliberately left running'. Run 33902080916 pm2 table at 17:49Z: expiry uptime 8D (started ~2026-08-27, before ff58bac/f83d148/faa6145), liquidation/market-maker/taker 115m, funding/twap 96m, web 0s; fill-log stopped. `git reset` on the box updates keepers/src on disk, so pm2's autorestart (ecosystem.config.js:28,48) loads NEW code on the next crash while siblings keep OLD code - the version each keeper runs is whichever restart happened last. slipstream-web is in no ecosystem file (ecosystem.config.js:57-105 lists 7 apps), so its pm2 config exists only on the box. Also ecosystem.config.js:26 `--env-file` means a keepers/.env change is likewise only picked up per-process.

**Failure.** A keeper fix (e.g. the RPC failover in keepers/src/shared/connection.ts:1-100) is live on some keepers and not others with nothing recording which; a shared-module change (ertx.ts, connection.ts) that is incompatible across versions can split the fleet silently. Reproducing the box from the repo is impossible for the web app.

**Fix.** Add `keeper("web", ...)` (script `node_modules/.bin/next start`, cwd frontend) to ecosystem.config.js so the whole box is one file, and have deploy.sh end with `pm2 restart ecosystem.config.js --only $(changed apps)` or simply `pm2 restart all` after a keepers `npm ci`; print `git rev-parse HEAD` into each keeper's first log line so `pm2 logs` names the version.


### [medium/repo] The remediation cannot be operated from anything checked in: no client builds 0x28/0x29/0x2A, no program-deploy runbook exists, and deploy.json's deployedAt is 2.5 months behind the last upgrade

`deploy.json:17`  · prior: S10-02

**Evidence.** instructions/mod.rs:108-110 define IX_SEED_CREDIT_LEDGER 0x28, IX_RESET_PENDING_FILLS 0x29, IX_SET_KEEPER 0x2A; `grep -ri seed_credit|resetPending|setKeeper` over client/src, frontend/src/lib/slipstream, keepers/src, tests/integration returns nothing but the PDA seed constant. docs/spec/remediate.md:572 requires 46 seed_credit_ledger calls and :543 a set_keeper before the fleet works; no runbook or script carries them. Only deploy procedure in the tree is README.md:152 `cargo build-sbf` (no `solana program deploy` anywhere; S10-02's scripts/deploy.ts still absent). Live ProgramData 6BBrTY... last write slot 484488785 = 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z (sig 3UtrgCoq..., matches deploy.json:18) while deploy.json:17 says deployedAt 2026-05-30; no build hash recorded, so nothing ties the 205,909-byte live account to any commit (master's last programs/ change was 8914e31 2026-07-27).

**Failure.** When the operator finally redeploys, the twelve P0 fixes land with no way to run the migration that makes R1/R7 usable; the deploy itself is reconstructed by hand from instruction sources, and afterwards nobody can prove which source is live.

**Fix.** Add the three builders to client/src/instructions.ts (they are 1-byte discriminator + u64/pubkey), a `keepers/src/migrate-remediate.ts` that runs set_keeper then seed_credit_ledger over the 46 credits, and record `sha256(target/deploy/slipstream.so)` + commit into deploy.json at deploy time. Not a deploy proposal - the tooling only.


### [low/deployed] The RPC key lives in three files under two variable names, and Next loads .env.local ABOVE .env.production on the box, so a rotation applied to .env.production silently does not take effect

`keepers/.env.example:5`

**Evidence.** Deploy logs (runs 33894214951, 33902080916): `- Environments: .env.local, .env.production` - both exist in /opt/slipstream/frontend. Next's precedence is .env.local > .env.production for the same key. Frontend reads BASE_RPC_UPSTREAM (rpc/[layer]/route.ts:29, status/route.ts:26, faucet/route.ts:55, settlement-status.tsx:15); keepers read BASE_RPC (keepers/src/shared/connection.ts:7, .env.example:5). Local frontend/.env.local carries the same Helius URL. /api/status at 2026-09-05T06:41Z: `base.degraded: true` - the key is still exhausted a day after the outage, and the three-file layout is why the operator could not tell which value was live.

**Failure.** Rotating or replacing the key means editing three files with two names; missing one (or editing .env.production while .env.local shadows it) leaves a dead key in service with the failover masking it.

**Fix.** Delete frontend/.env.local on the box, keep one frontend/.env.production, and have the keepers read the same name: in connection.ts:7 `process.env.BASE_RPC || process.env.BASE_RPC_UPSTREAM || PUBLIC_DEVNET`. Document BASE_RPC_UPSTREAM as the only place the key lives.


### [low/both] Launch-surface drift S10-X04 predicted has happened: pm2 runs mm-v3, docker-compose and .env.example still say mm-v2, and the Docker image default still starts the impossible settlement keeper

`keepers/ecosystem.config.js:79`  · prior: S10-X04

**Evidence.** ecosystem.config.js:79 `BOT_MM_PREFIX: "mm-v3"` (comment :74-78: mm-v2 credits went the same way as mm-v1); docker-compose.yml:111 `BOT_MM_PREFIX: mm-v2`; keepers/.env.example:43 `# BOT_MM_PREFIX=mm-v2`. keepers/Dockerfile:55 `CMD ["npm","run","all"]` -> keepers/package.json:14 still `settlement + funding + liquidation + twap + expiry`, no fill-log (S10-03 open). docker-compose.yml:110 says the stale prefix makes 'every bot order silently fail'.

**Failure.** Anyone standing the stack up the documented Docker way gets bots on the deadlocked mm-v2 wallets and a settlement keeper the docs call impossible; the third rotation (v3) was applied to one of three copies.

**Fix.** Put BOT_MM_PREFIX/BOT_TAKER_PREFIX in keepers/.env (both launchers already load it), delete the copies at ecosystem.config.js:79,99 and docker-compose.yml:111-112, and change package.json:14 `npm:settlement` -> `npm:fill-log-keeper`.


### [low/repo] Keeper Docker image still builds with npm install and still bakes keepers/.verify-keys if present (S10-04, S10-05 unchanged)

`keepers/Dockerfile:24`  · prior: S10-05

**Evidence.** keepers/Dockerfile:24 `RUN cd client && npm install`, :26 `RUN cd keepers && npm install`, :23/:25 `package-lock.json*` glob tolerates a missing lock; .dockerignore:22-23 excludes keepers/.bot-keys and keepers/data only, keepers/.gitignore:7 knows .verify-keys. Neither directory exists in this checkout (`ls keepers/.verify-keys` -> ENOENT) so no image built here would leak, but the pattern gap is unchanged. Production does not use the image (pm2 on bare host), which is why this stays low.

**Failure.** An image built on the operator laptop after verify-session/verify-close ships funded devnet keypairs in a layer; the tree it runs is not the tree CI checked.

**Fix.** .dockerignore: add `keepers/.verify-keys`; Dockerfile:24,26 `npm install` -> `npm ci`, drop the `*` on :23,:25.

### [low/repo] ci.yml is still unpinned and now provably gates nothing that ships; the GITHUB_TOKEN default S10-07 could not read is confirmed read-only

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:5`  · prior: S10-07

**Evidence.** ci.yml:5-7 `branches: [master]` for both push and pull_request; `gh run list` shows the last CI run 2026-08-17 on master and 15 'Deploy frontend' runs on factory/remediate since 2026-08-27 with no CI run on that branch; `git log --oneline master..HEAD | wc -l` = 51, including ~1,000 changed lines under programs/ (diff --stat) that CI's clippy/cargo-audit/mollusk job has never executed on GitHub. ci.yml:14,16,20,24,79,80,103,104 still mutable tags; :54 `cargo install cargo-audit --locked` unversioned. `gh api repos/.../actions/permissions/workflow` -> default_workflow_permissions: read (S10-07 left this unrecorded); allowed_actions: all; 100/100 recent commits unsigned.

**Failure.** The branch production runs has no automated gate at all; the pinning risk is bounded (read-only token, deploy.yml uses no third-party actions) but the cache poisoning path in S10-07 stands.

**Fix.** ci.yml:5,7 -> `branches: [master, factory/remediate]`; add `permissions: {contents: read}` explicitly; pin the six action refs to SHAs; `cargo install cargo-audit --version 0.21.x --locked`.


### [low/repo] deploy.yml trusts whatever host key answers on each run while its comment says it pins one

`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:55`

**Evidence.** deploy.yml:54-55 `# Pin the host key rather than accepting blindly on every run.` followed by `ssh-keyscan -H "$DEPLOY_HOST" >> ~/.ssh/known_hosts` - which fetches a fresh key from the network on every run, i.e. trust-on-first-use, every time. DEPLOY_HOST is not secret in practice (slipstream.ansht.tech -> 139.59.45.112, faucet/route.ts:97 says the hostname resolves straight to the droplet).

**Failure.** A network-position attacker can impersonate the box, absorb the deploy trigger and return 'deployed ok'; the smoke test then passes against the untouched real site, so a deploy is silently skipped. Public-key auth means no credential is lost.

**Fix.** Store the box's `ssh-keyscan` line once as a repo secret/variable DEPLOY_HOST_KEY and `printf '%s\n' "$DEPLOY_HOST_KEY" > ~/.ssh/known_hosts` instead of scanning.


### [low/repo] Operator docs disagree with the box: README says nginx and no OPERATOR_KEYPAIR, the faucet says Caddy and names a FAUCET_KEYPAIR variable that does not exist

`frontend/src/app/api/faucet/route.ts:200`

**Evidence.** faucet/route.ts:197-201 comment: 'Opt in explicitly via FAUCET_KEYPAIR or KEEPER_KEYPAIR' - the code at :192-203 reads OPERATOR_KEYPAIR, KEEPER_KEYPAIR and FAUCET_ALLOW_DEFAULT_KEYPAIR; `grep -rn FAUCET_KEYPAIR` matches only that comment. frontend/README.md:55-57 'under pm2 behind nginx'; faucet/route.ts:95-97 'Caddy is the only hop'; live `curl -sI https://slipstream.ansht.tech/` -> `via: 1.1 Caddy`, `x-powered-by: Next.js`, no CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options (S10-X03 still open). frontend/README.md:36-46 env table omits OPERATOR_KEYPAIR entirely; README.md:163 is the only mention of KEEPER_KEYPAIR.

**Failure.** The variable that puts the mint authority into a public web process is undocumented, and the one the comment tells the operator to set does nothing.

**Fix.** Fix the comment at :200 to OPERATOR_KEYPAIR; add OPERATOR_KEYPAIR and the clientIp() Caddy assumption to frontend/README.md's table; `poweredByHeader: false` in next.config.ts.

### [critical/both] Funding premium is TWAP-of-oracle minus oracle spot (pure momentum, never the book); the ring now spans ~5.6h so the R6 clamp binds 41% of intervals, and the deployed unclamped index (-10.38) has $69,149 of phantom funding claimable today that R6 does not rewind

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/compute_funding.rs:74`  · prior: S3-01

**Evidence.** compute_funding.rs:74-78 comment says 'Mark price = local TWAP from book midprice samples' but the ring is only ever fed by crank_twap.rs:71 with the Pyth reading (price = reading.price, :48); no book price ever enters twap_prices (grep push_twap_price: crank_twap.rs:71 only). Index = the same Pyth feed's spot (compute_funding.rs:81-91). Prior S3-02 assumed the ring was book midprice - it is not. Live ring decode (twap_count=225, write_index=55): 64 distinct prices in runs of 3-4 identical samples (avg 3.52 duplicates/publish, because twap-keeper.ts:9 cranks every 8s inside each 45s window), i.e. the '30-min TWAP' (market.rs:165, crank_twap.rs:13 says 5-min) spans ~64 publishes x 314s = 5.6h. Coinbase 5.5h-mean vs spot, hourly samples over 7 days: median 0.354%, p90 1.17%, max 2.86%, >0.5% (MAX_FUNDING_RATE_PER_INTERVAL, funding.rs:18) in 41.2% of samples; sign follows oracle momentum (rising market -> TWAP<spot -> negative rate -> shorts pay longs). Deployed (no clamp, program source = 8914e31): live cumulative_funding_index = -10379622647083427790 (-10.38 = -1038%), moved from -9.12 at the Aug-21 audit; funding_interval=28800, last_funding_ts=1788559400. Replaying compute_funding_payment (funding.rs:66-100) over the 15 open positions at mark 101.877385: longs are owed $69,149.63 total (HRTNWjSq... size 23.6 SOL, collateral $2,092, owed $24,796; FdVp3mi9... owed $17,237; 5keWTGsy... owed $13,659; 7fW1hjit... owed $10,717), shorts owe $1,011. claim_funding.rs:97-102 credits the negative payment to free_collateral unconditionally; vault BTWVG5oD... holds 725,915.85 USDC. docs/spec/remediate.md:511-513 states the clamp 'only bounds future increments ... no already-accrued value is rewritten'.

**Failure.** Deployed now: any of the 12 long holders calls claim_funding then withdraw_collateral and takes up to $69,149 (9.5% of the vault) that no counterparty ever paid; the largest single claim is 11.9x that position's collateral. Repo after deploy: the -10.38 index still pays out; and going forward funding is +/-0.5% of notional per 8h (1.5%/day) in 41% of intervals with a sign set by oracle drift, never by book premium - longs are paid to be long in an uptrend.

**Fix.** Repo (NEEDS-DEPLOY): source the funding mark from a book-derived price (best bid/ask mid or fill VWAP committed via the FillLog) or, minimally, from the SAME fresh oracle read as the index so the premium is 0 and only INTEREST_RATE_PER_INTERVAL accrues; fix the compute_funding.rs:74 comment. For the accrued -10.38: the redeploy must be paired with a one-time authority-gated snapshot migration (set every open Position.funding_index_snapshot to the current index) or an index reset - R6 alone leaves the $69k live.

**Verifier verdicts (partial):**
- `?` → stands (severity→critical) (target→both): NOT a bare restatement; every leg carries new evidence and target "both" / severity critical stand.

Leg 1 (ring is oracle-only) — NEW. `grep push_twap_price` across programs/slipstream/src hits exactly one caller, crank_twap.rs:71, fed with `reading.price` from parse_pyth (:41,:48). No book price ever enters `twap_prices`. compute_funding.rs:74 ("local TWAP from book midprice samples") is false, and prior S3-02:194 built its blast-radius on the same false premise ("lagging average of the *local book* midprice"). Nothing in s1–s13 identifies the ring as oracle-only. Real design defect in both repo and deployed: premium = TWAP(oracle) − spot(oracle), i.e. oracle momentum, never book premium.

Leg 2 (clamp binds ~40% of intervals) — NEW, independently reproduced. Live ring decode: twap_count=225, 64 distinct prices, avg run 3.52 (matches claim; write_index has moved 55→90 since). Independent Coinbase SOL-USD hourly, 7 days, 6h trailing mean vs spot: median 0.443%, p90 1.61%, max 3.77%, >0.5% in 45.1% of samples (claim: 41.2% at 5.5h — same conclusion, window/timing drift). This contradicts funding.rs:16-17 ("ordinary funding never touches the clamp") and remediate.md open decision 5. Repo defect.

Leg 3 ($69k phantom credit, R6 does not rewind) — restates S3-01's second ("latched") leg but with material new evidence: (a) live Market read (slot 493391924): cumulative_funding_index = -10379622647083427790 exactly as claimed, last_funding_ts=1788559400, interval=28800, mark_minute age = 0 min → `mark_price_for_close` returns Some, so S3-01's latch is OFF (S3-01 reported it latched at 16 days stale). (b) Replaying compute_funding_payment over the 15 live positions at last_mark_price 101.821777: longs owed $69,111.89 (claim $69,149.63 at mark 101.877385 — same figure, mark drift), shorts owe $1,011.21, largest HRTNWjSq $24,782.77 = 11.8x its $2,092 collateral, 9.52% of the 725,915.85 USDC vault. (c) claim_funding at 8914e31 gates only on mark_price_for_close (:62) and withdraw_collateral.rs:86 gates only on free_collateral. (d) Two positions carry snapshot -10.6686 vs current -10.3796: the index swung >1.5 (>150% of notional) since Aug 21, impossible under R6's ±1.5%/call bound, and `git show 8914e31:math/funding.rs` has no clamp — so the deployed program is confirmed unclamped. (e) remediate.md:511-513 and docs/checks/remediate/R6.md:77-78 explicitly leave accrued value unrewritten and the repo ships no snapshot migration; R6 closes S3-01's rate-multiplication leg only. That gap is a repo finding, not covered by the 12 P0s.

Target: "both" is correct — deployed: $69k claimable now by any of 10 long holders (funds loss reachable now → critical); repo: redeploying R6 alone still pays the same $69k, plus the oracle-momentum funding design persists. Not among the items established today. Cite as: extends S3-01 (second leg, now unlatched), corrects S3-02:194, overlaps S3-05 (unconditional credit) — all with new evidence.
- `?` → stands (severity→critical) (target→both): No guard exists anywhere on the path; the claim survives with live confirmation.

Mark source: `push_twap_price` has exactly one caller, `crank_twap.rs:71`, and the value pushed is `reading.price` from `parse_pyth` (`crank_twap.rs:41,48`). `compute_funding.rs:77` reads `get_twap()` of that ring as "mark", and `:83` reads the same Pyth feed via `apply_dual_oracle` as "index". No book price ever enters the ring; the `:74` comment is wrong. Same at the deployed commit 8914e31 (`compute_funding.rs:48-52` there).

Accrued index, live read of ECUp8p... (public devnet, 2026-09-05): `cumulative_funding_index = -10379622647083427790` (-10.38), `last_funding_ts=1788559400`, `funding_interval_secs=28800`, `twap_count=225`, 64 distinct prices in the ring, `last_mark_price=101.821777`, mark minute stamp == current minute (fresh, so `mark_price_for_close` returns Some and the S3-01 "latched" long-side credit is now unlatched). The drift from the Aug-21 projection of -9.12 to -10.38 over ~15 days is -1.26, which exceeds the R6 bound of 45 intervals x 0.5% = 0.225; that alone proves the live bytecode is unclamped, independent of commit identity.

Replay of `compute_funding_payment` (`funding.rs:66-100`) over the 15 live non-empty Position accounts (dataSize 96, disc 4): 10 longs are owed $69,112 total (HRTNWjSq $24,783 on $2,092 collateral; FdVp3mi9 $17,228; 5keWTGsy $13,652; 7fW1hjit $10,711; rest <$1k); shorts owe $1,011; the two longs with snapshot -10.67 (5YZyYzEm, 665D6fPZ) pay ~$100/$488, so "12 longs" in the claim is 10 receivers. Vault BTWVG5oD... = 725,915.85 USDC. Figures match the claim within mark drift.

Guards checked and absent: `claim_funding.rs` checks only owner signer, market PDA, market_index, and mark freshness (`:28-63`); no pause, restricted, breaker, or solvency check (grep for `ensure_not_globally_paused|is_restricted|circuit_breaker` in claim_funding.rs, compute_funding.rs, withdraw_collateral.rs returns nothing). `withdraw_collateral.rs:77-91` gates only on pending_fills==0, reserved_margin==0, free_collateral>=amount; no vault-solvency or deposit cap. Positions and UserAccounts are program-owned on L1 (not delegated; `user_account.rs:19` says never delegated), so no ER indirection blocks the base-layer call. Worse than the claim states: `close_position.rs:163-168,190-191,227-231` also credits the accrued funding into free_collateral on a normal UI close, so no hand-built claim_funding tx is needed. New positions cannot join the payout: `settle_trades.rs:385` snapshots at the current index on open, so the claimant set is exactly the pre-existing long holders. Of the $69.1k, ~$39.8k is withdrawable this block (owners of HRTNWjSq, 5keWTGsy, 9UPbaPkC, 6kPyPN1e, E3TuvjDX all have pending_fills=0, reserved_margin=0); the remaining ~$29.3k (FdVp3mi9 pending=1, 7fW1hjit pending=7, BHKAyZHe, DZWtMexV, JDfG3Ay7) is credited to free_collateral but held at withdraw Gate 1 until settlement clears their pending fills.

Repo does not rewind it: the only writers of the index are `compute_funding.rs:106` (add) and `initialize_market.rs:182` (which refuses a non-empty account at `:112`); no instruction resets snapshots; `docs/spec/remediate.md:511-513` states the clamp "only bounds future increments ... no already-accrued value is rewritten". So a redeploy of HEAD leaves the $69k live, and the forward funding premium is still TWAP-of-Pyth minus Pyth. The 41%-of-intervals clamp-binding statistic is plausible from SOL vol but was not independently reproduced here; it is supporting colour, not load-bearing. Prior S3-01 documented the latched credit at $58.7k-$72.3k pending a crank restart; new evidence here is that the crank is running, the mark is fresh, the index has grown, and the payout is reachable via close_position as well as claim_funding. Severity critical and target both stand as filed.


### [high/both] Liquidation is oracle-blind ~80% of the time while close_position/execute_trigger keep settling at a mark up to ~5 min stale

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:96`  · prior: S3-03

**Evidence.** liquidate_position.rs:96 -> apply_dual_oracle -> oracle.rs:262-264 requires the Pyth reading itself fresh (oracle.rs:22 MAX_STALENESS_SECS=60, is_fresh oracle.rs:49-52). close_position.rs:121-125, execute_trigger.rs:80-83 and (repo only) place_order.rs:174-176 instead use mark_price_for_close (market.rs:173-183) whose window is 30 MINUTES (market.rs:16) and whose stamp is the CRANK minute, not the publish time (crank_twap.rs:75-76). Measured 2026-09-04 20:23-20:31Z: publish gaps 315s, 314s; on-chain post lands 5-13s after publish_time; age<=60s in 30/126 samples (24%; theoretical 60/315=19%); max age seen 324s; cranks land 9-41s after each publish (7 cranks in 444s). So in steady state the mark close settles against is the previous publish, 20s-330s old, while liquidate_position is rejected with OracleStale for ~80% of every cycle. Ring of the last 58 consecutive publishes (decoded from Market.twap_prices, chronological): |publish-to-publish move| median 6.1 bps, mean 11.2, p90 19.7, max 172.6 bps. Insurance fund = 0 (Market offset 192). Program logic identical deployed vs HEAD (oracle.rs/crank_twap.rs/market.rs/close_position.rs/execute_trigger.rs unchanged since deploy commit db8f77d; on-chain last upgrade slot 484488785 = 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z). S3-03 described the reverse asymmetry (exit blocked, liquidation live) under a dead crank; under today's cadence it inverts.

**Failure.** A trader watching a live SOL feed sees the real price move against them by up to the p90 19.7 bps (max 172.6 bps observed) after the last on-chain publish and closes at the pre-move mark via close_position (or lets an SL fire at it); at 20x that is 3.9%-34.5% of margin transferred from the counterparty (the protocol's own MM wallets) with nothing in the insurance fund. Conversely a position that goes underwater right after a publish cannot be liquidated for up to ~5 min while its owner can still exit at the stale, better mark.

**Fix.** No-deploy path first: fix the cadence, not the gate — have the TWAP keeper post fresh Pyth updates itself (receiver post_update from Hermes into a keeper-owned PriceUpdateV2, then rotate the market to it with the already-deployed set_market_oracle 0x25), so publish age stays under 60s and both gates see the same price. Program path (NEEDS-DEPLOY): store the reading's publish_ts beside last_mark_price and have mark_price_for_close reject a mark whose PUBLISH age exceeds a bound shared with liquidation, so exit and seizure use one freshness rule (S3-03's 'freeze both').


### [high/both] The funding 'premium' is the same Pyth feed at two lags: the mark ring is not a book mid, so funding is an inverted momentum subsidy

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/compute_funding.rs:74`  · prior: S3-02

**Evidence.** compute_funding.rs:74 comment: 'Mark price = local TWAP from book midprice samples'; :75-78 reads market.get_twap(). But the only writer of twap_prices is crank_twap.rs:71 (grep push_twap_price: market.rs:114-121 + crank_twap.rs:71 only), which pushes the PYTH price from parse_pyth (crank_twap.rs:41,48). Index at :81-91 is the same Pyth account. So premium_rate (funding.rs:44-48) = (lagged Pyth - current Pyth)/current Pyth: a pure drift term with no market content. Under today's cadence the keeper cranks 3-4 times per publish with the identical price (twap-keeper.ts:9 8s loop, :34-36 45s send window), so the 225-slot ring = 59 distinct publishes ≈ 5.06 h of history (measured), not the '5-min' (crank_twap.rs:13) or 30-min (market.rs:14-15) window the code claims. Live: get_twap 101,111,062-101,137,194 vs index 101,768,072/101,657,197 -> premium -65/-52 bps; oldest-vs-newest ring drift +197 bps. Repo clamps to +/-0.5%/interval (funding.rs:18,54; R6) with funding_interval_secs=28800 live, so up to 1.5%/day of notional moves between sides on the oracle's own past; OI is 85.8 SOL long vs 0.4 SOL short, so the receiving side is paid by nobody (S3-05). Deployed program has NO clamp (git diff db8f77d HEAD -- math/funding.rs) and is still accruing: cumulative_funding_index now -10.3797 (S3-01 measured -9.12 on 2026-08-21), 15 open positions carry $69,001.00 receivable vs $1,009.33 payable (largest: 23.6 SOL long owed $24,743), vault 725,915.85 USDC. S3-02's premise ('30-minute lagging average of the local book midprice') is factually wrong: no book price ever enters the ring.

**Failure.** In an uptrend the lagged ring sits below spot, the rate goes negative, and longs are PAID for being long (real perp funding does the opposite); every 8h interval up to 0.5% (repo) / unbounded (deployed) of notional is credited to one side with no paying counterparty, drawn from the shared vault via claim_funding. User-visible wrong money on every funding claim.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. Until a real book mid exists on L1, drop the premium term: compute_funding_rate returns INTEREST_RATE_PER_INTERVAL only (one-line change in funding.rs:50-54) and delete the get_twap() read at compute_funding.rs:75-78. Separately correct the three cadence comments (crank_twap.rs:13, market.rs:14-15, compute_funding.rs:74).


### [high/both] Owner exits settle against a Pyth print that is 0-314s old by design while liquidation needs <=60s: the stale mark is a repeatable free option paid from the pooled vault

`programs/slipstream/src/state/market.rs:168`  · prior: S3-03

**Evidence.** close_position/execute_trigger/claim_funding settle at market.mark_price_for_close(now) (close_position.rs:122-125, execute_trigger.rs:80-83, claim_funding.rs:60-63), which returns last_mark_price whenever the crank stamp is <=30 min old (market.rs:168, :306-335). last_mark_price is only ever written by crank_twap (crank_twap.rs:72), which the program gates on the ORACLE publish age <=60s (crank_twap.rs:45, oracle.rs:22,49-52); the keeper cranks only while age<=45s (twap-keeper.ts:34-36,66). Measured 2026-09-05 04:52-05:05 UTC: the pinned feed 7UVimff... published at 1788583781, 1788584095, 1788584409, 1788584722 -> gaps 314/314/313s, so the on-chain mark is the LAST publish and is 0-314s stale 100% of the time; consecutive publish-to-publish moves were +0.089%, -0.101%, -0.131%. Coinbase SOL-USD 1-min candles, last 170h (10,195 samples): |5-min return| median 0.088%, mean 0.122%, p90 0.256%, p99 0.572%, max 2.907%; >0.1% in 44.9% of windows. Round-trip cost is 3 bps half-spread (market-maker-bot.ts:53) + 6 bps taker fee (live Market.taker_fee_bps=6); do_close charges no fee. The MM quotes around the SAME stale on-chain print (market-maker-bot.ts:284, readPythPrice, no age check; REFRESH_BPS=6 so quotes never move between publishes). PnL on close is credited to free_collateral straight from the vault with no counterparty (close_position.rs:229-235); the two sides of a trade may close at different marks, so vault inflow != outflow. Prior S3-03 found the asymmetry in the other direction (crank dead, closes blocked); on this branch and live it is inverted: closes always work at the stale print, liquidation only 19.1% of the time.

**Failure.** An off-chain-informed trader (Hermes/Binance) buys the MM's stale ask when spot has moved >0.09% above the on-chain print (45% of every 5-min window), waits <=314s for the next publish, and close_position pays the move minus 9 bps out of the vault; the MM bot's short bleeds the same amount later. Conversely a trader underwater at spot but not at the stale print exits at the print, and the loss is never collected. At 20x, mean |move| 0.122% is 2.4% of margin per cycle, p99 is 11.4%.

**Fix.** No-deploy: run the keeper as a Pyth pusher into a keeper-owned PriceUpdateV2 account (receiver post_update with FULL verification, since oracle.rs:130 rejects Partial) and rotate to it with set_market_oracle (0x25, already deployed) so publish cadence is ~10s and the window is ~100%. Program-side (NEEDS-DEPLOY): have do_close/execute_trigger/claim_funding take the feed accounts and use the same apply_dual_oracle fresh read liquidate_position uses, so both directions settle on one price.

**Verifier verdicts (partial):**
- `?` → stands (severity→high) (target→both): Not a restatement of any remediated P0: none of R1-R7 touch the mark/oracle path, and `git diff master..HEAD` is empty for market.rs, close_position.rs, crank_twap.rs, oracle.rs, execute_trigger.rs, claim_funding.rs (liquidate_position.rs changed only in the bounty/insurance branch, not the apply_dual_oracle read). Not merely the ESTABLISHED-TODAY cadence fact (~309s publish vs 60s gate) or faa6145: the claim takes those as inputs and derives a new, quantified consequence. It IS an extension of open P1 S3-03 (class S3-C6/S3-C13, docs/audit/audit-e2e/s3.md:203-253) with genuinely new evidence: S3-03 documented "liquidation live, owner exits hard-blocked" during a dead crank; with publish-aware cranking the direction inverts — closes always work at a 0-314s-stale print while liquidation is only possible ~19% of the time — and the claim adds the free-option quantification and the MM-bot-quotes-the-same-stale-print half (market-maker-bot.ts:284, REFRESH_BPS=6). Code citations verified on HEAD 87f9eb1: close_position.rs:122-125, execute_trigger.rs:80-83, claim_funding.rs:60-63 all settle at mark_price_for_close (market.rs:173-183, 30-min window via is_mark_price_fresh :154-161); crank_twap.rs:44-47 gates on reading.is_fresh (oracle.rs:22 MAX_STALENESS_SECS=60, :49-52) and :72 is the only writer; twap-keeper.ts:34-36 sends only at age<=45s; liquidate_position.rs:94-103 -> apply_dual_oracle -> dual_oracle_read rejects age>60 (oracle.rs:263-278); close_position.rs:227-231 credits settlement to free_collateral with no counterparty; do_close has no fee line. Live read-only confirmation 2026-09-05 (unix 1788586431): Pyth 7UVimff publish_time 1788586328 (age 103s, >60s so liquidate would return OracleStale), market last_mark_price 101.818731 == that print, stamp 56428 vs now_min 56429 (age 1 min, fresh, so close_position settles at it), taker_fee_bps=6, insurance_fund_balance=$0.00 (so any MM-side deficit is booked as bad debt immediately). Target "both" is correct: the stamp/gate logic is present in the deployed bytecode (S5-C7 verified it live in Aug; stamp populated today) and unchanged in repo. Severity high (= P1, matching S3-03) is correct: exploit needs one precondition (off-chain price access while the MM bot quotes around the stale print, which is the live state), bounded per cycle by 9 bps but repeatable every publish window; not critical (no authz bypass, no outage). Filing note: evidence should say "extends S3-03, inverted direction" — the proposed program-side fix is S3-03's own first remediation option.
- `?` → stands: No guard exists anywhere on the path. Verified on factory/remediate @ 87f9eb1 and against the live program (ProgramData 6BBrTYsX6ioS9Fu9uAPiLPRVDsBxCvy6wYWtzi85WihN last deployed slot 484488785 = 2026-08-16 15:38:54 UTC, i.e. commit e1304e1; `git diff e1304e1 HEAD` on close_position.rs, crank_twap.rs, state/market.rs, oracle.rs, execute_trigger.rs, claim_funding.rs is EMPTY, so the settlement-price logic is byte-identical in repo and deployed).

Callers checked: (1) do_close (close_position.rs:122-125) takes the price from mark_price_for_close(now) (market.rs:173-183) which returns last_mark_price for any stamp <= 30 min (market.rs:154-161, MARK_PRICE_MAX_STALENESS_MINS=30 at :16); no hold-time check, no fee, no oracle account passed. Same at execute_trigger.rs:82-83 and claim_funding.rs:62-63. (2) The only writer is crank_twap.rs:72,76, gated on publish age <= 60s (crank_twap.rs:45, oracle.rs:22,49-52); the stamp records crank time not publish time. (3) The keeper cranks only at age <= 45s (twap-keeper.ts:34-36,66), so between publishes the mark is frozen at the last print. (4) withdraw_collateral.rs:76-88,180-196 pays free_collateral (which do_close credits at close_position.rs:229-235) with no PnL ceiling; the only gates are pending_fills==0, reserved_margin==0, and the same-slot open_slot guard (withdraw_collateral.rs:125), which does not cover a 5-minute cycle. (5) place_order has no oracle band on limit prices (settle_from_log.rs:339 comment) and MARKET orders use the same stale mark (place_order.rs:174-176). (6) liquidate_position.rs:96 uses apply_dual_oracle with the 60s freshness gate, so the asymmetry (closes 100% of the time at a stale print, liquidation only inside the ~60s window) is real. (7) The production MM quotes around the same on-chain PriceUpdateV2 with no age check (market-maker-bot.ts:284 -> shared/pyth.ts readPythPrice) and ecosystem.config.js sets BOT_MM_SPREAD_BPS=2 / REFRESH_BPS=2 (tighter than the claimed 3 bps), and an eaten-down wallet re-quotes immediately at the same stale mid (market-maker-bot.ts:205-208), so the drain is bounded by MM credit, not ladder depth.

Live read 2026-09-05 ~05:34 UTC (api.devnet.solana.com): Market.last_mark_price=101.818731, Pyth 7UVimff price=101.81873196 published 141s earlier, mark stamp 2 min old, taker_fee_bps=6, maker_rebate_bps=1, insurance_fund_balance=0, OI long 85.8 SOL / short 0.4 SOL, 15 non-empty L1 positions. The mark IS the last print and closes would settle at a 141s-old price right now.

Two corrections to the claim's framing, neither refuting it: (a) "paid from the pooled vault" is overstated: the winner's PnL is credited immediately but the loser's (MM's) collateral stays locked in its position, so the vault is net-short only when a loser closes bankrupt (close_position.rs:236-255) with insurance at 0. (b) "exploitable NOW" holds on deployed only for the exit leg (existing positions closing at a stale print). The repeatable open-then-close leg is NOT reachable on the live program today because no new ER fill can become an L1 Position: Market.last_settled_sequence=44189 vs ER next_fill_sequence=53734; the fill-log keeper is stopped; deployed mirror_fills (permissionless) copies ring fills oldest-first into an 80-slot FillLog with no drain, the only fresh delegated epoch (460 of 461) would receive seqs 49638-49717, and initialize/delegate_fill_log are authority-gated (e1304e1 initialize_fill_log.rs:60, delegate_fill_log.rs:98); settle_trades reads the L1 book frozen at next_fill_sequence=10 (S4 P2, restated). That is an outage, not a guard, and it vanishes the moment settlement runs again (which the repo design, R3/R4/R7, is built to do). Minor citation error: market.rs:306-335 is the settlement-cursor code, not the mark logic (154-161, 173-183). Severity high stands under the rubric (exploit needing one precondition = a working settlement pipeline, plus a stale-print exit that is reachable now); target both stands because the code is identical on both.


### [high/both] liquidate_position is only executable in the 60s window after each ~314s publish (19.1% of the time); keeper spends the other 81% sending transactions the program rejects

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:96`  · prior: S3-03

**Evidence.** liquidate_position.rs:96 -> apply_dual_oracle -> dual_oracle_read (oracle.rs:262-265) returns OracleStale unless now-publish_ts in 0..=60 (oracle.rs:49-52). Measured publish gaps 314/314/313s -> fresh fraction 60/314 = 19.1%. Live Market: max_leverage=20 (maintenance = 2.5% of notional, liquidate_position.rs:136-137), insurance_fund_balance=0, oi_long=85.8 SOL vs oi_short=0.4 SOL. Coinbase data: |5-min return| p99 = 0.572%, max 2.907% this week - a 2.5% move inside one 314s gap wipes a 20x position with nobody able to liquidate. Keeper side: liquidation-keeper.ts:208-222 falls back to computeTwap(market) (the 5.6h ring, see finding 3) when Pyth age>60s, computes health off it, and sends liquidate_position every 5s (POLL_INTERVAL_MS=5000, line 26) for every flagged position; the program rejects each with 0x104 OracleStale, which the catch at :313-315 logs as 'Failed to liquidate' with no per-position backoff (only 0x11a gets cooldown, :293). ER cannot help: Market is never delegated (S5-C1).

**Failure.** A position that goes below maintenance at t=publish+61s cannot be liquidated until the next publish (~4 min); at 20x a 2.5% move in that gap is already bad debt, and the insurance fund is 0 so it is socialised (liquidate_position.rs:236-239). Meanwhile the keeper burns one doomed simulation per underwater position per 5s for 81% of the day against the RPC quota that failed on 2026-09-04.

**Fix.** Keeper (no deploy): when Pyth age > 60s, skip the liquidation scan entirely instead of falling back to TWAP (liquidation-keeper.ts:216-222) - the program cannot accept the call. Root cause is feed cadence: same pusher remediation as finding 1. NEEDS-DEPLOY alternative: widen MAX_STALENESS_SECS only if paired with the pusher, never alone.

**Verifier verdicts (partial):**
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→both): No guard exists. Program: liquidate_position.rs:96 -> apply_dual_oracle (oracle.rs:316-358) -> dual_oracle_read (oracle.rs:249-297) -> is_fresh (oracle.rs:49-52, (0..=60).contains(&age)); both the Switchboard-dead branch (oracle.rs:262-265) and the dual branch (oracle.rs:276-278) return OracleStale (0x104, error.rs:9). liquidate_position is the only liquidation arm (instructions/mod.rs:128); execute_trigger is owner SL/TP. verify_feeds (oracle.rs:327) pins the feed to Market.pyth_feed so no substitute account works. The 60s constant and Pyth-only fallback have been in oracle.rs since 7a8f301 (2026-05-31, v1), so every deployed build carries them -> target both. Keeper: liquidation-keeper.ts:210-222 falls back to computeTwap when ageSecs>60, :228-248 flags, :270 sends with preflight on (connection.ts:178); the catch at :293-315 backs off only on 0x11a, 0x104 is just logged. No Pyth pusher anywhere in keepers/, frontend/src, client/src (grep hits only docs/audit/audit-e2e/s12.md). ecosystem.config.js runs the liquidation keeper in production. Live devnet (slot 493391011): Market.pyth_feed=7UVim..., switchboard value=0/ts=0 (single-oracle path), max_leverage=20, insurance=0, OI 85.8/0.4 SOL, Pyth age 183s at read time (a liquidation sent then is rejected). The feed is a push-oracle PDA (write_authority==self, owner rec5...), so a keeper-side Hermes push is a valid no-deploy fix. Severity corrected to medium because the claim overstates impact: (1) compute_maintenance_margin = initial_margin/2 (fixed_point.rs:73-75), so at 20x a 2.5% move reaches health=1.0 (liquidatable), NOT bad debt; bad debt (total_settlement<0, liquidate_position.rs:206-209,230-239) needs loss > ~5% of notional inside one <=~250s blind stretch, which this week's max 5-min move (2.9%) does not reach. (2) All 15 open positions have health >= 15.96 at the live price (0 underwater), so the per-5s doomed-tx burn is currently zero and engages only when a position goes underwater during the stale stretch. Real defect, bounded impact. New angle vs S3-03 (which observed Pyth at 44s fresh and concerned the mark_price_for_close asymmetry), not a restatement.
- `?` → stands (severity→medium) (target→both): Survives, severity corrected high->medium, target both confirmed. VERIFIED: liquidate_position.rs:96 -> apply_dual_oracle -> dual_oracle_read (oracle.rs:262-265 devnet Pyth-only path, :276 dual path) errors OracleStale unless is_fresh (oracle.rs:49-52, MAX_STALENESS_SECS=60 at :22). The gate and its use in liquidate_position are present in the first commit 7a8f301 (2026-05-31), so the 2026-05-30 deployed bytecode has it too => target both is right. Only crank_twap, liquidate_position and compute_funding need fresh Pyth; owner exits use mark_price_for_close (30-min mark), so liquidation is the one money path that goes dark between publishes. Keeper: liquidation-keeper.ts:212-222 detects age>60s then falls back to TWAP and still sends; catch at :290-315 cools down only on 0x11a, so 0x104 is retried every 5s (:26). Today's faa6145 applied the skip-when-stale fix to twap-keeper.ts only; git log shows no change to liquidation-keeper.ts since. NOT a restatement of a remediated P0. Root cause (Pyth ~309s cadence vs 60s gate) is the ESTABLISHED TODAY item, but the liquidation surface and the un-fixed liquidation keeper are new consequences faa6145 did not cover - evidence should cite 'root cause per faa6145 note' and 'extends S3-03 (inverse direction: crank alive, owner can exit, liquidator cannot)' rather than PRIOR S3-03 as a restatement. WHY MEDIUM NOT HIGH: (1) the 'burns a doomed send per underwater position per 5s for 81% of the day' is conditional on health<1.0 (:232-238); live scan of all 41 Position accounts shows 15 open, 0 below health 1.0 and 0 below 1.5 at the current mark, so the doomed-send cost is currently zero, and preflight-rejected sends cost no lamports (faa6145 message). (2) Bad debt needs a further ~2.5% adverse move inside a <=254s blind window after the position crosses maintenance (bonus capped at 20% of net collateral at :192-196 cannot itself create bad debt); tail event, bounded by ~$8.7K devnet USDC of long OI, worst case ~$400. (3) Fix is keeper-side (mirror faa6145's skip in liquidation-keeper.ts:216-222), no deploy. Real defect, bounded impact = medium on the given scale. Coinbase p99/max figures in the claim were not verified but are not load-bearing.


### [high/both] The OrderBook's 10-commit budget is already spent (10/10): the book can never be committed or undelegated again, 4.36 SOL of rent is stranded, and the L1 mirror is frozen at fill sequence 10 vs 53,734 on the ER

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/commit_orderbook.rs:74`  · prior: S5-04

**Evidence.** Live read 2026-09-05: OrderBook delegation record H95AVqss... (owner DELeG..., len 96) at offset 8: authority=MAS1Dt9qreoRMQ14YQuhg8UTZMMzDdKhmkZMECCzk57 (MagicBlock devnet validator; delegate_orderbook.rs passes validator=None, :105 comment), owner=7qujfsb4..., lamports=4,362,973,440 (4.36 SOL), commit_frequency_ms=4294967295 (delegate_orderbook.rs:30 COMMIT_FREQUENCY_MS=u32::MAX). Delegation metadata Dht4oGs9... u64 at offset 8 = 10. The same field reads 10 on FillLog epochs 0,1,2,3 - exactly the accounts docs/03-ephemeral-rollups-and-delegation.md:66-76 says 'committed exactly 10 then capped' - and 0 on never-committed epochs 4-11; it is the sponsored-commit counter, and the book is at the cap. L1 copy of the book (owner DELeG..., 626,736 bytes) header: next_order_id=26, next_fill_sequence=10; ER copy: active_orders=30, next_order_id=1,877,219, next_fill_sequence=53,734; Market.last_settled_sequence=44,189. commit_orderbook.rs:74 still gates on payer.is_signer() only (restates S5-04); settlement-keeper.ts:101-121 still schedules commit_orderbook every cycle and every call is now doomed. undelegate_orderbook.rs:85-94 and emergency_undelegate are ScheduleCommitAndUndelegate CPIs with nothing left to schedule (S5-02).

**Failure.** Market 0's order book has no exit from the ER: if the MagicBlock devnet validator MAS1Dt9q... is retired, reset, or hostile, every resting order and the 4.36 SOL rent are unrecoverable and the PDA [b'orderbook', 0] can never be re-created; the L1 view of the book is permanently a 10-fill snapshot. On the repo side any ER account can do the same to each fresh FillLog epoch with 10 transactions.

**Fix.** No code path recovers market 0's book; the only exit is a new market index with a fresh OrderBook PDA (ops decision, not proposed here). Repo (NEEDS-DEPLOY): gate commit_orderbook and commit_fill_log on GlobalState.authority or the R7 keeper role (set_keeper 0x2A) like their delegate/undelegate siblings; stop the settlement keeper's commit_orderbook call (settlement-keeper.ts:101-121) since it can never succeed.


### [medium/both] Circuit breaker now compares spot to a ~5-hour mean; a normal volatile day trips it and recovery takes 20-113 min with orders, closes and liquidations all blocked

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/crank_twap.rs:57`  · prior: S5-03

**Evidence.** crank_twap.rs:57-69: breaker set when |price - get_twap()| > twap/10, cleared only when a later crank is back within 10% of the (slowly moving) mean; get_twap (market.rs:123-134) is a plain count-based mean with no timestamps. Gates: place_order.rs:123, close_position.rs:101, liquidate_position.rs:87 all return MarketPaused. Measured: ring holds 59 distinct publishes (3.8 duplicate cranks each) ≈ 5.06 h; trip thresholds today are +944 bps / -1046 bps vs spot over that span. Replaying crank_twap.rs:57-69 on the live ring at the measured cadence (3.8 cranks per 309s): a step to 0.89x spot clears after 15 cranks ≈ 20 min; 0.85x after 84 cranks ≈ 113 min; 1.11x after 25 cranks ≈ 34 min (at the documented 8s cadence: 2.0 / 11.2 / 3.3 min). twap-keeper.ts:85-87 'cranks through' the breaker but can only do so inside the ~19% fresh windows, which is why recovery stretches. S5-03 needed a crank outage to make the ring stale; today's cadence makes the baseline stale in steady state. Breaker currently 0 (Market offset 5).

**Failure.** A 10% SOL move over ~5 hours (not a bad tick) halts new orders, all closes/SL/TP and all liquidations for 20-113 minutes while positions carry 20x — the exact window in which liquidations are needed.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: store a crank timestamp (or per-sample slot) and have get_twap ignore samples older than the intended window and return None when fewer than N fresh samples remain; skip the push when the price is unchanged from the last sample (crank_twap.rs:71) so duplicates stop weighting the ring. Keeper-only mitigation now: crank once per publish (twap-keeper.ts: skip when px.publishTime equals the last cranked publishTime).


### [medium/repo] Market-maker bot quotes a 3-bps ladder around a Pyth read up to ~5 min stale with no staleness gate; 47/58 publish gaps move more than the spread

`keepers/src/market-maker-bot.ts:284`

**Evidence.** market-maker-bot.ts:284-285 reads readPythPrice (on-chain PriceUpdateV2, shared/pyth.ts:64-71) and only LOGS pyth.ageSecs; :180 mid6 = pyth.price6; :53 SPREAD_BPS default 3; :128-130 ladder at k*3 bps. taker-bot.ts:265 gates on PYTH_MAX_STALENESS_SECS; the MM does not. Measured from the ring's 58 consecutive publishes: |move| > 3 bps in 47/58, > 6 bps in 29/58, > 9 bps (past all three ladder levels) in 25/58; max 172.6 bps; publish gap 314-315s. Fills settle verbatim on L1 (S4-C8 / settle_from_log), then the taker exits at the next mark (finding 1).

**Failure.** Any taker with a live feed lifts the stale side of the protocol's own MM wallets on ~80% of publish gaps for (move - spread) of notional each time; the protocol bankrolls the bots (keepers/src/bot-wallets.ts), so this is a steady drain of protocol funds (fake USDC on devnet, real on mainnet).

**Fix.** One guard in cycleWallet/main: if (pyth.ageSecs > PYTH_MAX_STALENESS_SECS) cancel resting orders (or skip re-quoting and widen) — mirror taker-bot.ts:265. No program change.


### [medium/both] Circuit breaker baseline is a 5.6h duplicate-weighted oracle average, not a 5/30-min TWAP: a real 12% intraday move latches the breaker for ~52 min, and any caller can flatten the ring to spot in one window

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/crank_twap.rs:57`  · prior: S5-03

**Evidence.** crank_twap.rs:57-69 compares the live print to get_twap() (market.rs:275-286, count-weighted mean, no timestamps) and sets circuit_breaker_active=1 on >10%; it clears only on a later in-range crank (:64-66), and cranks only land inside the 60s post-publish windows (crank_twap.rs:45). Live ring: 225 samples = 64 distinct publishes x avg 3.52 duplicates (the keeper re-cranks the same publish every 8s, twap-keeper.ts:9,104; crank_twap has no same-publish/same-slot guard). Window = 64 x 314s = 5.6h, not the '5-min' of crank_twap.rs:13 or the '30-min' of market.rs:165 / close_position.rs:96-100. Recovery after a step to 1.12x: TWAP must reach 1.018x -> 35 new samples -> 10 publishes -> ~52 min with place_order (:123), close_position (:101) and liquidate_position (:87) all returning MarketPaused. This week's max |spot - 5.5h mean| was 4.25%, so not tripped now. Flattening: 225 crank_twap calls at ~5,000 lamports each inside one window (no signer, crank_twap.rs:19-29) overwrite the ring with spot, zeroing the funding premium right before compute_funding (finding 3) - the repo's clamp does not stop a premium of 0.

**Failure.** On a 10%+ day the market halts every exit and every liquidation for ~52 min at the moment 20x positions are already through maintenance; separately, any large long can cheaply zero the funding it would otherwise pay, or restore the breaker baseline, with no authority.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: store the publish_ts alongside the ring (or a last_publish_ts in Market._padding2[4..6] as a u16 minute) and have crank_twap return Ok(()) without pushing when reading.publish_ts <= last; have get_twap ignore samples older than the window. Fix the three inconsistent window comments. Keeper-only mitigation (no deploy): crank once per distinct publish_time (track it in twap-keeper.ts) - removes duplicates but widens the ring to ~19.6h, so it is not a fix on its own.


### [low/repo] Liquidation keeper falls back to the 5-hour TWAP when Pyth is stale, then sends liquidations the program is certain to reject, every 5s, outside its own backoff

`keepers/src/liquidation-keeper.ts:216`

**Evidence.** liquidation-keeper.ts:209-225: on pyth.ageSecs > 60 it substitutes computeTwap(market) (the ring, ≈5 h lagged per finding 2) as markPrice and proceeds to :263-273 sendAndConfirm(createLiquidatePositionInstruction). liquidate_position.rs:96 -> oracle.rs:263-264 rejects with OracleStale (0x104) whenever Pyth is stale, unconditionally. The inner catch at :287-312 matches only 0x11a; 0x104 is logged at :311 and consecutiveErrors (:332-339) is never incremented, so the loop retries at POLL_INTERVAL_MS=5000 (:26) for ~80% of every publish cycle (measured fresh share 19-24%).

**Failure.** No wrong money (the program is authoritative) but every position the stale TWAP mis-flags produces a failing simulate every 5 s plus a getProgramAccounts scan, with the keeper's shared RPC quota (yesterday's Helius outage class) as the victim.

**Fix.** Delete the TWAP fallback: when pyth.ageSecs > PYTH_MAX_STALENESS_SECS skip the liquidation loop this cycle (still run executeTriggers, which uses lastMarkPrice). ~6 lines removed.


### [low/deployed] Deployed place_order sizes MARKET orders and their slippage band off an ungated last_mark_price (S2-X03 unfixed on chain)

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/place_order.rs:174`  · prior: S2-X03

**Evidence.** git diff db8f77d HEAD -- place_order.rs: deployed code is `if market.last_mark_price == 0 {OracleStale} else market.last_mark_price` and `apply_bps_u64(market.last_mark_price, ..)`; HEAD :174-176 and :235-240 route both through mark_price_for_close(now). Program last upgraded 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z (slot 484488785, matches deploy.json:18 lastUpgradeSignature; deploy.json:17 'deployedAt 2026-05-30' is stale). Not exploitable at the time of measurement: mark stamp age 3 min, mark == latest publish. Precondition: the TWAP crank dead > 30 min (or the feed's 315s cadence stretching to >30 min), after which the ER accepts market orders margined against a price no L1 instruction will settle at.

**Failure.** During a crank outage a MARKET order opens at a stale reference: under-margined when the real price is higher, and with a slippage band bracketing a price from the outage's start.

**Fix.** Already fixed at HEAD; NEEDS-DEPLOY. Until then keep the TWAP keeper's liveness alert tight (it is the only writer).


### [low/both] commit_orderbook has never landed: zero base-layer commits of the OrderBook since delegation on 2026-05-30, so the permissionless commit is an unexercised, unmeasured surface

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/commit_orderbook.rs:226`  · prior: S5-04

**Evidence.** commit_orderbook.rs:226-228 gates on payer.is_signer() only; :262 invoke ScheduleCommit. Measured: getSignaturesForAddress(83zMFL6c…, limit 25) on base returns 25 txs all dated 2026-05-30 (newest 13:45:40Z, the delegation itself, payer A5sV4… then MAS1Dt9q… via DELeG…); base owner is DELeG…, ER owner is the program, 626,736 bytes / 4.363 SOL. So the L1 copy of the book that settle_trades reads (settle_trades.rs:94,319; cursor shared with settle_from_log.rs:104,361) is 3 months old and no commit budget has been spent. A stranger is therefore the only party who has ever been able to choose when the L1 snapshot jumps.

**Failure.** Bounded today: settlement runs off the FillLog, not the book. The griefing cost/budget in S5-04 remains unmeasured because no commit has ever been observed to price it.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: gate commit_orderbook on GlobalState.authority like delegate/undelegate (S5-04); no live behaviour changes since nothing currently commits the book.


### [low/both] set_market_oracle writes new feed keys without parsing them or resetting the ring/breaker, so a rotation to a differently-scaled feed trips the breaker on the first crank

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/set_market_oracle.rs:147`

**Evidence.** set_market_oracle.rs:147-149 writes pyth_feed/switchboard_feed and nothing else; no parse_pyth/parse_switchboard call, twap_prices/twap_count/last_mark_price/circuit_breaker_active/restricted_mode untouched. crank_twap.rs:57-63 then compares the new feed's price to a ring full of the old feed's prices. twap-keeper.ts:41-43 records exactly this happening once ('legacy frozen PYTH_SOL_USD_DEVNET ($139) constant that tripped the >10% circuit breaker'). Authority-only (:125-128), so operator error not attacker.

**Failure.** An operator rotating feeds (the instruction's stated purpose) pauses the market for the finding-3 recovery window and, with a mis-typed key, blocks every price instruction until a second rotation.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: call verify-by-parsing (parse_pyth on the new account) before writing, and zero twap_count/circuit_breaker_active in the same instruction. Keeper-side today: keepers/src/set-market-oracle.ts should parse the candidate account and refuse if parse fails or |price - last_mark_price| > 10%.


### [low/repo] deploy.json says the program was deployed 2026-05-30; the live ProgramData was last written 2026-08-16 15:38:54 UTC, so the deployed bytecode is program source at 8914e31 (Jul 27) - the exact tree the prior audit froze on

`deploy.json:17`

**Evidence.** getAccountInfo 7qujfsb4... -> ProgramData 6BBrTYsX6ioS9Fu9uAPiLPRVDsBxCvy6wYWtzi85WihN (205,909 bytes), slot field @4 = 484488785, getBlockTime -> 1786462734 = 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z, upgrade authority A5sV4Pkk... (matches deploy.json:5). git log -- programs/: no program commit between 8914e31 (2026-07-27) and 385414d (2026-08-22, remediate wave 1a); commit e1304e1 (2026-08-16 21:12 IST) 'record devnet redeploy' changed only deploy.json:18 lastUpgradeSignature and left deployedAt at 2026-05-30. oracle.rs, crank_twap.rs and state/market.rs are byte-identical between 8914e31 and HEAD (git diff empty), so every oracle-path finding here applies to the live program unchanged; the 25 files that differ (961 insertions) are the R1-R7 + wave-2 fixes and are repo-only.

**Failure.** Anyone triaging from deploy.json (including this audit's brief, which states 'deployedAt 2026-05-30') mis-dates the live program by 11 weeks and cannot tell which findings are live; the correct statement is 'live = 8914e31, prior-audit freeze b98f050 has the same program source'.

**Fix.** Set deploy.json deployedAt to 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z and add the source commit (8914e31) next to lastUpgradeSignature; no code change.


### [low/both] set_market_oracle accepts any 64 bytes and parse_pyth never checks feed_id, so a feed rotation to the wrong asset or a non-feed account is silently accepted as the settlement price

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/set_market_oracle.rs:142`  · prior: S7-09

**Evidence.** set_market_oracle.rs:142-149 copies data[..32]/[32..64] into Market.pyth_feed/switchboard_feed with no account passed, no parse, no owner check. parse_pyth (oracle.rs:109-147) dispatches on data.len() alone and never checks the account owner or the PriceFeedMessage feed_id at data[41..73]; the live account's feed_id is ef0d8b6f...b56d (SOL/USD) and its write_authority is the account key itself (a keypair account, not the push-oracle PDA - Eroz5bwx.../2kZFXkjg... for shards 0/1 do not exist on devnet). S5-06 covered the missing binding structurally; the new point is that the only writer of the binding validates nothing either, and the same instruction is the intended no-deploy fix for findings 1-2.

**Failure.** A rotation to a BTC/USD PriceUpdateV2 (same 134-byte layout, same verification byte) prices SOL positions at ~$100k until the next rotation; a rotation to a 134-byte non-Pyth account is accepted if data[40]==1 and data[73..81]>0.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: have set_market_oracle take the feed account, require it to parse via parse_pyth, and store/compare the 32-byte feed_id (Market has no free 32 bytes; compare against a per-program constant for market 0 or add it on a re-init). Keeper-side (no deploy): keepers/src/set-market-oracle.ts should parse and print feed_id before sending.
