### [critical/both] Poisoned funding index (-10.38) is live and unlatched: $68,989 of minted funding credits are claimable now at a fresh mark, $39,733 withdrawable immediately, and the 10 credited longs are immune to liquidation; R6 preserves it across a redeploy

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/claim_funding.rs:209`  · prior: S3-01, S3-05

**Evidence.** Live read 2026-09-05T05:06:06Z of market ECUp8pXzVLzxjVs8mtKBJma3mdcHf8zSC4cqPeBy8MPy: cumulative_funding_index = -10_379_622_647_083_427_790 (-10.3796 in 18-dp, i.e. -1038% of notional), last_mark_price 101_641_137, mark_price_minute age 0-3 min (fresh, market.rs:154-161), insurance_fund_balance 0, vault BTWVG5oDmomaMkpdX4xYgfjr1gpEGodW5oPe3k5P71qr = 725,915.85 USDC. 10 of 12 open longs carry snapshots of +0.0018 or -0.0663 (positions.json), so compute_funding_payment (funding.rs:66-101) returns a NEGATIVE payment for each; claim_funding.rs:170-173 passes the gate because the mark is fresh, and :209-213 credits `(-payment)` into free_collateral by unconditional checked_add with no counterparty and no fund debit (S3-05, unchanged at HEAD). Replay of the program's exact integer arithmetic over the 15 open positions: claimable = $68,989.28 across 10 longs (largest HRTNWjSq…: 23.6 SOL, snapshot -66_332_971_881_250_147, credit $24,739); $39,733.00 of that belongs to 5 owners with pending_fills == 0 and is withdrawable via withdraw_collateral.rs:78,89 (only gates pending_fills and free_collateral >= amount). The other 5 are blocked only by S4-04's stuck counter. Liquidation side: liquidate_position.rs:141-158 adds `-funding_payment` into the health factor, so the unclaimed credit is a shield — HRTNWjSq… reads health 457 at $101.64, 462 at $50, 500 at $10, 928 at $1; scanning each of the 10 credited longs down to $0.001, the highest price at which any of them crosses health < 1.0 is $5.322 (9UPbaPkC…), 8 of 10 never cross. Deployed bytecode (programdata 6BBrTYsX…, last deploy slot 484488785 = 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z, no seed_credit_ledger/reset_pending_fills/set_keeper strings) has no clamp and identical claim_funding. At HEAD, docs/spec/remediate.md:512-513 states the R6 clamp 'only bounds future increments … no already-accrued value is rewritten', and funding.rs:18-23 confirms: the constants never touch the stored index. claim_funding.rs is byte-identical between 8914e31 and HEAD (git diff --stat).

**Failure.** Any of the 5 unblocked owners signs claim_funding then withdraw_collateral and removes up to $39,733 of USDC that no payer ever posted from the shared vault today; all 10 can do so for $68,989 once their pending_fills clears. Until they claim, the same 10 longs ($8,720 notional at $101.64) cannot be liquidated at any SOL price above $5.32, so a crash books the loss beyond their $32,459 collateral as bad debt against an insurance fund that is already $0. Deploying HEAD as-is changes none of these numbers.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, two parts. (1) claim_funding.rs:209-213 (and settle_trades.rs update_position credit branch, S4-X04): source the credit from insurance_fund_balance and cap it at what the fund holds (log the shortfall), so credits can never exceed collected debits. (2) One-time authority-gated repair instruction that takes market + N open Position accounts and sets each Position's funding_index_snapshot to the current index (15 accounts today), forfeiting the poisoned accrual; without (2), fix (1) merely converts the $69K into a permanent liquidation shield.


### [high/both] 1 lamport sent to the LiquidationIntent PDA makes liquidate_position fail forever for any position whose owner has pending_fills > 0 (CreateAccount on a pre-funded address)

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:323`

**Evidence.** handle_grace_window (liquidate_position.rs:295-354) is the ONLY path to liquidate a position when `user.pending_fills > 0` (:169-185). It branches on `intent_acc.data_is_empty()` (:312) and creates the intent with pinocchio_system CreateAccount (:323-330, SystemInstruction 0 per pinocchio-system create_account.rs:57-64). The intent address is the public, deterministic PDA `["liq_intent", position_pubkey]` (:304-307, liquidation_intent.rs:102). A system transfer of 1 lamport to that address creates a 0-data, system-owned account; data_is_empty() is still true, and the system program's create_account rejects any `to` with lamports > 0 (SystemError::AccountAlreadyInUse), so the CPI errors and the whole liquidation reverts, on every attempt, forever. Nothing in the program can consume or clear a 0-data account at that address (close_liquidation_intent :372-398 requires DISC_LIQUIDATION_INTENT). The precondition pending_fills > 0 is common and sticky: live read shows 13 of 52 UserAccounts with pending_fills in {1,1,1,1,1,1,1,1,2,2,2,2,7} and 5 of 15 open positions owned by them (7fW1hjit… 10.2 SOL pending 7; FdVp3mi9… 16.4 SOL pending 1; BHKAyZHe…, DZWtMexV…, JDfG3Ay7…). S4-04 documents the counter never returns to zero on its own; the only reset (reset_pending_fills 0x29, mod.rs:109) exists at HEAD but not in the deployed bytecode. Checked all 15 intent PDAs on devnet: none pre-funded yet (0 existing). place_trigger.rs:102-124 has the identical shape on the trigger PDA `["trigger", owner, market_index, kind]`, so a third party can also block a victim from ever placing a stop-loss/take-profit.

**Failure.** A trader with pending_fills > 0 (5 of 15 open positions today) pays 1 lamport + one tx and can never be liquidated; at 20x they hold a free option — profit if price recovers, unbounded bad debt to the vault if not, since close_position's deficit branch (close_position.rs:232-243) is the only way the loss is ever realised and only the trader can call it. Separately, anyone can permanently deny a chosen wallet the SL/TP feature for 1 lamport per PDA.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. In handle_grace_window (and place_trigger.rs:117-124) replace CreateAccount with the pre-funded-safe sequence: if intent_acc.lamports() < rent then Transfer the difference from the payer, then Allocate(space) and Assign(program_id) both invoke_signed with the PDA seeds. Identical behaviour for a 0-lamport account, so test_pending_fills_regressions.rs and the liquidate tests are unaffected.


### [high/both] S3-11 still at HEAD and byte-identical on chain: the recovery branch clears the LiquidationIntent then returns Err, so no path ever removes an intent and the next dip is liquidated with zero grace window; close_position/execute_trigger never clear one either

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:160`  · prior: S3-11

**Evidence.** liquidate_position.rs:160-166: `if health >= THRESHOLD { if !intent.data_is_empty() { close_liquidation_intent(...)?; } return Err(HealthFactorAboveThreshold) }` — the Err discards the zeroing and lamport move at :386-396. The file's own comment at :288-294 explains for the sibling branch that an Err rolls back the intent write and that both grace outcomes MUST be Ok; that reasoning was never applied one branch up. The only surviving clear is :275-277 on the successful-liquidation path. `grep -c LiquidationIntent close_position.rs execute_trigger.rs` = 0, so an owner close or a trigger close also leaves the intent in place, and because the intent PDA is keyed on the Position PDA (per owner+market), it survives a close-and-reopen. On the next call with pending_fills > 0, handle_grace_window :345-353 finds the intent, is_expired(now) (liquidation_intent.rs:168-170, deadline = created + 60) is true, returns Ok(true), and :184 falls through to liquidate in the same call. Live: 0 intent accounts exist today; 5 of 15 open positions have pending_fills > 0 and would create one on their first dip. The protected test test_liquidate_position_regressions.rs:185-273 passes an EMPTY intent and asserts the HealthFactorAboveThreshold error code, so a fix must keep Err when there is nothing to clear.

**Failure.** Any position that once dipped below health 1.0 with pending fills and then recovered is liquidated instantly (no 60 s window) the next time it dips, before its in-flight fills — the reason the window exists (record_pending_fill.rs) — can settle; the trader loses the position plus the bounty. The first liquidator's intent rent (LiquidationIntent::LEN = 64 B) is also stranded until an actual liquidation.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, 3 lines at :162-165: when the intent exists, clear it and `return Ok(())` with a sol_log("position healthy, intent cleared"); keep `Err(HealthFactorAboveThreshold)` only when there is no intent (keeps the protected test green). Optionally accept an optional intent account in do_close and clear it on full close.


### [medium/both] The funding 'premium' compares Pyth against a trailing average of Pyth: the TWAP ring is filled by crank_twap with the oracle price, never the book, so funding measures oracle momentum (wrong sign vs a real basis) and the ring now spans ~5 h, not 30 min

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/compute_funding.rs:74`  · prior: S3-02, S5-03

**Evidence.** compute_funding.rs:74-78 comments 'Mark price = local TWAP from book midprice samples' and uses market.get_twap() as the mark; funding.rs:36-55 computes premium = (mark - index)/index. But `grep -rn push_twap_price programs/` shows exactly one caller, crank_twap.rs:71, and the value it pushes is `reading.price` from parse_pyth (:41,:48) — the same Pyth feed that apply_dual_oracle returns as `index_price` (:81-91, single-oracle path oracle.rs:253-262). The order book lives on the ER and is never read by compute_funding. So premium = (trailing Pyth mean - Pyth spot)/spot: in an uptrend the mean lags below spot, premium < 0, and funding.rs:62 semantics make LONGS RECEIVE — the opposite of a perp trading above index. The prior audit (S3-02) believed the ring held 'the local book midprice'; it does not. Ring span: the keeper now cranks 3-4 times per Pyth publish then waits (twap-keeper.ts:9,14-30; market tx gaps measured 10,11,10,283,10,11,10,288,… s), and the live ring holds 62 distinct consecutive values across 225 slots, i.e. ~62 publishes × ~300 s ≈ 5.2 h — not the '30 min' in market.rs:6,12-16 nor the '5-min TWAP' in crank_twap.rs:13. Live numbers 05:06Z: get_twap 101.899512 vs Pyth 101.774668 → premium +0.1226% + 0.01% interest = +0.1327%/8 h interval; longs (85.8 SOL, $8,720.7 notional) would pay $11.57 per interval ($34.7/day) of which shorts (0.4 SOL) receive $0.054 and the rest is absorbed by the vault (S3-05 asymmetry in reverse). At HEAD the clamp (funding.rs:18,23) bounds this to 0.5%/interval = $43.6 per interval; on the deployed bytecode it is unbounded (that is how the index reached -10.38). The same 5-hour ring feeds the circuit breaker (crank_twap.rs:57-68): a 15% move now needs ~68 new samples (~19 publishes ≈ 98 min) to clear, during which liquidate/close/trigger all return MarketPaused (S5-03 quantified 116 cranks at 8 s ≈ 15 min).

**Failure.** Every 8 h the side that is on the right side of the trend is charged up to 0.5% of notional (HEAD) with no economic basis, and the payment is not conservative (payer debited, most of it stranded in the vault or minted to the other side). The comment misleads any operator tuning the constants, and the breaker pause window that blocks liquidations has grown from ~15 min to ~98 min for the same move.

**Fix.** Repo: correct compute_funding.rs:74 and market.rs:6,12-16; either drop the premium term (interest-only funding, one line: `let funding_rate_per_interval = INTEREST_RATE_PER_INTERVAL;`) until a book price exists on L1, or have settle_from_log push the last settled fill price into a separate ring so the premium compares book vs oracle. NEEDS-DEPLOY either way. Keeper-side, nothing to fix: the 5 h span is the honest consequence of the feed cadence.


### [medium/both] An SL/TP trigger outlives the position it was placed for: execute_trigger only garbage-collects while the position is empty, so a trigger left behind by a full close fires against the owner's next position on the same market

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/execute_trigger.rs:270`

**Evidence.** Trigger PDA is `["trigger", owner, market_index_le, [kind]]` (place_trigger.rs:90-98; trigger_order.rs:182) — one per owner+market+kind, with no field tying it to a position instance (trigger_order.rs:190-204 stores kind, trigger_above, market_index, owner, trigger_price, created_ts only). execute_trigger.rs:263-272 returns the stale-cleanup path only `if pos.is_empty()`; once the owner re-opens (Position PDA is likewise per owner+market, so it is the same account with size != 0 again), :274-290 evaluates `trigger.is_met(mark)` and calls do_close(…, 0, 0) — a full close at mark with no bound. Neither close_position.rs nor do_close touches triggers (grep = 0 hits). The frontend knows: positions-table.tsx:476-483 comments 'A full close leaves any SL/TP PDA armed with no position under it' and only refreshes a banner (TRIG-1); it does not append cancel_trigger to the close tx. The operator's keeper scans and fires every met trigger permissionlessly (liquidation-keeper.ts:98-148, executeTriggers called each cycle :320-324). Example: long SL at $95 (trigger_above=0) placed, position closed at $100, owner later opens a new long at $90 → is_met(90) is true → first keeper cycle closes the new position at mark; a stale TP (trigger_above=1) survives a flip to a short and closes it into a loss when the price rises. Live: 0 trigger accounts exist right now, so nothing is armed today.

**Failure.** A user who closes and re-enters without clearing the old trigger (the UI does not do it for them; third-party clients have no hint at all) has the new position force-closed at mark by any executor — losing the taker fee paid to open, the intended exposure, and on a stale TP the direction is inverted so it closes into a loss. The executor is paid the trigger rent for doing it.

**Fix.** Smallest, no deploy: in positions-table.tsx handleClosePosition, when triggers.stopLoss/takeProfit exist append createCancelTriggerInstruction for each to the same Transaction before sending. Program-side (NEEDS-DEPLOY, protects every client): stamp the Position's open_slot into the trigger at place_trigger (repurpose created_ts, or extend TriggerOrder::LEN by 8 — 0 live triggers so the layout change is free) and in execute_trigger require `pos.open_slot == trigger.open_slot`, else take the stale-cleanup path.


### [medium/both] liquidate_position never checks that the victim's UserAccount is program-owned, so a forged account with pending_fills = 0 skips the grace window on any fully-underwater position

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:111`  · prior: S3-06

**Evidence.** liquidate_position.rs:111-114 loads `user_account_acc` with UserAccount::from_account_info, which checks only data.len() >= 56 and data[0] == DISC_USER_ACCOUNT (user_account.rs:42-51), then compares `pos.owner != user.owner`. No `owner() == program_id` and no PDA re-derivation — in contrast to the liquidator's own account, which :406-424 (find_user_account_owned_by) explicitly owner-checks with the comment 'That check is what makes the account safe to CREDIT'. The only field read from the victim's account before settlement is `user.pending_fills` (:169). The only write to it is :226-229, inside `if total_settlement > 0`; total_settlement = net_collateral - bonus with bonus <= net/5 (:192-196), so it is > 0 iff net_collateral > 0. When net_collateral <= 0 the else branch (:230-240) writes only the market, the bounty is 0 (:256), and the transaction never mutates the forged account — the runtime's not-owned-write check does not fire and the call succeeds. An attacker therefore deploys any program, creates a 56-byte account under it with byte0 = 3, owner = victim, pending_fills = 0, and passes it as account [2]; the real UserAccount's pending_fills (13 of 52 live accounts are > 0) is never consulted. Position PDA is per (owner, market) so the forged account cannot be confused with a program-owned one that has the same owner. Protected tests (test_liquidate_position_regressions.rs:196,222,307,344) build user accounts as `program_account(&program_id, …)` at random pubkeys, so an owner() check passes them but a PDA re-derivation would break all four.

**Failure.** The §11 grace window — the only protection for a trader whose in-flight fills would change the outcome — is bypassable by any liquidator on exactly the positions where a pending reducing fill at a better price would have reduced the deficit the insurance fund (currently $0) absorbs; after the bypass the pending fill settles as an unintended new position (settle_trades.rs update_position opening branch) with fill_margin the trader did not mean to commit.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, 3 lines after :111: `if user_account_acc.owner() != program_id { return Err(ProgramError::IllegalOwner); }`. Owner check only — do not add a [SEED_USER, owner] re-derivation, it would fail the protected tests that use random program-owned pubkeys.


### [medium/both] S3-03's one-sided gate now runs the other way: Pyth posts every ~300-600 s against the 60 s oracle gate, so liquidate_position is callable ~20% of wall time while close/claim/trigger succeed 100% of the time on the 30-min mark budget

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:94`  · prior: S3-03

**Evidence.** liquidate_position.rs:94-104 prices health off apply_dual_oracle, which on the devnet single-oracle path requires `pyth.is_fresh(now)` with MAX_STALENESS_SECS = 60 (oracle.rs:22,49-53,253-262) or returns OracleStale. close_position.rs:122-125, claim_funding.rs:170-173 and execute_trigger.rs:280-283 instead use mark_price_for_close, whose budget is MARK_PRICE_MAX_STALENESS_MINS = 30 on the crank stamp (market.rs:16,154-161). Measured: Pyth publish_time age 226 s at 04:53:27Z and 282 s at 05:06:06Z (both > 60 → liquidate_position and compute_funding revert with OracleStale at both instants); consecutive on-chain publishes observed at 1788583781 and 1788584409 (628 s apart); the keeper's crank bursts recur every 282-293 s. Mark stamp age was 3 min and 0 min at the same reads (fresh). So at any random instant the owner can close, claim funding, or have a trigger fired, and nobody can liquidate. The keeper's pre-filter (liquidation-keeper.ts:202-203) also reads live Pyth so it cannot even try in the gap. S3-03 (freeze) documented the inverse case with the mark 16 days stale; the asymmetry — two settlement prices with independent gates — is the same defect.

**Failure.** A 20x position can move 5% (100% of initial margin) inside a 5-10 min gap in which no liquidation can land; every such gap is bad debt to the $0 insurance fund. Conversely when the crank dies again the freeze-time direction returns (liquidate on live oracle, exits blocked).

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: make both sides consult one source — in liquidate_position, when apply_dual_oracle returns OracleStale, fall back to `market.mark_price_for_close(now)` (feed-bound, <=30 min, written only by the freshness-gated crank_twap) and error only if that is None too; or gate the three exit paths on the same 60 s oracle read (freezes both directions, safer default per S3-03).


### [medium/both] S3-10 restated with the date: the next stamp==0 minute is 2026-09-11T13:20:00Z-13:20:59Z, six days out; any crank_twap that lands in it switches the mark-staleness gate off until the next non-zero crank

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/crank_twap.rs:75`  · prior: S3-10

**Evidence.** crank_twap.rs:75-76 stamps `((now_ts / 60) % 65536) as u16`; market.rs:154-158 treats stamp == 0 as fresh at any age and :136-141 documents the 1-in-45-days minute as an accepted allowance. 1789132800 / 60 = 29,818,880 = 455 × 65,536, so the window is unix 1789132800..1789132859 = 2026-09-11T13:20:00Z..13:20:59Z (verified). crank_twap has no signer check (:19-29) and is feed-bound and freshness-gated (:37,:45), so a crank in that window needs a Pyth publish <= 60 s old; with the measured ~300-628 s publish cadence the operator's own publish-aware keeper (twap-keeper.ts:14-30) lands there with probability ~60/300-60/628 (10-20%), and an attacker who waits for a publish inside the window does it deterministically. The gate then serves the frozen price through close_position/claim_funding/execute_trigger until some later crank writes a non-zero minute; if the keeper is down at that point (the K1 pattern), it is off indefinitely. Files unchanged between 8914e31 and HEAD.

**Failure.** During any keeper outage that begins after a window crank, every close, funding claim and trigger settles at an arbitrarily old price with no OracleStale — the silent settlement the gate was written to stop; an attacker who opens before the window and cranks inside it later closes at the frozen mark while the index has moved.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, one token at crank_twap.rs:76: `market.set_mark_price_minute(now_min.max(1))` (one minute of resolution lost once per 45.5 days); or drop the stamp == 0 escape at market.rs:156-158 entirely since :174 already keys 'never cranked' off last_mark_price == 0.


### [low/both] S3-06 still at HEAD: cancel_trigger binds neither owner() nor the trigger PDA before zeroing the account and draining its lamports

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/cancel_trigger.rs:176`  · prior: S3-06

**Evidence.** cancel_trigger.rs:163-181: `_program_id` is unused; the trigger is loaded by discriminator only (trigger_order.rs:209-218), `trigger.owner` is compared to the signer, and close_trigger_account (:186-200) zeroes all data and moves every lamport to the signer. execute_trigger.rs:249-259 re-derives the PDA for the same account type; close_liquidation_intent (liquidate_position.rs:378-380) owner-checks its sibling. Not exploitable today: the runtime refuses writes to non-program-owned accounts and DISC_TRIGGER_ORDER = 9 is unique among the program's types, so the only account that passes is a real TriggerOrder owned by the signer. Byte-identical on the deployed bytecode.

**Failure.** No money path today; the file is a general 'zero this account and take its lamports' primitive guarded by one byte and one 32-byte field, which becomes the bug already fixed once at liquidate_position.rs:360-371 the day a new program-owned type shares byte 0 with a trigger.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, 3 lines: `if trigger_acc.owner() != program_id { IllegalOwner }` and re-derive `[SEED_TRIGGER, owner, trigger.market_index, trigger.kind]` before close_trigger_account (execute_trigger.rs:251-259 already has the exact code).


### [low/both] S3-07 still at HEAD: funding_interval is written unvalidated and compute_funding divides by it, so a zero-interval market aborts every funding call permanently

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/compute_funding.rs:43`  · prior: S3-07, S3-X02

**Evidence.** initialize_market.rs:85-92 rejects tick_size/lot_size/max_leverage == 0 and a bad fee pair but writes `funding_interval` straight through at :173. compute_funding.rs:36-43: with interval_secs == 0 the guard `elapsed < interval_secs` is false for any now >= last_funding_ts and `elapsed / interval_secs` panics (overflow-checks on, Cargo.toml:13-14 — but integer division by zero always panics), an unrecoverable SBF abort rather than a returned error; nothing else can write the field. Live market reads funding_interval_secs = 28800, so not reachable on the deployed market. test_initialize_market_regressions.rs:57 passes 3_600, so a zero-reject does not touch the protected suite.

**Failure.** A future market created with 0 (operator typo or a permissionless market path) has funding bricked for its lifetime and every compute_funding call burns the caller's fee on an abort.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, one condition: add `|| funding_interval == 0` to initialize_market.rs:85 and `if interval_secs == 0 { return Err(DivisionByZero) }` at compute_funding.rs:36.


### [low/deployed] S3-04 is live on the deployed bytecode (bounty charged and destroyed; the keeper's 9th account is ignored) and half-fixed at HEAD (bonus is still 0 exactly when bad debt is generated)

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:192`  · prior: S3-04

**Evidence.** Deployed tree 8914e31 liquidate_position.rs:193,:206 — liquidator_bonus is referenced on two lines, neither pays anyone (git show 8914e31:…). The operator's keeper already passes its UserAccount as a 9th account expecting payment (liquidation-keeper.ts:251-270), which the deployed program never reads. HEAD :242-272 pays it to that account or to the insurance fund, but :192-196 still yields bonus = min(50 bps, net_collateral/5) = 0 whenever net_collateral <= 0 — the only positions that create insurance/bad-debt exposure — with no insurance-funded floor; the live insurance_fund_balance is 0 so a floor would be unfunded anyway. Live: 0 of 15 positions are liquidatable at Pyth $101.77 at this instant, so nothing is being destroyed right now.

**Failure.** The next liquidation on devnet over-charges the trader by up to 20% of remaining net collateral and pays nobody; third-party liquidators have no reason to clear underwater positions on either build.

**Fix.** Repo half already landed at 676e9f1 (NEEDS-DEPLOY). Remaining at HEAD: fund a small floor bounty from insurance_fund_balance when net_collateral <= 0 and the fund can cover it, so the incentive is non-zero on the positions that matter.


### [low/both] Funding is scaled by the mark at realisation, not at accrual, and only the receiver ever has to call claim_funding — so each side picks its own price and credits never equal debits

`programs/slipstream/src/math/funding.rs:86`  · prior: S3-05

**Evidence.** compute_funding_payment (funding.rs:66-101) = notional(|size|, mark_price_now) × (index_now - snapshot) / 1e18: the cumulative index is a dimensionless rate sum, and the notional it is applied to is priced at whatever mark the realising instruction passes — claim_funding.rs:170-181 (owner-chosen time), close_position.rs:163-168, liquidate_position.rs:141-146, settle_trades.rs update_position (:346). claim_funding is owner-signed and voluntary (:138-140), so a payer never claims; its debit is realised only when it closes, is liquidated or a fill settles, and it can wait for a low mark; a receiver claims at a high mark. For the same interval the two sides therefore use different notionals. Live example: HRTNWjSq…'s credit is $24,739 at mark $101.64 and would be $12,170 at $50 (both replayed) — the same index delta, a factor of two apart. Bounded per position by |Δmark| × accrued rate (S3-05's mint is the unbounded part, this is the structural non-conservation underneath it).

**Failure.** Even after S3-05's credit cap, the sum credited to receivers is not the sum debited from payers; the difference is a claim on or gift to the shared vault sized by mark timing, chosen by users.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: make the index quote-denominated per base atom — in compute_funding add `rate × mark_at_interval` (18-dp × 6-dp, carry in i128) instead of `rate`, and in compute_funding_payment use `size × Δindex` without a current mark. Existing snapshots would need the same one-time re-snapshot as finding 1.


### [low/both] liquidate_position's insurance-deficit branch writes no log and no counter, so bad debt beyond the (currently $0) fund is booked nowhere

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/liquidate_position.rs:230`  · prior: S3-01

**Evidence.** liquidate_position.rs:230-240: `deficit` is subtracted from insurance_fund_balance or the fund is zeroed and the remainder silently dropped ('absorb the rest as protocol bad debt'), with no sol_log_64. The sibling path close_position.rs:232-251 logs [deficit, fund_before, fund_after, bankrupt] with the comment 'a silent one is unauditable'. Live insurance_fund_balance = 0, so every liquidation deficit from now on is 100% unlogged bad debt. Market has no bad_debt field (market.rs:20-55).

**Failure.** An operator cannot reconcile the vault (725,915.85 USDC) against free_collateral (551,264.96 USDC sum over 52 accounts) plus position collateral (32,459.39) because the liquidation losses that explain the gap were never recorded.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY, copy close_position.rs:244-251 into the else branch at liquidate_position.rs:235-239 (5 lines).

### [critical/both] Delegation-program undelegate callback (EXTERNAL_UNDELEGATE 0xC4) is not dispatched, so no delegated TradingCredit can ever return to L1; R1/R2's whole migration and seed_credit_ledger are unreachable for 100% of live users, and R1 turns the stuck state into a free_collateral lock

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/mod.rs:180`  · prior: S5-02

**Evidence.** mod.rs:112-180 dispatches on the first byte 0x00..0x2A and `_ => Err(InvalidInstructionData)`; there is no arm for the delegation program's owner-program callback. delegation-program src/processor/fast/undelegate.rs:224-300 — when the delegated account has data, the validator's L1 `undelegate` copies the committed bytes into an undelegate buffer, closes the delegated account, and CPIs the OWNER program with data = EXTERNAL_UNDELEGATE_DISCRIMINATOR = [196,28,41,206,48,37,51,167] (dlp-api/src/consts.rs:22-23) plus borsh seeds, then requires `delegated_account data == buffer data` (undelegate.rs:370-375) or fails InvalidAccountDataAfterCPI. Live simulate (2026-09-05, replaceRecentBlockhash) of data [196,28,41,206,48,37,51,167] against 7qujfsb4… -> `InstructionError [0, InvalidInstructionData]` (a known ix 0x13 returns NotEnoughAccountKeys, so the probe discriminates). Consequence measured on chain: 30/30 slipstream-owned modern credits (delegation records name owner 7qujfsb4…, e.g. record 3o1GmW6t… for credit bbUpyrnp…) and 12/12 legacy credits are delegated; the only 5 program-owned credits (4 legacy + Bkcwr5R4… 500 USDC, 2 txs in its history) were never delegated; 0 accounts have ever come back. Repo: seed_credit_ledger.rs:69-71 refuses a delegated credit (`CreditStillActive`), fund_trading_credit.rs:52-54 and withdraw_trading_credit.rs:41-43 likewise; withdraw_collateral.rs:85 gate 2 refuses while `reserved_margin > 0`. Spec docs/spec/remediate.md:324-330 and R1.md:70-71 plan '46 seed calls, each after its owner undelegates' — that step cannot execute. docs/03-ephemeral-rollups-and-delegation.md:94-95 already hints 'No clean callback / stale ABI' but only for the OrderBook.

**Failure.** Deployed: 98,711.60 USDC in 30 modern credits + 36,470.00 USDC in 12 legacy credits are permanently unreachable (S5-02's 'four bot credits' is actually every credit that ever got delegated). Repo, if deployed as-is: (a) no live user can ever be seeded, so their credit stays un-withdrawable forever; (b) every NEW user who calls fund_trading_credit gets ledger > 0 for a credit that can never be undelegated, so withdraw_collateral refuses their entire free_collateral for life (intake ruling 1's premise 'withdraw your credit first' is impossible); the only repair is an undocumented trick (initialize_trading_credit at an unused market_index, then seed_credit_ledger(0) through that throwaway credit), one authority tx per user.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. Add a dispatch arm for the 8-byte EXTERNAL_UNDELEGATE_DISCRIMINATOR (data[0]==196, check all 8 bytes) that re-creates the PDA from the undelegate buffer, and REFUSE (return Err, which leaves the account delegated rather than paying out) unless buffer.len()==TradingCredit::LEN, data[0]==DISC_TRADING_CREDIT, owner/market_index unchanged and the address matches [b"credit", owner, market_index]. Do not deploy R1 before this arm exists and the 30 live credits have been undelegated and seeded.


### [critical/both] S3-01 has already detonated on devnet: cumulative_funding_index is -10.38, 10 open longs are owed 69,149.63 USDC of unbacked funding claimable now, and R6 ships nothing that can rebase the index or re-snapshot positions

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/compute_funding.rs:101`  · prior: S3-01

**Evidence.** Live Market ECUp8pXz… read 2026-09-05: cumulative_funding_index = -10_379_622_647_083_427_790 (-10.3796 as a fraction of notional), last_funding_ts = 1788559400 = 2026-09-04T22:03:20Z, funding_interval_secs = 28800, last_mark_price 101_877_385 with mark minute 56391 vs now-minute 56392 (fresh). The audit snapshot docs/audit/audit-e2e/repro/s3/market-ECUp8p-devnet-2026-08-21T19-13-46Z.json decodes to index -0.0387; s3.md:68 predicted one call would install -9.12. Two open positions carry snapshot -10.6686, so the index went at least that low. Decoding all 41 Position accounts (15 open): 10 longs with snapshots in [-0.0665, +0.0018] are owed compute_funding_payment (funding.rs:66-101) of -69,149.63 USDC in total (largest HRTNWjSq… 23.6 SOL: -24,796.31 on 2,404 USDC notional; FdVp3mi9…: -17,237.34; 5keWTGsy…: -13,658.98; 7fW1hjit…: -10,716.88). claim_funding.rs:59-70 prices it at mark_price_for_close (fresh) and :99-103 credits `free_collateral += -payment` with checked_add and no counterparty or cap; close_position.rs:190-231 does the same via `settlement`. 39,825.35 USDC of it belongs to owners whose UserAccount has pending_fills == 0, so withdraw_collateral's gates 1-3 are open; the other 29,324.28 is behind pending_fills only. Repo: compute_funding.rs:96-107 is the ONLY writer of the index besides initialize_market.rs:182; R6 (funding.rs:18-23, 54; compute_funding.rs:49-70) clamps future increments only; docs/spec/remediate.md:509-513 explicitly promises 'no already-accrued value is rewritten'. Vault BTWVG5oD… holds 725,915.85 USDC, so this is 9.5% of the vault.

**Failure.** Any of the 10 long holders signs claim_funding today and mints up to 24.8k USDC of free_collateral out of the shared vault, then withdraws; the six with pending_fills==0 can do it end-to-end now (39.8k). Deploying R6 changes nothing about the 69k already accrued; the number keeps growing at the (now clamped) rate every 8h interval for positions that stay open.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. Add an authority-gated `reset_funding_snapshot` that sets Position.funding_index_snapshot = market.cumulative_funding_index for one position (forgives accrual both ways) and run it over the 15 open positions in the same deploy window, before anyone can call claim_funding; alternatively an authority-gated index rebase plus the same per-position snapshot rewrite. Until then, no code path stops the claims.


### [high/repo] Ledger goes stale-high on every PENDING (unsettled) fill; the spec's repair (seed to 0) then makes R4 open the position with zero collateral

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/seed_credit_ledger.rs:79`

**Evidence.** Chain, all on this branch: fund_trading_credit.rs:75-78 raises reserved_margin; on the ER a fill drains credit.credit (place_order.rs:353-357, reconcile trading_credit.rs:238-256) but the L1 ledger is lowered ONLY inside settle_from_log.rs:258-273 / settle_trades.rs:216-224. While the fill is unsettled (live: fill-log keeper STOPPED, settlement keeper dead), the owner undelegates and withdraw_trading_credit pays min(credit=0, ledger)=0 (withdraw_trading_credit.rs:53-65), leaving ledger = unsettled margin; withdraw_collateral.rs:85-87 then refuses ALL free_collateral (gate 2). Spec Decision 4b (docs/spec/remediate.md:356-397) names only 'destroyed fill or close_trading_credit' as stale sources and prescribes seed_credit_ledger(amount=0): seed_credit_ledger.rs:79-82 writes min(credit.credit=0, 0)=0 unconditionally. When settlement later runs, settle_from_log.rs:260/270 applies min(filled_margin, ledger=0)=0 and settle_trades.rs:394-404 (update_position) does pos.collateral += 0 with pos.size = qty: a position with zero collateral, the user's margin gone. After R3 fills are never destroyed, so every stale-high ledger is a pending fill and the documented repair is exactly wrong. No test covers seed-then-settle; test_settlement_reader_regressions.rs:332 only covers ledger < filled_margin as an attack, not as an operator-caused state. Frontend decodes creditOutstanding (use-positions.ts:73) but never consults it before withdraw.

**Failure.** Any trader who trades, undelegates and withdraws before the (currently non-running) settlement lands has 100% of free_collateral locked; the operator's prescribed unlock (0x28 with amount 0) silently destroys the in-flight margin and hands the user a zero-collateral, instantly liquidatable position.

**Fix.** Drop gate 2 (withdraw_collateral.rs:85-87) — no protected test pins ReservedMarginExists there (grep test_withdraw_collateral_regressions.rs = 0 hits); the ceiling at withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63-65 already bounds the ER and gate 2 adds only the lock. Until then the runbook must forbid seeding below Σ unsettled filled_margin for that owner in the FillLog ring above Market.last_settled_sequence. NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [high/repo] R2's legacy branch pays ER-authored `credit` verbatim into free_collateral with no ceiling — the hostile-ER mint R1 closes is reopened for the 12 delegated 56-byte credits

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/close_trading_credit.rs:92`  · prior: S1-01

**Evidence.** close_trading_credit.rs:73-77 discriminates on data_len()==56 (LEGACY_LEN, trading_credit.rs:54), then :92-114 adds `credit_amt` (read_common, trading_credit.rs:67-77, bytes the ER owned while delegated) straight to user.free_collateral with checked_add and no reference to reserved_margin. R1's ceiling is bypassed by construction: the owner's ledger is 0 for these accounts (never funded via the new path), so withdraw_collateral.rs:85 does not bind either. Live (2026-09-05): 12 delegation-owned 56-byte DISC_TRADING_CREDIT accounts, Σ credit 36,470.00 USDC nominal (e.g. 3kz2Ex… 8,000; 6sr6Ux… 5,000; 7UkUnY… 5,000), whose bytes the ER validator controls; MagicBlock's delegation program lets the validator commit+undelegate without this program's CPI, so they can return as program-owned 56-byte accounts holding any credit value the ER chooses. R2.md 'Not graded' calls them merely 'not recovered' — it never treats them as an attack surface. The R1 threat model (user_account.rs:18-23 'a hostile ER owns every byte') is contradicted by R2 inside the same run.

**Failure.** Under the threat model R1 exists for, the ER sets credit on any delegated legacy account to N, undelegates, and the owner (or a colluding bot wallet — several of the 12 owners are bot keys) closes it for N USDC into free_collateral and withdraws it from the 725,915 USDC vault. Even under an honest ER the 36,470 USDC nominal is paid with no L1 record backing it.

**Fix.** Require GlobalState.authority as a second signer on the `is_legacy && credit_amt > 0` branch only (owner still signs; nothing is weakened), so the operator vets each of the 16 legacy payouts against L1 fund history. NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [high/repo] R2's legacy recovery is an uncapped payout of an ER-authorable field, and the delegation program's finalize resizes accounts to whatever length the ER commits, so a hostile ER can shrink any delegated credit to 56 bytes and cash it out around R1's ceiling

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/close_trading_credit.rs:110`  · prior: S1-01

**Evidence.** close_trading_credit.rs:72-77 treats data_len()==LEGACY_LEN(56) as legacy; :79-82 reads (owner, credit, committed, active_orders) by fixed offset via trading_credit.rs:67-77 `read_common`; :92-114 then `free_collateral.checked_add(credit_amt)` with no reference to UserAccount.reserved_margin and no authority attestation. R2.md:57-60 argues the branch 'is not a way around that ceiling' because only 56-byte accounts reach it, and the spec (docs/spec/remediate.md:157-162) states the ER owns every byte of a delegated credit. delegation-program src/processor/fast/finalize.rs:174-179: `delegated_account.resize(commit_state_data.len())?; ... copy_from_slice(&commit_state_data)` — the committed length is validator-chosen, so a 96-byte credit can be committed back as 56 bytes with credit = anything. 12 delegated legacy credits (36,470 USDC nominal, all 11 checked delegation records name owner 7qujfsb4…) already sit exactly in this path with ER-owned bytes. Not exploitable today only because undelegation cannot complete at all (see the 0xC4 finding); it becomes live the moment that callback is added without a length check.

**Failure.** A user colluding with (or being) the ER validator commits their own delegated credit as a 56-byte account with credit = 725_915_845_954, undelegates, signs close_trading_credit with their UserAccount as [2], and receives the whole vault into free_collateral, with reserved_margin untouched so gate 2 does not fire; R1's ledger never enters the path.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. In the 0xC4 undelegate callback refuse any buffer with len != TradingCredit::LEN, which makes the 56-byte branch reachable only by the 4 program-owned legacy accounts that exist today (Dojn38Q5… 13,163.24; three at 50.00). Additionally bound the legacy branch like seed_credit_ledger: require GlobalState.authority as a co-signer with an attested amount and pay min(credit_amt, amount).


### [high/repo] UserAccount identity is never pinned to its PDA, so an ER-authored TradingCredit committed with discriminator 3 masquerades as a UserAccount and withdraw_collateral pays its free_collateral field; Decision 1's proof covers ownership transfer, not this

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/withdraw_collateral.rs:71`  · prior: S5-01

**Evidence.** `grep -rn SEED_USER programs/slipstream/src/instructions/` hits only initialize_user.rs (3 lines); no other load site derives [b"user", owner]. withdraw_collateral.rs:68-74 accepts `user_account_acc` on `owner()==program_id` + `UserAccount::from_account_info` (user_account.rs:42-51: len>=56 and data[0]==3) + `user.owner==signer`; gates at :77-88 read pending_fills (offset 2) and reserved_margin (offset 48) from the same attacker-shaped bytes; withdraw_trading_credit.rs:45-49, close_trading_credit.rs:101-107, seed_credit_ledger.rs:74-77 and settle_trades.rs:534-548 `find_user_account` (disc+owner match, no PDA) share the pattern. delegation-program finalize.rs:174-179 writes validator-chosen bytes of validator-chosen length into the delegated credit, and undelegate.rs:273-300 hands those exact bytes to the owner program to re-create. docs/spec/remediate.md:172-214 proves only that no UserAccount can be re-owned; it never considers a credit PDA carrying DISC_USER_ACCOUNT. Latent today for the same reason as the R2 finding (no 0xC4 arm).

**Failure.** ER commits the attacker's delegated credit as 56 bytes: [3, bump, 0,0 (pending_fills), 0,0,0,0, owner=attacker, free_collateral=725_915_845_954, reserved_margin=0]; after undelegation the attacker passes that PDA as user_account to withdraw_collateral and drains the vault; R1's ledger reads 0 on the forged account so gate 2 is silent.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. Pin every UserAccount load to find_program_address([b"user", owner]) (a helper on UserAccount taking program_id+owner), at minimum in withdraw_collateral, withdraw_trading_credit, close_user_account, close_trading_credit, seed_credit_ledger and find_user_account; and have the 0xC4 callback refuse a buffer whose data[0] != DISC_TRADING_CREDIT.


### [high/repo] An un-seeded ledger turns every fill into a zero-collateral position: settlement applies min(filled_margin, ledger) as Position.collateral, all 30 live delegated users have ledger 0 and cannot be seeded, so after deploy their margin is drained on the ER and lands as 0 on L1

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_trades.rs:216`

**Evidence.** settle_trades.rs:214-225 `applied = fill.filled_margin.min(user.reserved_margin)` for both legs, passed as fill_margin at :296-309 into update_position, which does `pos.collateral += fill_margin` (:391-395, :409-413); settle_from_log.rs:258-272, 317, 328 is identical. Its own comment (:245-247) says 'under an honest ER the ledger tracks TradingCredit.credit atom for atom' — false for every pre-deploy user: 52/52 live UserAccounts read reserved_margin == 0 (offset 48) while 30 delegated slipstream credits hold 98,711.60 USDC (all idle: committed 0, active_orders 0). seed_credit_ledger.rs:69-71 refuses delegated credits and undelegation cannot complete (0xC4 finding), so the ledger stays 0. On the ER, place_order.rs:352-357 debits the taker's credit and :438 drains the maker slot regardless. Also latent: seed_credit_ledger.rs:79 `credit.credit.min(amount)` bounds a per-USER ledger by ONE credit, so with market_count > 1 the ledger can never be seeded above the largest single credit (initialize_trading_credit.rs:36-45 creates one credit per market_index, unvalidated against market_count).

**Failure.** The moment R1+R4 are deployed, the market-maker and taker bots (and every human among the 30) keep matching; each fill removes filled_margin from their ER credit and adds 0 to Position.collateral, so the position is created at health 0, is liquidatable on the first adverse tick, and the margin is stranded in the vault as unattributed USDC; seed_credit_ledger cannot repair it because the credit is delegated.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY. Make the trading_credit account optional in seed_credit_ledger (write `amount` directly when it is omitted — the min() bound is meaningless for a delegated credit and its stated rationale was a false positive, see the '22 garbage credits' finding), pause via set_global_pause (settle_* honour it), seed all 30 from L1 fund/withdraw history, then unpause; and pass Σ over the user's credits when seeding, not one.


### [medium/repo] The grandfather step cannot run at deploy: seed_credit_ledger refuses delegated credits and 52 of 53 live modern credits are delegated; no off-chain builder for 0x28 exists

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/seed_credit_ledger.rs:69`  · prior: S5-02

**Evidence.** seed_credit_ledger.rs:69-71 returns CreditStillActive when trading_credit_acc.owner() != program_id. Live count (getProgramAccounts, 2026-09-05): program-owned 96-byte credits = 1 (Bkcwr5…, 500 USDC); delegation-owned 96-byte credits = 52 (30 sane Σ 98,711.60 USDC + 22 corrupted at 7.017e18). All 52 UserAccounts read reserved_margin = 0. R1.md:74-75 says '46 live credits will be seeded with it' — only one is seedable at deploy; the other 52 need each user to undelegate first (an ER-only path, S5-02), after which withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63-65 fails CreditCeilingExceeded until the operator hand-seeds. grep -rn '0x28|seedCreditLedger|seed_credit' keepers/src client/src frontend/src = 0 hits: no builder, no script, no watcher. 'Believed' amount must come from L1 fund history; no tool computes it (inspect-credits.ts does not).

**Failure.** From the moment the branch deploys, every existing user with credit (≥98,711.60 USDC across 30 honest accounts) is locked out of withdraw_trading_credit until an unscripted, per-user, timing-dependent operator action; the 22 corrupted-credit owners are additionally blocked forever unless the operator states a number.

**Fix.** Add keepers/src/seed-credit-ledger.ts: scan program-owned DISC_TRADING_CREDIT accounts whose owner's reserved_margin is 0, compute Σfund−Σwithdraw from L1 tx history, send 0x28 with min(that, credit.credit). Run it on a loop post-deploy. No program change.


### [medium/repo] The keeper role bundles a protocol-wide withdrawal-freeze and liquidation-immunity capability, and only the admin key can undo it

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/record_pending_fill.rs:64`  · prior: S10-01

**Evidence.** record_pending_fill.rs:64-67 accepts GlobalState::is_authority_or_keeper (global_state.rs:91-97). The file's own doc (:24-36) states a bump is 'a permanent, unbounded DoS on any listed victim's withdrawals' and 'a self-liquidation immunity switch' — nothing ties the bump to a real fill (no FillLog account, no sequence). set_keeper.rs:13-21 explicitly intends the keeper to be 'a low-value key' on a keeper VM. The only repair, reset_pending_fills.rs:61-64, is authority-only and one user per call. Live: 52 UserAccounts, Σ free_collateral 551,264.96 USDC, 13 already stuck with pending_fills > 0.

**Failure.** A compromised keeper VM key freezes withdraw_collateral and close_user_account for all 52 users and detours every liquidation into the grace window (liquidate_position) until the hot admin key — the one R7 exists to take offline — signs 52 reset_pending_fills.

**Fix.** Bind record_pending_fill to evidence: pass the FillLog PDA and bump only owners named by fills with sequence in (Market.last_settled_sequence, head]; or at minimum cap pending_fills at a small constant so the DoS is bounded. NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [medium/both] R6's clamp is already binding on live state and the funding sign is TWAP lag, not basis — so the 'policy' constant is the effective rate every interval, minted one-sided

`programs/slipstream/src/math/funding.rs:18`  · prior: S3-01

**Evidence.** Measured 2026-09-05T00:25Z: Market.twap (225 samples) = 101,111,062; last_mark_price = 101,768,072 = Pyth 6dp; premium = −0.6455% per interval, already outside MAX_FUNDING_RATE_PER_INTERVAL (funding.rs:18, ±0.5%). funding_interval_secs = 28,800 → the composed bound is ±1.5%/day; last_funding_ts 22,906 s old so raw_intervals = 0 (compute_funding.rs:49). OI long 85.8 SOL vs short 0.4 SOL; claim_funding credits the receiving leg unconditionally (S3-05 open). At current OI ≈ $8.7k long notional that is ≈ $131/day minted with a $40 counterparty, scaling linearly with any long an attacker opens. Sign is steerable: crank_twap has no signer (S5-03 open). Deployed (no clamp): −0.6355%/interval, i.e. S3-01 is latent and bounded only by funding-keeper liveness (one missed 8h interval = one extra −0.64%). The R6 test (test_funding_clamp_regressions.rs:18-66) checks only the replay magnitude, not that a lagged TWAP keeps the rate pinned to the clamp.

**Failure.** Funding is dominated by the clamp value rather than market basis; with the S3-05 mint, longs are paid ~1.5%/day of notional from the vault whenever the oracle drifts >0.5%/8h, and an attacker who cranks the TWAP chooses the side.

**Fix.** Clamp is fine; the root is the mark: compute_funding.rs:74-78 should take the book-mid TWAP, not the oracle-restamped TWAP (S3-02), and crank_twap needs a signer (S5-03). Interim: lower MAX_FUNDING_RATE_PER_INTERVAL to ~1e15 (0.1%) so a 30-min oracle lag is inside the clamp. NEEDS-DEPLOY.


### [medium/repo] R7's keeper key is not low-value: it can bump pending_fills on every UserAccount, freezing withdraw_collateral/close_user_account protocol-wide until the authority resets accounts one tx each

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/record_pending_fill.rs:65`  · prior: S10-01

**Evidence.** record_pending_fill.rs:64-67 now accepts `GlobalState::is_authority_or_keeper` (global_state.rs:91-97); :72-86 bumps pending_fills for up to `remaining.len()` accounts with no tie to any fill. The file's own docstring :24-36 describes the capability as 'a permanent, unbounded DoS on any listed victim's withdrawals ... and a self-liquidation immunity switch'. withdraw_collateral.rs:77-79 and close_user_account.rs:83-85 refuse on pending_fills > 0; liquidate_position.rs:169-183 detours into the 60s grace window (liquidation_intent.rs:26). The only clear is reset_pending_fills.rs:71, authority-only, one account per call. set_keeper.rs:13-21 and docs/spec/remediate.md:527-535 describe the keeper as a key that 'can come down' / low-value. Live: 52 UserAccounts, 13 already stuck behind pending_fills.

**Failure.** A leaked keeper keypair (the whole point of R7 is that it lives hot on a VM) issues a few txs listing every UserAccount; all 52 users lose withdraw_collateral and close_user_account until the authority signs 52 reset_pending_fills txs; every liquidation against a colluding account is delayed by 60s per intent.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY (small). Let reset_pending_fills take N accounts per call so recovery is one tx, and state in set_keeper's doc that the keeper gates a protocol-wide withdrawal freeze; longer term, bound record_pending_fill to a FillLog sequence above Market.last_settled_sequence that names the user.


### [low/repo] R7 code half is unwired: no set_keeper builder, fill-log keeper still signs with the shared authority-path loader, GlobalState unextended

`keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:74`  · prior: S10-01

**Evidence.** grep -rn '0x2A|0x2a|setKeeper|set_keeper' keepers/src client/src frontend/src = 0 hits. fill-log-keeper.ts:74 `const keeper = loadKeypair()`; connection.ts:96-98 resolves KEEPER_KEYPAIR || ~/.config/solana/id.json — the same loader every authority-needing keeper (delegate_orderbook, grow_orderbook, emergency_undelegate at delegate_orderbook.rs:108, grow_orderbook.rs:60, emergency_undelegate.rs:105) uses, so no per-service key separation exists. Live GlobalState hyPv4…: data len 104, lamports 1,614,720; rent-exempt minimum for KEEPER_EXTENDED_LEN 168 = 2,060,160 → set_keeper.rs:77-93 needs a 445,440-lamport top-up from the authority. R7.md graded items never exercise the 136→168 path (propose first, then set_keeper) — test_keeper_role_regressions.rs builds accounts at 104 or 168 only.

**Failure.** After deploy the upgrade/mint key is still required hot on the keeper VM until someone hand-writes the 0x2A transaction; S10-01's code half is landed but delivers nothing operationally.

**Fix.** Add IX_SET_KEEPER builder to client/src + frontend/src/lib/slipstream (both SDK copies), a keepers/src/set-keeper.ts, and a FILL_LOG_KEEPER_KEYPAIR env read in fill-log-keeper.ts:74 distinct from the authority loader. No program change.


### [low/repo] Settlement debits the TAKER's ledger by the MAKER's filled_margin; the taker's ER credit was charged a differently-rounded number — drift is zero at 20x but non-zero on any leverage that does not divide 100

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_from_log.rs:270`

**Evidence.** place_order.rs:438 filled_margin = maker slot drain, order_slot.rs:94-105 floor(margin_reserved×fill/remaining); place_order.rs:441-443 taker charged compute_initial_margin(compute_notional(fill,price), max_leverage) = floor(floor(size×price/1e9)/lev) (fixed_point.rs:53-70), passed from market.max_leverage at :259. settle_from_log.rs:270 (and settle_trades.rs:223) debits taker_user.reserved_margin by fill.filled_margin — the maker's number. Simulation over 7,800 (rest,fill,price) combos at lot 1e8/tick 1000: mismatches 0 at 20x (live market), 1,477 at 3x, 1,588 at 7x (e.g. K=4 lots, fill 3, p=100000: maker 9,999,999 vs taker 10,000,000). The protected test test_settlement_reader_regressions.rs:255-276 pins 'debit by filled_margin per leg' with a 2x fixture, so correcting the taker leg on-chain would require changing a protected test — reported as a finding, not a fix.

**Failure.** On any future market with max_leverage ∉ {1,2,4,5,10,20,25,50,100}, each fill leaves the honest taker's ledger ±1 atom off credit.credit; ledger>credit locks the whole free_collateral behind withdraw_collateral.rs:85 until an admin seed, ledger<credit strands atoms so close_trading_credit.rs:92-96 refuses forever.

**Fix.** Validate max_leverage at initialize_market.rs:85-93 to the divisors of 100 (so notional/lev is exact for lot 1e8 / tick 1000), which touches no protected test. NEEDS-DEPLOY for new markets only.


### [low/repo] seed_credit_ledger is a per-credit SET on a per-user ledger: cannot express multi-market sums and overwrites a concurrent fund

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/seed_credit_ledger.rs:82`

**Evidence.** seed_credit_ledger.rs:79-82 `user_mut.reserved_margin = credit.credit.min(amount)` — assignment, keyed on one TradingCredit (owner-match only, :75-77, no market_index), while reserved_margin (user_account.rs:15-36) is the user's total across all credits. With market_count > 1 the second seed clobbers the first. Same shape races an honest fund_trading_credit.rs:75-78 that lands between the operator computing `amount` and the seed landing: the fresh deposit's ledger entry is overwritten and that credit becomes unwithdrawable until re-seeded. Live market_count = 1, so only the race is reachable today; Open decision 9 (spec:917-925) chose 'repeatable set' without noting either.

**Failure.** Operator seeds after a user funds → user's new credit is below the ceiling and CreditCeilingExceeded until another manual seed; on a second market the ledger can never be seeded to the correct total.

**Fix.** Seed against the sum: take Σ credit.credit over all of the owner's program-owned credits passed as remaining accounts, and reject if user.reserved_margin > seed (only ever lower via explicit amount 0). NEEDS-DEPLOY; alternatively runbook: seed only while the user's credit is idle and re-read after landing.


### [low/repo] accept_authority leaves the outgoing admin's keeper installed

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/accept_authority.rs:56`

**Evidence.** accept_authority.rs:56-57 rewrites authority and zeroes only [104..136] (pending); the keeper at [136..168] (global_state.rs:10-11) survives rotation. set_keeper.rs:67-72 is the only writer and is gated on the NEW authority, so revocation needs a separate explicit call. The S10-01 key ceremony (spec Decision 8, :515-545) is exactly the moment the old operator's chosen key should lose record_pending_fill / initialize_fill_log / delegate_fill_log rights.

**Failure.** After the admin rotation the previous holder's keeper key retains the freeze capability from the medium finding above until the new admin remembers to call set_keeper(zeros).

**Fix.** accept_authority.rs:57 — also `fill(0)` on [136..168] when data_len >= 168 (three lines, no protected test touches the keeper region on rotation). NEEDS-DEPLOY.

### [critical/both] Settlement cursor is stuck behind an unbridgeable gap: R4's contiguity rule has no cursor-advance path and the spec's 'does not stall immediately' premise is false today

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_from_log.rs:169`  · prior: S4-01

**Evidence.** Live 2026-09-05T04:58Z: Market.last_settled_sequence=44189 (offset 2058); ER OrderBook next_fill_sequence=53734, fill_event_count=4096, head=tail=485 -> oldest surviving = 49638. Sequences 44190..49637 (5,448) are below the ring; enumerating all 461 FillLog epochs on the ER shows only 48544..48623 survive in a log (epochs 455-459, identical), so 5,368 sequences exist in no account on either layer. settle_from_log.rs:169-171 breaks unless fill.sequence == last_settled+1 and :356-357 errors FillQueueEmpty when nothing settled; the ONLY writers of the cursor are settle_trades.rs:319 and settle_from_log.rs:361 (market.rs:207) — no authority path moves it. docs/spec/remediate.md:139-143 asserts 'R4 does not stall the live cursor immediately' from an Aug-22 ring (40094..44189); that window is gone. Deployed side: repo keeper mirrors the contiguity rule client-side (keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:252-258) against a high-water-mark program that would accept a jump, so zero fills have settled since the last settle tx on epoch 403 at 2026-08-22T14:00:44Z (5znKNKp…); 9,544 fills (44190..53733) unsettled in 14 days while the keeper minted 255 new epochs (206 -> 461).

**Failure.** Settlement is dead now and stays dead the moment R3/R4 ship: every fill's margin is debited on the ER (credit.credit) and never lands in Position.collateral; under R1 the ledger is never lowered so withdraw_collateral gate 2 locks free collateral (see ledger finding). No operator action short of a new instruction can move the cursor past 44189.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: add an authority-gated, forward-only `set_settlement_cursor(seq)` (same key model as seed_credit_ledger, refuse seq <= current) and runbook it to the ring's `next_fill_sequence - fill_event_count - 1` at deploy time; correct remediate.md:139-143 and deploy.json:17 (real last deploy 2026-08-16T15:38:54Z, slot 484488785).


### [critical/repo] Any credit holder without an L1 Position halts settlement for everyone, permanently: the documented operator escape does not exist

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_from_log.rs:225`  · prior: S4-12

**Evidence.** settle_from_log.rs:208-231 `break`s on a fill whose maker/taker has no UserAccount+Position and :225-229 says the operator can 'supply the accounts or rotate the FillLog epoch'. Neither works: initialize_position.rs:47 requires the OWNER as signer and :64-71 pins the PDA to owner, so only the orphan's key can create it; rotation never touches Market.last_settled_sequence (only settle_* write it) and the next epoch's first sequence != cursor+1 -> :169 break -> FillQueueEmpty. place_order.rs:142-157 gates only on TradingCredit authorization — no Position exists on the ER — so any funded credit can produce a fill with no L1 Position (the frontend creates one at init, use-session.ts:583-586, but a raw-SDK caller need not). Keeper confirms the stop at fill-log-keeper.ts:281-296 ('queue blocked, not skipped'). Cost to trigger: one 0.1 SOL lot at the live mark $101.877 = $10.19 notional -> $0.51 margin at 20x, refundable via withdraw_trading_credit. On the deployed high-water-mark program the same orphan is merely skipped (S4-01 loss), so this is a regression introduced by R4.

**Failure.** Permissionless, ~$0.50 total-settlement outage with a ransom-only recovery (the orphan's owner must sign initialize_position). Every other trader's margin stays in limbo behind the orphan forever.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: the authority-gated cursor instruction from the gap finding also covers this (skip a named orphan sequence); alternatively let initialize_position be paid by any payer for a given owner (it allocates an identity-bound, zeroed PDA — no balance moves without the owner's signature elsewhere) so the operator can un-orphan; update :225-229.


### [high/repo] Fill-log keeper rotates epochs BEFORE committing, so the last mirror's fills are drained from the ring and never reach L1 — under contiguity that is a permanent halt every ~9 commits

`keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:162`

**Evidence.** tick() at :430-443 calls mirror() then commit(). commit() at :162-165 rotates FIRST when commitsThisEpoch >= 9 and only THEN derives the FillLog at :166 — the NEW epoch, whose ER count is 0 -> return false at :170. The fills the preceding mirror appended to the old epoch are never committed, and mirror_fills.rs:156-160 already drained them from the OrderBook ring (`drain_through_sequence(new_max_seq)`), so they exist only in the old epoch's ER copy. settle() at :220 reads only the current epoch, and settle_from_log.rs:169 refuses to advance past them. Recovery requires a manual commit_fill_log on the abandoned epoch (1 of 10 sponsored commits left, :33-36) plus a manual settle against it — no script does this.

**Failure.** Settlement stops at the first rotation after a busy mirror and stays stopped until an operator hand-commits and hand-settles the abandoned epoch; recurs on every 9th commit.

**Fix.** Keeper-only: in commit(), commit the CURRENT epoch first and rotate after a successful commit (or move the budget check to the top of tick() before mirror()), so no mirrored fill is ever left uncommitted in an epoch the keeper abandons.


### [high/repo] commit_fill_log is permissionless and the sponsored-commit budget is a hard 10 per FillLog: ten attacker commits strand every fill mirrored afterwards in an ER-only copy with no commit budget and no undelegate path

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/commit_fill_log.rs:56`  · prior: S7-12

**Evidence.** commit_fill_log.rs:56-61 checks only `payer.is_signer()` and the two magic addresses — any key can schedule a commit for the live epoch's PDA (:77-83 pins the PDA, not the caller). fill-log-keeper.ts:33-36 records the ER's cap as 'a hard 10 PER delegated account (verified live)' and :192-196 treats the cap error as 'rotate'. After R3, mirror_fills.rs:156-160 drains mirrored entries out of the ring, so fills appended to an epoch whose budget an attacker exhausted exist only in that epoch's ER copy; there is no undelegate for a FillLog (emergency_undelegate.rs:39 and :57-60 cover SEED_ORDERBOOK only) and settle_from_log.rs:169 cannot skip them. Contrast initialize_fill_log.rs:63-66 and delegate_fill_log.rs:101-104, which gate on GlobalState::is_authority_or_keeper.

**Failure.** ~10 ER transactions from any keypair permanently halt settlement under the contiguity rule (on the deployed program the same attack only forces a 0.059 SOL rotation because the un-drained ring re-mirrors the fills).

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: require `GlobalState::is_authority_or_keeper(global_data, payer.key())` in commit_fill_log exactly as initialize_fill_log/delegate_fill_log do (add global_state to its accounts); client/keeper pass the account.


### [high/repo] R1's ledger couples every user's free collateral to settlement liveness: with settlement halted, withdrawing credit leaves reserved_margin > 0 and withdraw_collateral/close_user_account refuse forever; seed_credit_ledger to 0 then destroys the in-flight margin at settlement

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/withdraw_collateral.rs:85`

**Evidence.** fund_trading_credit.rs:75-78 raises reserved_margin; the only non-authority lowerers are withdraw_trading_credit.rs:74 (bounded by credit.credit, which the ER already debited for the fill) and settlement (settle_from_log.rs:258-273). A fill that never settles therefore leaves ledger = filled_margin after the user withdraws min(credit, ledger). withdraw_collateral.rs:85 `if user.reserved_margin > 0 -> ReservedMarginExists` blocks ALL free collateral; close_user_account.rs:80 blocks closure. Settlement is halted (gap finding: 9,544 unsettled fills) so this hits every trader after their first fill post-deploy. The repair path seed_credit_ledger.rs:82 sets the ledger; if set to 0 while fills are still unsettled, settle_from_log.rs:260 applies min(filled_margin, 0) = 0 and the Position opens with zero collateral — the margin is destroyed. Spec Decision 4b (remediate.md:356-395) scopes the lock to the historical 33,146 destroyed fills and 'close_trading_credit'; it does not consider a halted pipeline. No protected test asserts gate 2 (test_withdraw_collateral_regressions.rs:153-227 cover vault/same-slot/position only).

**Failure.** Every user who trades after R1 deploys is locked out of their entire free_collateral until the authority hand-repairs their ledger, and the hand-repair silently forfeits the pending margin.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: drop gate 2 from withdraw_collateral (Open decision 1's second option — the vault ceiling lives in withdraw_trading_credit.rs:63 and is unaffected); keep the close_user_account gate. Runbook: never seed a ledger below the user's unsettled filled_margin sum.


### [high/both] update_position's reduce branch drops losses beyond released collateral silently (no insurance debit, no log) while the counterparty's gain is paid in full — a vault deficit close_position already handles

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_trades.rs:525`  · prior: S3-05

**Evidence.** settle_trades.rs:475-476 net_credit = released_collateral + reduce_margin_refund + pnl; :521-526 credits with saturating_add or debits free_collateral with `saturating_sub((-net_credit) as u64)` — a loss larger than the position's released collateral plus free_collateral vanishes. Same shape at :369-376 for the funding shortfall (`pos.collateral.saturating_sub(shortfall)`). close_position.rs:235-248 debits market.insurance_fund_balance for the identical deficit and sol_log_64s it. The winning leg's pnl is credited unconditionally on its own reduce (:475-476, :523). Honest reachability: a 20x position (5% margin) more than ~5% under water, reduced by an ER fill, with free_collateral 0 — the product's own init moves the whole free balance into credit (frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:853). Live: insurance_fund_balance reads 0 (was 20.73 USDC on Aug 21), Σ Position.realized_pnl = -102,162.51 USDC across 41 positions.

**Failure.** Claims on the vault exceed deposits by every unabsorbed settlement loss; the last withdrawer eats it. Extends S3-05's class (claim_funding) to the settlement path, which is the only path the taker bot exercises.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: in the else branch at :524-526 compute the shortfall beyond free_collateral, debit market.insurance_fund_balance (saturating, as close_position.rs:239-242) and sol_log_64 it; same for the funding shortfall at :373-375.


### [high/both] Settlement finders pin only a discriminator byte; post-R4 a forged zero-balance UserAccount turns S4-05's fee evasion into margin destruction — blocked on devnet by direct mapping, open on mainnet

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_trades.rs:534`  · prior: S4-05

**Evidence.** find_user_account/try_find_user_account/find_position_account/try_find_position_account (settle_trades.rs:534-605) match `data[0] == DISC` and the embedded owner; no `acc.owner() == program_id`, no PDA. With a non-owned forgery carrying the victim's pubkey and free=reserved=pending=0, every write to it is a no-op (settle_from_log.rs:260-271 applied = min(filled_margin, 0) = 0; :282-283 collected 0; :302-305 checked_add(0)), so the runtime's copy-path check (data unchanged) passes and the fill settles: real Position (:311-331) gains size with zero collateral, the real ledger is not debited, fee/rebate/insurance are 0, pending_fills is not decremented, and the cursor advances (:352, :361) so the fill can never be re-settled. On the deployed (pre-R4) program filled_margin is applied verbatim, so the same forgery only evades the fee (S4-05's residual). Reachability measured: `solana feature status -u devnet` shows 'enable account data direct mapping' (CR3dVN2Yoo95Y96kLSTaziWDAQT2MNEpiWh5cqVq2pNE) active since epoch 1074 (slot 463,968,000, before the Aug-16 deploy) — non-owned accounts are mapped read-only so the `-= 0` store faults; on mainnet-beta the same feature reads inactive, so the copy path and its unchanged-data comparison apply. Precondition: one trivial attacker program to author the 56 bytes. Full PDA pinning is blocked by protected fixtures that use Pubkey::new_unique() for user/position keys (test_settle_trades_regressions.rs:164-167, 254-257); an owner check is not (fixtures are program-owned, :30-38).

**Failure.** On any cluster without direct mapping, a permissionless front-runner destroys both parties' margin on every fill it settles and leaves their Positions instantly liquidatable and their ledgers un-closable.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: pass program_id into the four finders and skip any account with `acc.owner() != program_id` (closes the forgery outright; test fixtures already satisfy it). The PDA re-derivation S4-05 asks for would require changing protected fixtures — recorded as a finding, not done.


### [high/repo] pending_fills residue via permissionless front-run: settling a prefix of the keeper's window before its bundled record_pending_fill lands leaves +2 permanent bumps per pre-settled fill

`keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:352`  · prior: S4-04

**Evidence.** The keeper bundles record_pending_fill (bumps once per (fill, side), fill-log-keeper.ts:352-356, :366-376) with settle_from_log for a window it read at :237-258. settle_from_log is permissionless (no is_signer in the file); a third party that settles the first k fills of that window first makes the keeper's settle `continue` past them at settle_from_log.rs:160-162 without reaching the decrements at :284-287/:306-308, while the keeper's bumps for them still land (the tx succeeds because settled > 0). Only an all-settled window reverts (:356-357). Result: pending_fills += 2 for each pre-settled fill's maker and taker, gating withdraw_collateral.rs:76-78 and close_user_account.rs:83 until the authority runs reset_pending_fills (0x29) per account. Live: 13 of 52 accounts already hold 438,643.10 USDC behind pending_fills > 0 (same 13 as Aug 21; R5 not deployed).

**Failure.** Any keypair polling the committed FillLog and sending 1-fill settles ahead of the keeper (priority fee) locks arbitrary traders out of withdrawals until manual authority resets, repeatedly.

**Fix.** Keeper-only, no deploy: drop the record_pending_fill leg from the settle transaction — R1's ledger gate already bounds unsettled margin and the counter protects nothing the ledger does not; withdraw_collateral's gate 1 then stays at 0. (R5's frozen test only asserts reset works, so nothing protected breaks.)


### [medium/both] Taker fee is collected only from L1 free_collateral, which the product's own onboarding drains to zero — fees, maker rebates and the insurance cut are structurally uncollectable and trivially evadable

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_from_log.rs:280`

**Evidence.** settle_from_log.rs:280-288 `collected = taker_fee_owed.min(taker_user.free_collateral)`; :289-297 scale maker_rebate and insurance_cut to `collected`. place_order.rs only stamps taker_fee_bps into the FillEvent (:453) and reserves margin, never the fee, so settlement is the sole collection point. frontend/src/hooks/use-session.ts:853 `fundAmount = existingFree + depositAmount` moves the entire free balance into the credit at init, leaving free_collateral = 0. The fee is taken (:280) BEFORE update_position lands the reduce refund (:311-331), so re-funding after each reduce keeps free at 0 indefinitely. Live: Market.taker_fee_bps=6, maker_rebate_bps=1; insurance_fund_balance = 0; 4 of 52 UserAccounts sit at free_collateral = 0 (the rest are bot/legacy wallets holding 551,264.96 USDC free).

**Failure.** Frontend users pay zero fees, makers receive zero rebate on those fills, the insurance fund never accrues, and fill spam is free for the producer while each 80 fills cost the operator a 0.059 SOL epoch.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: reserve `margin + fee` from the taker's credit in place_order (stamp the reserved fee into FillEvent) and collect it from `taker_applied`/the credit side at settlement instead of free_collateral; until then, stop the frontend funding 100% of free collateral (use-session.ts:853) so fees have a source.


### [medium/repo] Keeper settle() reads only the current epoch's log, so once the contiguous next sequence lives in an older epoch the keeper self-stalls even when the chain could progress

`keepers/src/fill-log-keeper.ts:220`

**Evidence.** settle() derives the FillLog at :220 from the in-memory `epoch` only and windows from cursor+1 at :251-258; rotate() at :128-134 never records the old epoch. Any rotation that leaves unsettled fills behind (rotate-before-commit finding; an orphan at :281-296 that is later resolved by its owner; the FillQueueFull rotation at :151-153 while settle was still draining) puts cursor+1 in an epoch the keeper never reads, so windowFills is empty and settle() returns at :259 forever, logging nothing.

**Failure.** Settlement stops with no signal even in cases the program could settle, until an operator hand-settles the older epoch.

**Fix.** Keeper-only: when the current epoch's log does not contain cursor+1, scan epochs downward (findFillLogPda for epoch-1, -2, …) until one does, and settle from that log; log the epoch actually used.


### [low/repo] The :182 ponytail marker: the 1x bound is 20x loose but harmless after F1; its real residual is a settlement-stall lever, and the protected fixture's wrong constant pins it

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_from_log.rs:182`  · prior: S4-06

**Evidence.** What it bounds: :190-191 rejects filled_margin > compute_notional(quantity, price), i.e. margin at under 1x leverage. Honest producer maximum is notional / max_leverage (place_order.rs:183 for makers via OrderSlot.margin_reserved, :442 for takers); live Market.max_leverage = 20, so the bound admits 20x the honest maximum. Not wrong: it never rejects an honest fill at leverage >= 2 (only at max_leverage = 1 can drain_margin_for_fill's floor at order_slot.rs:98-101 put a last partial fill <= 2 atoms above its own fill_notional -> false FillMarginExceeded -> under :169 contiguity, a permanent stall). Not needed for safety: the F1 clamp :258-273 bounds applied margin by the never-delegated ledger, so nothing mints via this path — the marker's stated ceiling ('a hostile ER can still mint up to 1x notional') is stale. What remains is contradictory design: :246-250 explains that R4 clamps rather than rejects so 'a lying ER cannot abort settlement and stall the cursor', yet :190-191 and :153-154 are exactly such rejects, and under contiguity there is no way past a rejected fill. The fixture constant `QTY / MAX_LEVERAGE` = 5,000,000 (test_settlement_reader_regressions.rs:180, 204, 230, 261, 291, 385) is 10x the honest `compute_notional(QTY, PRICE)/20` = 500,000 (:340-342 states notional = 10,000,000), and F1's test :332-367 requires a REJECT above the relational bound, so tightening or clamping breaks protected tests either way.

**Failure.** Documentation debt plus a hostile-ER (and a leverage-1 rounding) stall lever; no minting.

**Fix.** Correct the fixture constant to `compute_notional(QTY, PRICE) / MAX_LEVERAGE` — a protected test, so recorded as a finding; then tighten :190 to `compute_initial_margin(fill_notional, market.max_leverage)` and rewrite the marker to name the stall lever as the ceiling (NEEDS-DEPLOY for the bound).


### [low/both] S4-10(b) still open: `fill_qty as i64` in update_position is unguarded after R4 closed only the sequence half

`programs/slipstream/src/instructions/settle_trades.rs:388`  · prior: S4-10

**Evidence.** settle_trades.rs:387-391 `signed_qty = fill_qty as i64` / `-(fill_qty as i64)` with no try_from; compute_notional at :177 only rejects when quantity*price/1e9 exceeds u64, so price in {0, 1} with quantity > i64::MAX passes (:185 then admits filled_margin <= ~9.2e9, clamped by the ledger). R4 added the sequence bound (:156-158) but not this one. Hostile-ER only (S4-C8 boundary).

**Failure.** A BID fill opens a short (or vice versa) and corrupts open_interest_long/short and every health computation; with overflow-checks on, `pos.size += signed_qty` at :427/:518 can also panic and stall the cursor.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY: `let q = i64::try_from(fill_qty).map_err(|_| SlipstreamError::MathOverflow)?;` at :387.


### [low/both] FillLog epochs are never reclaimable and the pipeline has burned 255 dead epochs since Aug 21 while settling nothing

`programs/slipstream/src/state/fill_log.rs:14`  · prior: S4-02

**Evidence.** 461 FillLog epochs exist on L1 (206 on 2026-08-21); each is 8,344 bytes -> (8,344+128)*6,960 = 58,965,120 lamports rent-exempt, ~27.2 SOL locked, and no close_fill_log instruction exists (instructions/mod.rs dispatch has none). Epochs 455-459 all hold the identical block 48544..48623 — five rotations re-mirroring the same 80 fills. Epoch 460 (created 2026-08-31T06:41:26Z) has count 0 and no ER transaction since; L1 copies with count > 0: 356 of 461, so commits do land (S4-X03 was transient). Under R3 the ring caps at 4,096 unmirrored fills with the keeper mirroring 32 per 4 s (fill-log-keeper.ts:45, :59), so sustained load above ~8 fills/s halts matching and each 80 fills costs a 0.059 SOL epoch that fill producers pay nothing for (fee finding).

**Failure.** Operator SOL drains linearly with fill count with no recovery of rent; a fill-spammer converts free fills into operator cost.

**Fix.** NEEDS-DEPLOY (later): authority-gated close_fill_log for epochs whose max sequence <= Market.last_settled_sequence; keeper-side now, stop rotating when the cursor cannot advance (compare cursor vs epoch min sequence before initializing a new epoch).
